# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2001

## Introduction by Urmilā-devī dāsī

Every morning we sing two prayers to our spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. While chanting our Gāyatrī thrice daily we offer him our obeisances and meditate on his enthusing us as he is enthused to serve Śrī Kṛṣṇa. And in the numerous times during each day that we offer obeisances, we say mantras glorifying him. Daily we offer him *ārati* or observe the ceremony. Yet we do not think we have done enough. Rather, every year we also compile this book of formal homages and present it to him on his appearance day as part of the Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony.

Yes, it is traditional among the followers of the *Vedas* to especially honor their guru on the anniversary of his appearance day. It is also traditional to term the occasion Vyāsa-pūjā, as all bona fide spiritual masters represent Vyāsadeva, who compiled Vedic knowledge. And it is traditional when there is a great *ācārya* for all the present generations of disciples in the branches of his line to observe Vyāsa-pūjā in his honor in addition to worshiping their own guru.

But it is not only for the sake of respecting these traditions that we members of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness compile this book to revere our founder-*ācārya* and spiritual master.

The daily rituals and prayers can become automatic, and tradition can be done for its own sake. However, when we write an offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda, we must think and pray deeply to gain at least some measure of a specific understanding of his greatness. The majesty and miracle of his preaching then astound and humble us. We renew our pride at being even his most menial servant, and regain inspiration to further his mission.

No matter how sweet and profound our own realization is when brought to the surface while writing an offering, we gain even more from also hearing the offerings of other devotees. Each individual follower of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s has a unique perspective on his greatness, the service he or she offers to him, how to increase the *saṅkīrtana* movement, or how to pray for his continued mercy and guidance. This book is a sharing of these international, multifaceted prayers, requests, and glorifications. Reading this book allows each of us to expand the length, breadth, and depth of our relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Many devotees keep the Vyāsa-pūjā book as a dear companion throughout the year, not only for the written offerings but especially for the pictures. Here in this publication dedicated to Śrīla Prabhupāda we literally see him in a variety of loving, instructing, worshiping, and managing interactions with the devotees and Kṛṣṇa. One has a sense when looking through these pictures that Prabhupāda is again with us on this planet as our fatherly guide. The book itself thus becomes an example of *yukta-vairāgya*—using modern technology to further our devotion and service to our spiritual master.

Beyond what is published in this book, most ISKCON members write offerings for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance anniversary. Generally, each member reads his or hers aloud in community gatherings worldwide. Upon hearing these many realizations, each listener deepens his or her love and indebtedness to Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is easy to think that only those who regularly lecture and teach, or who serve in prominent positions, have a meaningful depth of understanding. Hearing and reading the Vyāsa-pūjā offerings reminds us that every one of Prabhupāda’s followers is glorious in his or her varieties of realization.

Maybe some of this book’s readers are not committed followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda who wish to renew their dedication to his mission. Perhaps they are curious or even wanting to criticize our devotion to someone who appears to them as an ordinary man. This Vyāsa-pūjā book is valuable for such readers as well. People outside the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement can consider that glorification of heroic people is a part of every culture. Every schoolchild in every country writes essays praising those their society deems have made significant contributions to their country or who display exemplary attributes. How much greater is one whose contribution is for the spiritual welfare of all humanity?

And Śrīla Prabhupāda is worthy of study and appreciation from any angle of vision. Seventy years old, he gets a free trip to America on a cargo ship. Coming with a suitcase of Indian-printed books (not exactly up to Western publishing standards!) and a few dollars worth of rupees, he has a sponsor for one month and tells the immigration official that he might stay for one more. His first followers were not wealthy or influential, nor were they knowledgeable in the tradition he was teaching.

Twelve years later he has traveled around the world fourteen times, written and translated volumes of books, met with leading world figures, and established 108 centers on six continents. By these efforts he affected a deep positive change in the lives of millions. There are his disciples, for whom all things, actions, thoughts, and goals are now connected with the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Absolute Truth. But many more people in all countries have begun their spiritual lives through grand public festivals, distribution of sanctified food (*prasādam*), and most of all through ISKCON’s unprecedented selling of books.

Someday, when there is a re-spiritualization of society in general, as Śrīla Prabhupāda predicted, these Vyāsa-pūjā books will be held as great treasures. Let us treasure them now as primary instruments of our own spiritual transformation.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Urmilā-devī dāsī  
(written on the appearance day of Lord Nityānanda, the original spiritual master)

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Vipramukhya Swami

The term Vyāsa-pūjā has two principal meanings, the grammatical and the internal, or the objective and the subjective. The former is the dictionary meaning. The latter is how it affects us within. Arguably, the internal meaning is more important than the grammatical, dictionary meaning.

Objectively, the dictionary meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā can be understood as “worship of Vyāsa.” We call the appearance day of the spiritual master Vyāsa-pūjā because on that day we worship the spiritual master as the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Subjectively, or internally, Vyāsa-pūjā means a celebration by the disciple of salvation at the lotus feet of the spiritual master. For the disciple it evokes a sense of inadequacy, humility, and re-dedication to the mission of the spiritual master. It kindles the fire of love and spiritual reminiscence. Invariably it is accompanied by a reunion of friends, Godbrothers and Godsisters, and together there comes a spirit of self-appraisal and critical awareness. Vyāsa-pūjā means “intense.” For me it is a day for feeling separation and union at the same time. It means feeling happiness, yet feeling sadness. It means feeling the swells of emotion rising from the ocean of separation, yet knowing the spiritual master remains with us forever.

For the followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda, Vyāsa-pūjā has yet a third meaning.

Devotees are not ordinary people, and Śrīla Prabhupāda was not ordinary among devotees. He was and remains the Lord’s topmost devotee, who united people around the world under the common banner of ISKCON and Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He accomplished what no one before or since has matched. Śrīla Prabhupāda was a pure *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee specifically empowered by Lord Caitanya and by his spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. He started from scratch and built an empire by making nondevotees into devotees. Indeed, that is the real test of the *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee. As Śrīla Prabhupāda writes in his purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 16.74: “One should judge by action, not by promises. A *mahā-bhāgavata* can turn a living entity from abominable material life to the Lord’s service. This is the test of a *mahā-bhāgavata*.”

For these reasons and many others, we accept Śrīla Prabhupāda as the topmost devotee of the Lord, and his appearance day, or Vyāsa-pūjā, as the topmost of all *pūjās*.

Because Śrīla Prabhupāda is the founder of the modern Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in the West, and because he has saved us from the clutches of Māyā, or illusion, through his personal association, books, and dedicated followers, Vyāsa-pūjā carries with it this third meaning: the celebration of being specifically connected to such a great soul.

In other words, for devotees of Kṛṣṇa, Vyāsa-pūjā is much more than an official celebration. It also means a feeling of love and re-dedication to the mission of the spiritual master. And for the followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda, it means specifically to humbly feel oneself connected to him and to rededicate oneself to his mission.

I hope you will enjoy this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā book, and I hope you will imbibe from it some of the internal reasons why we call Vyāsa-pūjā “the most blessed event.”

Vipramukhya Swami

## Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your young and inexperienced followers were surprised when, in the summer of 1966 in New York, you announced during an evening class that an attorney was present and you wanted to incorporate your fledgling movement.

They had come for class and *kīrtana*. You had a vision for an international society. They desired your association. You planned to provide Kṛṣṇa conscious association across the globe. You were creating a society that would “systematically propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” It would be called ISKCON and, as you said later, was nondifferent from your body.

You gave many instructions how your society should be developed, and you traveled almost constantly to assure its healthy growth. Without the association of devotees, you wrote, it is not possible to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. ISKCON was your creation to provide that association.

Association with those engaged in a similar line of business is very conducive to advancement in that business....Similarly, we have established the International Society for Krishna Consciousness to give people an opportunity to associate with those who have not forgotten Kṛṣṇa. \[*The Nectar of Instruction,* Text 2, purport\]

Like a loving father, you watched ISKCON grow with delight, and when some disciples or centers went astray you felt the loss with a pained heart. You gave us many instructions. One was very simple, yet very difficult: Our society should be based on love and trust.

That was fairly easy when you were physically with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because your followers could see you, hear directly from you, be with you, receive your loving glances (and your loving chastisements). They felt trust grow in their hearts. They trusted you, and they trusted in that blue cowherd boy you described as the ultimate worshipable object. We trusted you because we knew you genuinely cared about us.

You loved your followers. They felt it. Many, many personal stories testify to the fact that despite the challenges of spiritual practice and the sometimes rough treatment by immature peers or leaders, those touched by your personal affection felt blessed and truly protected by your love.

You sacrificed for us. You cared not just about our spiritual growth but also about our health, our marriages, our children, and our education. Even as a *sannyāsī,* you presided over our marriages, you taught us how to build buildings and to keep financial records. You even gave us a formula for tooth powder. You cooked for us and you gave up your health for us. You loved us, and therefore we tried to love you.

O King, a saintly person is just like water because he is free from all contamination, gentle by nature, and by speaking creates a beautiful vibration like that of flowing water. Just by seeing, touching or hearing such a saintly person, the living entity is purified, just as one is cleansed by contact with pure water. Thus a saintly person, just like a holy place, purifies all those who contact him because he always chants the glories of the Lord. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* 11.7.44\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as your sons, daughters, and grandchildren, we must recognize that it is now up to us to continue your mission. It is our job to develop your society based on love and trust. But it is not easy.

We conditioned souls in this material world have been cheated lifetime after lifetime. Our hearts have been broken, and we are full of fear. We are not trustworthy, because we think of ourselves first. We do not trust others, because we know they want to exploit us.

But you have made it our duty to become trustworthy, to build a society based on love and trust. Trust is not inherited—it is earned. Each new generation must strive to inspire trust in the world it serves. As the adage states, we should command respect, not demand it.

To command respect we must be willing to check ourselves constantly. To question our own motives, to be open to critics, to listen to those who feel injured. Doing so is not a sign of weakness; it is strength. It is the price we must pay to become pure. It is the price we must pay to become worthy of trust; especially in the hearts of those we lead.

And we must struggle to manifest love in our relationships, both with those inside ISKCON and with those in the larger society around us. Those who we “preach to” don’t want to be told, they want to be loved. Because you loved, we listened. Because you cared, we trusted. If we don’t love, if we don’t care, then our message will fall on deaf ears.

O, spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat your message, so if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding.

Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life

O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands, so if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like. \[*Mārkine Bhāgavata Dharma,* September, 1965).

Such is the anxiousness of your love, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You appeared in this world to display your mercy. Now, by your mercy and the will of the Lord you have disappeared from our sight. Now we, your disciples, must become instruments of your love. We must become worthy of the trust of others, on your behalf.

And we all share that burden. For this reason you have created the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. It is not the GBC’s job, or just for those serving as gurus or as *sannyāsīs*. It is the responsibility of each and every one of us. Each one of us is a teacher. Each of us is a preacher. Each of us is a guru. Each of us must become worthy of trust. Each of us must become a transparent medium for your compassion. Free from duplicity, we must genuinely care for the welfare of everyone we meet.

To the degree that our network of association is based on caring for the needs of those we serve—and not influenced by selfish exploitation for personal aggrandizement, sense gratification, power, control, or fame—to that extent our society will flourish.

We cannot accomplish anything on our own. It is only when we please you by our sincere endeavor and our prayers that we will become happy, and we will become empowered. We may be successful in the short run by less than pure means, but the long term growth of your institution depends on our pleasing you and submitting to your instruction: Build our society on love and trust.

For you, Prabhupāda, it is most easy to inspire love and trust. Being full in your realization of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, it is natural to act out of love and to always inspire trust. For us it is hard work. Please let us not shy away from that work. On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I pray for your mercy so that I might become a humble instrument in your hands—helping to build an ISKCON society based on love and trust.

Your aspiring servant,  
Anuttama dāsa

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Each year as I sit down to write my Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I am reminded of the example of a dwarf trying to reach for the moon. How can I properly glorify such a transcendental person as you? All I know is that you are the full manifestation of all the qualities decorating a true *sādhu,* that your glorious preaching confirms you are uniquely empowered to spead Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, and that somehow or other you have delivered me.

You saw a sign in Boston Harbor—“Unalloyed Steel”—and took it as confirmation that you should not water down or compromise your teachings. Driving in Detroit, you noticed the Sherwin-Williams logo of paint pouring over the globe, with the motto “We cover the world.” You commented, “Yes, this is Lord Caitanya’s movement.” An insurance company billboard showing a pair of cupped hands sheltering a house and family caught your eye. You read the phrase “You are in good hands with All-State” and said, “This is Kṛṣṇa. *Yoga-kṣemaṁ vahāmy aham*. \[‘I carry what they lack and preserve what they have.’\]”

The other day, while thinking about what to say in this offering, I saw a sign that read “A bargain at any price.” I thought “Yes, this is what you have given us.”

The dictionary defines the word *bargain* as follows:

an advantageous purchase, especially one acquired at less than the usual cost; an agreement between two parties settling what each shall do, give, receive, etc., in a transaction

As far as the “advantageous” nature of what you are offering, Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has delineated the value of such a “purchase”:

(1) Pure devotional service brings immediate relief from all kinds of material distress.

(2) Pure devotional service is the beginning of all auspiciousness.

(3) Pure devotional service automatically puts one in transcendental pleasure.

(4) Pure devotional service is rarely achieved.

(5) Those in pure devotional service deride even the conception of liberation.

(6) Pure devotional service is the only means to attract Kṛṣṇa.

As for “less than the usual cost,” you are the deputed agent of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is glorified as Mahā-vadānyāya, the most munificent and charitably disposed *avatāra*. Once in Los Angeles you were describing the strict austerities traditionally required to approach the Lord. As we sat stunned, you smiled and said, “Yes, if I asked for everything you would faint. Actually, I am 90% lenient.”

The other part of the definition of “bargain” is that it is an agreement between two parties. There is no doubt that you continue to fulfill every part of your side of the contract. All you ask from us in exchange is that we keep our simple vows, remain loyal to your teachings, and do our best to be engaged in the service of your mission.

I pray that I may always appreciate the “bargain” you are giving and that I may always remain determined to pay the simple price you ask.

Begging to be your eternal servant,  
Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances to your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Prabhupāda, you are full of compassion, and I thank you for that.

You feel sorrow for us souls in this world. We always make plans to succeed in material life. Our lives are wasted as we aspire to live and enjoy eternally in a body that is destined for doom.

To uplift us you have given the *mahā-mantra* and the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. By your grace we will not only awaken but will taste the ever-fresh elixir of loving Kṛṣṇa.

Clearly no one has done as you have done. I am certain that it will be many long days before anyone may even begin to approach the standard of your profound appreciation, realization, and love for the messages of your Guru Mahārāja and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Thus I again become aware that the only hope for my salvation is to serve as you have served, with the same mood of service and devotion to guru and Kṛṣṇa that you have shown.

Prabhupāda, my life is yours.

Please continue to guide me through the darkness of the night of Kali. Place the light of knowledge in my eyes that I may see, place your wonderful narrations of Kṛṣṇa in my ears that I may hear, and place nectarean *kṛṣṇa-kathā* in my mouth that I may and taste the reality of eternal life with you and Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most expert physician. I am slowly dying without your and Kṛṣṇa’s *darśana*. Please give me the medicine of your service and instructions, which will save my life. Show yourself to this useless servant and give me the needed inspiration to succeed.

I am lame, blind, and dumb. I have been devoured by the whale of lustful desires. I continue to make newer and newer offenses. Thus I am humbly requesting you to save me and keep me forever engaged in your service.

The preaching is going on. Let me preach more to show you my sincerity. Let me try to chant better to show you my sincerity. Then what will happen will all be in your hands.

Please keep me steadily fixed despite my many mistakes.

Your eternal grateful servant,  
Bhakti Bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear lord and master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you and your unlimited service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead!

Taking a straw between my teeth and some dust from your lotus feet on my head, I again bow before you and try to compose a small Vyāsa-pūjā offering for your glorification on this most sacred day of your appearance in this world.

This material world is an overwhelmingly difficult place. The more I experience this, the more I realize there is no safe place here. But then I remember there is one safe place, one place where there is no danger but rather an eternal security that is unshakable under any circumstances. Even death or things worse than death cannot touch us if we are in that place.

Where is that place? That is the shelter of your lotus feet, specifically in the form of your transcendental instructions.

I was reading through a number of statements you made based on a line from the song “Śrī Guru Vandana,” by Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, which we sing every morning in our ISKCON temples. The line is *guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā,* which translates as “My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from his lotus mouth.”

The message of this line is something you took very seriously, and this became clear to me when I read your statements based on it:

So this *guru-pūjā* which we are doing, it is not self-aggrandizement; it is real teaching. You sing daily, what is that? *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya ār nā koriyā aikya*. *Bās,* this is translation. I tell you frankly, whatever little success is there in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, I simply believed what was spoken by my Guru Mahārāja. You also continue that. Then every success will come.

You draw this out in a wonderful way in one class in which you are discussing Lord Caitanya’s pastime of giving His mercy to the illiterate *brāhmaṇa* in Śrī Raṅgam who was attempting to read *Bhagavad-gītā* daily on his guru’s order:

So he \[the *brāhmaṇa*\] also could understand that “Here is a person who has not come to criticize me. He is serious.” \[The *brāhmaṇa* said:\] “Yes, Sir, I am reading *Bhagavad-gītā*, but I am illiterate. I am illiterate.”

“Then what you are reading?”

“No, I am simply turning the pages. I cannot read anything.”

“Then why you are reading if you cannot?”

“No, my Guru Mahārāja ordered me. My Guru Mahārāja asked me that ‘You shall read *Bhagavad-gītā* daily, eighteen chapters.’”

Guru Mahārāja knew that he is illiterate, but still ordered. This is called *guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā*. This is called firm faith in guru. Guru ordered him that “You read.” Guru must know him, that he is illiterate. So what he will read? Why guru is ordering him? But because he had firm faith in guru and he was trying to read, Caitanya Mahāprabhu came to see him. Just see. How this line is important: *guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā*. *Ār* means anything more, you don’t try to understand. Whatever your guru says, you just try to carry it out.

To apply this understanding nicely is the secret of success for the *śiṣya*, or disciple, whereas not doing so is a sure formula for failure. You explain:

*Śiṣya* means under the order, regulation. A person cannot disobey the order of guru. Then he is *śiṣya*. If he argues, he’s not *śiṣya*. He’s not a *śiṣya*. Therefore Arjuna says, *śiṣyas te ’ham:* “I surrender, voluntarily surrender to become Your disciple. Now I shall not argue.” That is called *śiṣya*. If you argue, then you are not a *śiṣya*. *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā*. This is *tapasya,* that “I shall not act anything which is not ordered by my guru, that *tapasya*. *Tapo divyam putrakā* \[SB 5.5.1\]. Then we’ll be nicely guided, and then *sattva śuddhyam*. Then our this existence will be purified. And as soon as our existence is purified, then we realize the situation, what is God, what is our relationship with Him, what is our activities, *athāto brahma-jijñāsā, janmādy asya yataḥ*, everything.

Kali-yuga is displaying its potency in ways which are sometimes insidious and sometimes openly vicious. We see devotees drift from the unique shelter of your lotus feet in the form of your instructions, even for so-called “good” reasons, like “higher understandings” or “better association.” But this is the highest understanding, and those who follow it are the best association. You have certified this clearly.

In one lecture you explain how the knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is coming in disciplic succession from Lord Kṛṣṇa through the previous *ācāryas* and then to yourself, and that you are presenting it to us:

There is no difference. That is the specific procedure of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You are singing daily, *guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā*. Very simple thing. We are receiving the transcendental knowledge through *guru-paramparā* succession. So we have to simply take instruction from guru, and if we execute that to our heart and soul, that is success. That is practical. I have no personal qualification, but I simply tried to satisfy my guru. That’s all. My Guru Mahārāja asked me that “If you get some money, you print books.” So there was a private meeting, talking, some of my important Godbrothers also there. It was in Rādhā-kuṇḍa. So Guru Mahārāja was speaking to me that “Since we have got this Bagh Bazaar marble temple, there has been so many dissensions, and everyone thinking who will occupy this room or that room, that room. I wish, therefore, to sell this temple and the marble and print some book.” Yes. So I took up this from his mouth, that he is very fond of books. And he told me personally that “If you get some money, print books.” Therefore I am stressing on this point: “Where is book? Where is book? Where is book?” So kindly help me. This is my request. Print as many books in as many languages and distribute throughout the whole world. Then Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will automatically increase.

Exposing the fallacy that one has to have face-to-face association with advanced devotees or one will be unable to come to the highest levels of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you explained:

*Sarva-śāstre kaya, lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya.* For me, personally, I had the opportunity to talk with my spiritual master not more than ten times in my whole life, not more. It may be less than that. But I tried to follow his instruction, that’s all, although I was a *gṛhastha*...

So this is the process. That is the... You sing every day. *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya*. That is the process. Wherever you live, if you follow strictly the instruction of guru, then you remain perfect. But if we create, concoct ideas against the instruction of guru, then we are doomed, hell. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*. There is no more shelter, finished. *Yasya prasādāt*. If guru thinks that “This person, I wanted to take him back to home, back to Godhead. Now he is going against me. He is not following,” *aprasādāt,* he is displeased, then everything is finished...

Yes. Anyway, follow the instruction. That is required. Follow the instruction. Wherever you remain, it doesn’t matter. You are secure. Follow the instruction. Then you are secure anywhere. It doesn’t matter...

\[And now this ISKCON movement has developed in grand style\] because I strictly follow the instruction of my Guru Mahārāja, that’s all. Otherwise I have no strength. I have not played any magic. Did I? Any gold manufacturing? \[laughter\] Still, I have got better disciples than the gold-manufacturing guru.

You were unequivocal on this point. One time you said:

Yes. This is the order. *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya*. Now *citta* means consciousness or heart. “I shall do this only, *bās*. My Guru Mahārāja told me; I shall do this.” *Cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā*. So it is not my pride, but I can say, for your instruction, I did it. Therefore whatever little success you see than my all my Godbrothers, it is due to this. I have no capacity, but I took it, the words of my guru, as life and soul. So this is fact. *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya*. Everyone should do that. But if he makes addition, alteration, then he is finished.

Today I am praying that this message can become indelibly imprinted in my small brain. I am simply the lowest of your disciples, but I have faith that if I can do this I can be successful, despite everything, and please you and go back to Godhead. The force of *māyā* is showing me I have no potency to do anything worthwhile myself. I have no alternative than to take this shelter, and that is my good fortune. Please continue to be so kind to offer it to me.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Cāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

When you were in our midst, never did I realize how precious your association was. What would I not give today to have a moment’s association with Your Divine Grace? And in the depths of my heart I hope against hope that some day I will become purified enough to be in your divine association once again.

Often devotees get your association through dreams and tell me about their ecstatic association with you. Sometimes I also become blessed with that good fortune, and whenever you appear my heart becomes filled with an indescribable joy. From that experience I realize that the dreams about you are not actually dreams but a glimpse into the spiritual world—they are not just a subtle recollection of the mind but real perception of you in your spiritual form.

Recently I heard about such a perception of one devotee. You appeared to him, and when he asked you why so many problems in ISKCON are stifling the progress of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in this world, you explained that you go from one universe to another performing your pastimes. After Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu inundates the Bhāratavarṣa of a certain universe, you come and spread His *saṅkīrtana* movement throughout that earth planet. In some universes, after your disappearance, the devotees there cooperate with one another and carry out your instructions with all sincerity, and there everything goes on very nicely and Kṛṣṇa consciousness continues to spread. But in some planets, like ours, the devotees fail to do that, and as a result the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement suffers a terrible setback. However, you assured him that here also everything will eventually become all right: those who are sincere will act according to your instructions, and through them Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy will flow and the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will start to grow.

I feel convinced beyond a shadow of a doubt that it was not a dream but that you did appear to that devotee and answered his questions. I feel reassured that although we are not able to see you with our mundane eyes anymore, you are very much here with us. You emphatically instructed us that we must associate with you through your *vāṇī* and develop our real relationship with you by following your instructions. You assured us that if we mold our lives according to your teachings, you will always be present in our hearts.

Once again I have been blessed with the realization that you are still with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and that those who abide by your instructions will receive your mercy. They are your real followers, and it is through them that your mission will be accomplished. Due to their genuine love for you, they will cooperate with one another, and it is due to their sincere endeavors that ISKCON will be saved from its present calamities. They will become the true participants in Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement and will be recognized on this earth as the greatest heroes of all time, and at the end of their stay on this planet they will be awarded the greatest good fortune of entering in to the spiritual world to assist you in your eternal service to the Transcendental Couple. My only desire is to always remain a servant of such a servant of yours.

Aspiring to become a speck of dust at your lotus feet,

Your insignificant servant,  
Bhakti Cāru Swami

## Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Thank You for Rescuing Me**

My dear eternal father and spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Lately I have been reflecting more intensely on how fortunate I and thousands of other souls on this planet are to have met and served you personally or to have been influenced by your teachings, instructions, guidance, and encouragement. These days I normally like to personalize most of my reflections, so lately I have been thinking what would have been my fate if you had not so gracefully come into my life. I am bursting with gratitude. Thank you for rescuing me.

If I had not met you, perhaps I would be a dean of a university somewhere, overseeing an institution that creates sophisticated animals. The universities are becoming more and more degraded as they teach and campaign for relativism and massive data collecting. Morality, ethics, integrity, and the accumulation of wisdom have become extremely rare. Students as well as professors know so much, but understand so little. They live for simply refining their eating, sleeping, mating, and defending, which also make up the lifestyle of animals. I am bursting with gratitude. Thank you for rescuing me.

If I had not met you, perhaps I would be a politician, traveling here and there, supposedly representing a political party or a sovereign country, while actually representing myself. The world is presently overflooding with cheating politicians, who speak lies and who live an even bigger lie. Duplicity is their breakfast, exploitation is their lunch, and promiscuity is their dinner. Most politicians lack true commitment to their constituents and also lack courage to be principle-centered, and most unfortunately they also have very poor character. I am bursting with gratitude. Thank you for rescuing me.

If I had not met you, perhaps I would have pursued a business career in corporate America, sitting somewhere in a plush office and slowly dying. I can imagine dying of stress, boredom, depression, or frustration while trying to get various promotions. I can also imagine how unhappy I would have been having a big family for whom I must spend almost every waking hour thinking how to get more money to provide for all their material desires. I can reflect on how difficult it would be living in a big house, which would be like a prison with a high mortgage and lots of security. I would be like a slave to the house, having to work like hell to pay for it and keep it in good condition. I am bursting with gratitude. Thank you for rescuing me.

If I had not met you, perhaps I would have been a civil rights leader, totally absorbed in racial issues. There are so many people demanding their rights. There is the effort for gay rights, women’s rights, children’s rights, employees’ rights, military rights, diplomatic rights, minority rights, animal rights, etc. But often while different groups demand their rights they are not concerned with other people’s problems, needs, and rights. Most importantly, the various people who demand their rights almost always overlook their duties and obligations as nonmaterial entities. So in the process of strategizing and campaigning for material rights, one overlooks one’s spiritual responsibilities, commitments, and obligations. I am bursting with gratitude. Thank you for rescuing me.

If I had not met you, perhaps I would have been a leader in a religious or psychic order. I would have been pursuing popularity and mystic powers. Surely I would have been using spirituality to acquire a material empire. I would have been using God, His church, and His people to simply make me famous. While telling people about God, I surely would have been sectarian and fanatical so as to enhance my own power and prestige. I am bursting with gratitude. Thank you for rescuing me.

You have given me a chance to live most of my life as a celibate *sannyāsī* surrounded by so many wonderful Godbrothers and -sisters, projects, preaching opportunities, and disciples. How kind you have continuously been to me year after year, even though I am so unworthy! If I and others can give up the false ego and develop more compassion, each of us would be able to experience more of your blessings on each of us. Please continue to push us, more now than ever before. You, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, have given us the sweet, sublime science of *bhakti,* the culture of pure, radiant, eternal love. I thank you so much for your causeless guidance, protection, compassion, and love. I am bursting with gratitude. Thank you for rescuing me.

Your son,  
Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

In this mundane world, thinkers, writers, politicians, and other thoughtful people are constantly engaged in creating alternative ideas, religions, philosophies and world-views. On a very grand scale, affecting all of humanity, these intellectual leaders are using their power to influence society, building and destroying whole empires.

But, as you so often have told us, there is no need for us to accept all of their ideas. Instead, following in your transcendental footsteps, we need to logically analyze their faulty philosophical arguments and re-establish the Absolute Truth in the light of guru, *śāstra,* and *sādhu*. On this topic I would like to offer you a poem.

Rudyard Kipling once remarked,  
“East and West shall never meet.”  
With care you fused the two of them  
And produced a fruit that is so sweet.  
You proved him wrong.

“Religion is the opium for folks,”  
Was Karl Marx’s stand.  
You always held that dharma’s good,  
That dharma is the helpful hand.  
You proved him wrong.

Cārvāka Muni once opined,  
“Dine! Wine! And be fine!”  
You challenged this assertion  
And said to abstain is more divine.  
You proved him wrong.

The guess of Mister Darwin  
Put the ape right on the map.  
You said that man’s from Brahmā  
And always, always been a chap.  
You proved him wrong.

Man is seen through the eyes of Freud  
Bound to lust as the bottom line.  
You said it’s love, not lust,  
This world tends to confine.  
You proved him wrong.

Shelley’s tale of Frankenstein  
Tells he’s made of matter combined.  
You protested: How this can be?  
Without a soul can there be a mind?  
You proved him wrong.

Buddha made a claim on life  
That all is nil and all is not.  
You said of him, “If all is not,  
One might not say an awful lot.”  
You proved him wrong.

Shankar spoke of the Absolute One,  
Denying a personal God.  
You said that He is sentient,  
That a faceless God is odd.  
You proved him wrong.

Nietzsche said that God is dead.  
From you we know that God has fun.  
He plays a fine flute,  
Dances and so much loves to run.  
You proved him wrong.

Gandhi once expressed this doubt:  
Kṛṣṇa, He is where?  
You confirmed that not just He  
But also Rādhā must be there.  
You proved him wrong.

Your servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! On this first anniversary of your divine advent in this new millennium of the Christian era, I would like to humbly re-dedicate my life to executing your divine orders.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am very happy to report to you that last November we opened the newly constructed Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Krishnacandra Manimandir in Manipur in pursuance of your direct order to me. Although the entire construction is not yet complete, by your mercy the temple has become a landmark in Manipur. Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Krishnacandra have moved into Their new residence, along with the newly installed Deities of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrīmatī Lalitā and Śrīmatī Viśākhā, and Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā Mahārāṇī.

The King of Purī, Gajapati Mahārāja, kindly arranged for his two Rāja Gurus and a priest of the temple of Lord Jagannātha to conduct an elaborate installation ceremony for these Deities, similar to the procedure for the installation of the original Purī Deities. The two Rāja Gurus informed us that it had never been done before except for the original Deities.

The opening ceremony was a grand affair: both the chief minister and the governor of Manipur graced the ceremony, and there was very good coverage by the media. We held twenty-three days of Kṛṣṇa conscious cultural and educational programs. All the visiting guests and local people were very pleased. One of the main highlights on the opening day (Śarad Pūrṇimā, the day of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *rāsa-līlā* pastime) was the enactment of a *mahā-rāsa-līlā* dance performance in classical Manipuri style, featuring 108 *gopīs* and 108 Kṛṣṇas, all of them children below the age of ten. Such a *mahā-rāsa* dance, with so many performers, had never been enacted before in the cultural history of Manipur. We pray that this will mark a new beginning for the revival of *bhāgavata* culture in Manipur, as Your Divine Grace so strongly desired.

Since the opening ceremony, many new friends and devotees, being moved by the beauty of Their Lordships and the grandeur of Their half-finished new temple, have come forward to serve your mission in ISKCON Manipur. It is their desire to help complete the construction within the next two to three years with your kind blessings.

As a special offering to your lotus feet and to your many instructions that we should develop a center for scientific Vaiṣṇava culture in Manipur, we have conceived the temple as the centerpiece of the University of Bhāgavata Culture. We plan to teach the science and philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the specific benefit of the younger generation. In this way we hope to spread your glories and your teachings more systematically through the medium of spiritualized education.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very happy to inform you that the 4,000-plus students in our six Bhaktivedanta Institute Mission Schools in the northeastern states of India are progressing nicely. We beg your divine blessings for these children so that they may become worthy citizens of the world and sincere devotees of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

After the opening festivities of the Manimandir, we took 100 *saṅkīrtana* drummers from Manipur and organized the Śrī Śrī Jagannātha Millennium Padayātrā on the 31st of December, 2000. Over 1,000 devotees and guests participated in this millennium *padayātrā,* walking from Daṇḍabhaṅga-līlā Bhūmi of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in Orissa to Jagannātha Purī. Taking permission of the authorities of Śrī Jagannātha temple, we did the *parikrama* of Śrī Jagannātha temple drumming and chanting for the pleasure of Lord Jagannātha. The king of Purī, as well as all the priests of Śrī Jagannātha temple, were extremely pleased with this program, and all of them praised your divine glories, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The *padayātrīs* camped the whole night in at Jagannātha Purī beach (Gandhi Ghat) and continued the chanting and singing of the *harināma mahā-mantra* throughout the whole night. Early in the morning of January 1, 2001, the dawn of the new millennium, the *padayātrī* devotees took a holy dip in the ocean, and the first sunrise of the new millennium in Jagannātha Purī was received with the loud chanting of “*Jaya* Jagannātha and the *harināma mahā-mantra*. The program ended with a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* discourse on the beach on the morning of January 1, 2001, and with a big feast of Lord Jagannātha’s *mahā-prasāda*.

After the Millennium Saṅkīrtana Festival in Jagannātha Purī, we proceeded to participate in the Mahā-Kumbha Mela Festival at Prayag (Allahabad), along with the hundred Manipuri drummers. Our ISKCON camp in the Kumbha Mela was attractive, with the participation of the very dedicated Padayātrā Bulls (Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma) pulling Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura Nitāi, and our chanting of the *harināma mahā-mantra,* along with the hundred drummers, all over the Kumbha Mela grounds. It seemed that the whole Kumbha Mela site was our stage, and everyone there in the Mela highly appreciated your divine mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We also organized a seminar on “The Role of Spirituality in the New Millennium” at our ISKCON camp. Many local scholars and spiritual leaders from different camps of the Kumbha Mela participated in this historic seminar, and everyone who participated in this program spoke very highly of this new addition to the Kumbha Mela festivities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in this ever-turbulent world, we seek your merciful blessings so that we may be able to execute your divine orders.

Your insignificant servant life after life,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble and prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet!

The lotus feet of His Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the abode of unalloyed devotional service. Let us fallen souls bow down with great care and attention in our hearts. By his mercy one will be able to cross over the ocean of birth and death and attain the abode of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

It is impossible to receive the shelter of the Supreme Lord or pure devotional service without first receiving the shelter of the lotus feet of the bona fide spiritual master. As Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura explains, the words *śrī guru* refer to the spiritual master with all spiritual opulence. The opulence of the spiritual master is that he is endowed with the potency to deliver the conditioned souls from the clutches of nescience and bring them to the eternal shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Let the divine words of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, which are emanating from his lotus mouth, enter our hearts. Nothing else should remain there. There is no goal in life save and except serving his instructions. By his mercy all desires are fulfilled.

The words of the spiritual master carry the potency to enable one to return home, back to Godhead. Anyone who is serious about attaining the eternal shelter of the Supreme Lord must engrave the words of the bona fide spiritual master in his heart and consider them to be his very life and soul.

His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us transcendental knowledge that will shine in our hearts forever. Pure love for Lord Kṛṣṇa is emanating from his heart, and that love destroys the ignorance of this world. Let his glories be proclaimed throughout this entire creation.

The transcendental knowledge being delivered by the bona fide spiritual master qualifies one to behold the spiritual reality of our eternal relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa. He initiates the disciple into the process of pure devotional service.

*divyaṁ jñānaṁ yato dadyāt kuryāt pāpasya saṅkṣayam*  
*tasmāt dīkṣeti sā proktā deśikais tattva-kovidaiḥ*

**“Dīkṣā is the process by which one can awaken his transcendental knowledge and vanquish all reactions caused by sinful activity. A person expert in the study of the revealed scriptures knows this process as dīkṣā.” (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa)**

Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura states: *janame janame prabhu,* whether one is a *sādhaka* or in the perfected stage, the spiritual master is always one’s worshipable lord. His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda is an ocean of mercy. He is the teacher and friend of everyone. May he be merciful unto us and give us the shade of his lotus feet.

The sincere disciple is being trained and educated in the science of devotional service by the bona fide spiritual master on the basis of *bhaktyā śruta-gṛhītayā*. *Śruta* means *Veda*. *Svādhyāya-yajña* means to study the Vedic literature, to understand Vedic literature, and to act according to the direction of the Vedic literature.

The process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not some mere sentiment, without any basic principle of knowledge. Rūpa Gosvāmī was given lessons by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu for ten days continually at Allahabad. As a result of his instruction, he first wrote the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*. Similarly, Sanātana Gosvāmī was given instruction for two months at Benares, or Vārāṇasī.

For more than eleven years His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda taught his disciples all over world the process of pure devotional service, strictly following in the footsteps of the Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, and subsequent Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas,* and he will continue to educate and enlighten humanity in pure devotional service through his books, other recorded teachings, and followers for many thousands of years to come.

All glories to His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servant,  
Dāsa A.C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**For the Good of Others**

For the good of others  
For their welfare  
You struggled  
You planned  
You spoke  
You wrote—  
You lived  
For the eternal good of others  
For their Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
You gave all selflessly

For the service of the Unlimited  
You who are unlimited  
pushed us  
who were limited  
beyond our capacity  
beyond our sincerity

Straining and immature  
helping and hurting others  
—sometimes terribly—  
we sometimes  
like a tightened rubber bands  
released  
retracting forcibly back again  
to our normalcy

I’m learning  
Śrīla Prabhupāda  
That only the viewpoint  
of eternity  
reveals perfection  
Your eye  
and heart on Braja  
saw only eternal good  
And Kṛṣṇa’s indomitable will  
And saving fallen *jīvas*

Prabhupāda  
I read and talk  
of Philosophy  
of eternality  
of Goloka  
with intelligence  
and lecturing words I speak  
But realization—  
My soul longs  
to ease this duality  
longs to see from eternity  
from Braja in Kṛṣṇa’s service

Granted Braja vision  
How all would change!  
How different austerity would seem!  
As would inconvenience  
pleasure and pain!

But now  
soul and vision trapped  
encased  
small and dull  
like a jewel sparkling within  
fleeting time’s mud  
Times change  
lighting shifts the landscape view  
Noontime judges dawn

Yet clearly—  
for the good of others  
for their welfare  
You struggled  
You planned  
You spoke  
You wrote—  
You lived  
For the eternal good of others  
For their Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
You gave all selflessly

To that eternal good  
of Braja’s loving service  
that you carried—  
To that love  
inspiring your gift—  
and to you who act  
only for the good of others  
I offer my obeisances forever

Your eternal servant,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

This year after the GBC meetings I embarked on a tour of South India accompanied by twenty devotees. The purpose of this trip was to deepen my appreciation for the great gift you have given me—Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the Vedic culture—and thereby become more enthused in my service to you. Also I wanted to help the devotees who were traveling with me deepen their appreciation for your mercy.

The trip was undoubtedly successful in this regard, but in addition you arranged for some other important lessons for us.

We saw so many Deities, magnificent temples, places of pastimes, and places of the *ācāryas,* but I felt happiest when I was visiting your temples. Everywhere else I felt like a transcendental tourist, but when coming to your places I felt at home.

In fact, I rushed through some very important holy places—Śrī Raṅgam and Kāñcīpuram—so that I could be in your Tirupati temple for the celebration of Gaura Pūrṇimā. I could not envision being anywhere else than in one of your temples for this most important day.

Your devotees in the ISKCON Tirupati temple were so kind to us, exhibiting such wonderful Vaiṣṇava behavior, that we felt that we had directly entered the spiritual world. Their worship of the gorgeous Deities Rādhā-Govinda, the presiding Deities of ISKCON Tirupati, would attract the mind of even the most hard-core materialist. I realized that this display of devotion was due to your presence.

Furthermore I realized that your presentation of preaching, being the most important activity of the Vedic culture, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, was the clearest presentation of the *yuga-dharma, saṅkīrtana*. In every age there is a different primary process for self-realization. In Satya-yuga there was meditation. In Tretā, opulent sacrifices. In Dvāpara, opulent temple worship. And in Kali, *saṅkīrtana*.

Because your temples are based upon this preaching, they fully present the spiritual world. Your temples have life. There is no doubt that those who have a connection with you and this great movement you have started are embarking on the quickest path back to home, back to Godhead.

Thank you again and again for giving this fallen rascal these understandings. The only way that I can repay you is to attempt to serve you eternally. Please give me this boon.

Your servant,  
Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

## Giridhārī Swami

**Bhakta-sane Vāsa**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you on this auspicious day of your appearance!

Reading your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.6.51, I am reminded of how fortunate I was to have come in contact with your followers:

Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura also recommends that one engage in the service of the *ācāryas,* and if one wants to live in association, he must live in the association of devotees (*tāṅdera caraṇa sevi bhakta-sane vāsa*). The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is creating many centers just to create devotees so that by associating with the members of such a center people will automatically become uninterested in material affairs. Although this is an ambitious proposal, this association is proving effective by the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. By gradually associating with the members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, simply by taking *prasāda* and taking part in chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, ordinary persons are being considerably elevated.

One day I received your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and began to taste the pleasure of chanting the holy name and following the principles. I began corresponding with Svarūpa dāsa in Los Angeles, and he patiently answered all my questions and sent me more of your books and some of your lecture tapes. At the time I was living in the Midwest, hundreds of miles from the nearest temple, and I remember looking forward in great anticipation to receiving those letters from him. During that first year, he helped me in many ways to come in contact with you and your devotees. I am greatly indebted to him.

After some time, I moved to Florida. I continued chanting my sixteen rounds, following the principles, reading your books, and listening to your taped lectures. I had not yet visited one of your temples nor met one of your devotees. Svarūpa Prabhu then sent me a BTG with the complete list of temples. I noticed that ISKCON Gainesville was only about sixty miles from where I was living. I was elated.

Early one Sunday afternoon I drove to Gainesville. That was easy enough, but finding the temple was not. I drove for hours and couldn’t find it at the address given. I stopped again and again, asking people if they knew where the Hare Kṛṣṇa temple was.

“*Hare* what?” they would say. I was having a tough time and was just about ready to give up and drive home. Then, out of frustration and disappointment, I prayed to Lord Kṛṣṇa for guidance. Those prayers were answered, and the next person knew where the temple was. As it turned out, I had passed it many times, but the temple was far back from the road and there was no sign. Looking back, I think this was Kṛṣṇa’s test to see how determined I was.

Heading up the long driveway, I was filled with anticipation and uncertainty. As I stepped out of the car, I heard the celestial sounds of *karatālas* ringing and *mṛdaṅgas* beating, with the voices of many devotees sweetly chanting the holy name. The fragrance of incense entered my nostrils. It was an overwhelming experience, something definitely not of this world. Just as I reached the door, out came an effulgent monk with a freshly shaved head.

“Welcome,” he said with a broad smile. “My name is Bhakta Ron. Come in.”

*“What a nice guy!”* I thought. *“He treats me just like a brother.”*

Bhakta Ron led me to the temple room where the *kīrtana* was just finishing. He showed me how to offer obeisances to Your Divine Grace and Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. As I looked at the beautiful forms of the Deities and saw the devotees chanting and dancing blissfully, I felt like a long-lost family member who had finally come home. Although I was seeing this for the first time in this lifetime, nothing seemed foreign or strange. The whole experience felt natural, as if I had done these things many times in the past.

Then Bhakta Ron invited me to honor *prasādam* with the other guests. The devotees fed me plate after plate of delicious *prasādam*. I especially relished the *halavā* and *samosās*. Now I had first-hand experience of what it meant to be full up to the neck.

During *prasādam,* one devotee after another took turns preaching to me. Then a guest sitting close to me said something blasphemous about you. A huge, bare-chested devotee stood up. It was Amarendra dāsa, the temple president. He turned and glowered at the guest. “One more word out of you,” he said, “and I’ll knock your teeth down your throat.” The guest became quiet and meek.

After *prasādam,* the devotees took me to the *āśrama*. No less than ten devotees continued to preach to me until midnight. Every so often a devotee would come in with some *mahā-prasādam* from an offering and give it to me. It was clear that they wanted me to surrender right then and there. It was hard to resist their logic, reason, and love. Then they invited me to spend the night, since it was already late, but I have a conservative nature and I am not one to rush into things, so I deftly explained that my worldly responsibilities needed to be wound down before I could move into he temple. I promised to return soon, and they finally let me leave.

As I started my car, I took a deep breath and sighed in relief. “Whew,” I thought, “that was a close call.” Of course, I could not stop thinking about my experience as I drove home. I would become a full-time devotee some day, I concluded, but not yet.

After some weeks, Svarūpa Prabhu wrote me about a center in Tampa. I phoned, and a devotee enthusiastically invited me to visit the next Sunday. I took him up on the offer and had almost identical experiences with the Deities, the devotees, *kīrtana,* and *prasādam*. This time, however, I met a special devotee who charmed me in every way with his mastery in presenting the philosophy, his strong conviction, and his brotherly love. My time to become serious about Kṛṣṇa consciousness had finally come, and your servant Keśava Bhāratī Prabhu had been sent to bring me back to your spiritual family.

Keśava Bhāratī Prabhu, like the devotees in Gainesville, wanted me to surrender on the spot and move into the temple. I explained that I was running a small business and needed time to sell the business and equipment.

“Okay,” he said, “you’ve got one week to tie up all the loose ends.”

“*That’s impossible,*” I thought, but out of respect and love for this devotee, I couldn’t say no.

The pressure was on. My family and friends thought I was crazy, but they respected my decision and did not to try to dissuade me. I worked like a madman, calling everyone I knew and offering them the business for a song. Finally, on the fifth day, a stranger called and said he was interested. We closed the deal the next morning. On the seventh day, exactly one week later, an old, broken-down Volkswagen beetle was sent from the temple to pick me up. That Sunday, I surrendered $10,000 and my life to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa. I am greatly indebted to Keśava Bhāratī Prabhu, who acted as your emissary and convinced me to join as a full-time devotee of your wonderful ISKCON movement, a decision I have never regretted.

Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s instruction to engage in the service of the *ācāryas* and live in the association of Vaiṣṇavas is perfect and complete. Your devotees mercifully picked me up from the depths of illusion and kindly delivered me at the dust of your lotus feet. I can only attempt to repay this great debt by using my body, mind, and words birth after birth to help them bring more and more souls to surrender at your divine lotus feet.

Your eternal servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your merciful presence in our lives!

Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī prayed to his beloved Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī:

*tavaivāsmi tavaivāsmi na jīvāmi tvayā vinā*  
*iti vijñāya devi tvaṁ naya māṁ caraṇāntikam*

**“I am Yours! I am Yours! I cannot live without You. Understanding this, please bring me to Your lotus feet.”**

What else can I write to you?

You are everything to me. All things have value only in relation to your service. Otherwise they mean nothing to me.

After all, I want only you and service to you and your beloveds.

Kindly favor me and bring me to your lotus feet.

Your eternal servant,  
Girirāj Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Once again we are celebrating your most glorious appearance on this planet, and I am feeling totally unable to describe your numerous transcendental qualities and achievements. I have nothing to offer you but what you have given. I hope you will accept this humble offering, just as the Ganges accepts offerings of Ganges water.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you appeared in order to help realize Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the holy name would spread to every town and village. Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda had predicted your appearance by stating that a personality would soon appear who would travel all over the world to spread the teachings of Lord Caitanya. You established temples all over the world so that the conditioned souls could associate with your devotees and hear philosophical discourses based on your books. You introduced Kṛṣṇa conscious *prasādam,* which purifies the mind and senses and gives us a higher taste. Now the Food for Life program enables thousands around the world to eat *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. You gave us the opportunity to worship the Supreme Lord in His personal Deity form after so many births of impersonal conditioning. Your transcendental teachings, recorded in your books and tapes, have saved countless conditioned souls and will continue to save humanity for the next ten thousand years. No one within the three worlds can sufficiently praise your glorious activities.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.13.10) King Yudhiṣṭhira spoke to Vidura as follows: “My lord, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage.” This description of Vidura is very appropriate for yourself. Your only desire in life was to serve the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and engage everyone else in service to the Lord also. By your purity you were able to convert thousands of *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas*. You gave us an ideal Kṛṣṇa conscious example to follow.

You left us with a vision and a mission. You wanted ISKCON to be recognized in history as a movement that has saved humanity from going to hell. You wrote: “At the present moment there is nothing but great calamity, but by injecting Kṛṣṇa consciousness into the masses, we can change the situation greatly.” We have to work hard to realize this instruction of yours. You described ISKCON as your body and book distribution as your heart. Unfortunately, all is not well in your body and heart. Sections of devotees are not satisfied with the leadership, and the preaching is not expanding as you wanted. In fact, in some areas the preaching and book distribution have gone down. We need to remain humble, more humble than a straw in the street, so as to develop our taste for chanting the *mahā-mantra*. We need to emphasize the importance of austerity and purity so that the preaching spirit expands among the devotees to help realize your vision to see the preaching and book distribution expand. Differences among devotees can be settled only by sticking to your instructions. Everything we need to know for going back to Godhead and running your institution is in your books and instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me so that I may have the chance to keep serving you and your mission in some way, and please bless me so that I may not forget your transcendental instructions. Also, please bless me so that I may never offend you or your devotees.

Your insignificant servant,  
Gopāl Krishna Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

At the time of my initiation, I knew that I was accepting a bona fide spiritual master, but I did not know what that entailed. With the passing of every year, my awareness increases of how you embody the sum total of all qualities attributed to a topmost *mahā-bhāgavata*. It’s not that I did not read about those descriptions of a pure devotee mentioned in your writings and speeches, but I could never really fathom how special they are.

Previously we all thought that what you describe so deftly as real spiritual life is something at our fingertips. While being theoretically so, it has been proven time and time again, even in observing the lives of more advanced Vaiṣṇavas, that the position of one who bears the title Prabhupāda is indeed a very special one. According to your purports, the definition given by Jīva Gosvāmī is “one who occupies the post of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.” You are demanding so much of us, and as years go by I can observe that there are very few who can live up to your expectations.

Some question, Why have so many failed if Śrīla Prabhupāda had perfect vision? I ponder that such vision is not cheap. Even more important, your standards and indications are a manifestation of the purest love and caring compassion. As stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.88.8):

*yasyāham anugṛhṇāmi hariṣye tad-dhanaṁ śanaiḥ*  
*tato ’dhanaṁ tyajanty asya svajanā duḥkha-duḥkhitam*

The Lord shows special favor by taking away the obstacles in one’s spiritual path. But few are they who perceive what they really need, or what is truly interfering with their progress. But you have paved a perfect path for those who can appreciate how much you love them. So much so that you have taught us a process that may not be so easy, or even popular, at every step. However, it is steeped in loving care and concern. It is perfectly molded and insulated to assure that Māyā cannot enter or penetrate through any nook or crevice.

I pray to never lose the understanding of how merciful you are. Please don’t allow my senses and their aimless groping to obscure the glimmers of transcendental hope that penetrate my dull and torpid mind. Thank you for your causeless mercy, so adroitly engineered to prod me, by applied discipline, beyond the comfort zone of material existence.

Your fallen but hopeful servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

I offer my earnest obeisances at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata-śrī-śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is most dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa in this world, having achieved full shelter at His lotus feet.

Once, in the mid-seventies, we sat with Śrīla Prabhupāda in his garden in Los Angeles on a lovely summer evening. At one point he looked at us sitting at his feet and boldly said, “What is the use of your being Americans unless you do something wonderful to spread this movement?”

Those words entered my heart and have continued to boldly challenge and inspire me to this day. In Śrīla Prabhupāda’s first *praṇāma* mantra, we glorify him by saying *śrīmate,* literally “unto Śrīla Prabhupāda, who possesses *śrī—*opulence, beauty, good fortune.” In his second *praṇāma* mantra, we glorify him with the words *gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe,* “unto Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is preaching the message of Gaura, Lord Caitanya.” The word *pracāriṇe,* which we translate as “preaching,” literally means “unto one who is causing \[the message of Gaura\] to go forth.” We also glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda as *pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe,* “unto the savior of the Western countries.” The word *tāriṇe,* which we translate as “unto the savior,” literally means “unto Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is taking \[the Western countries\] across,” that is, across the material ocean to the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

We do not chant these words as a mere formality, simply because Śrīla Prabhupāda is our spiritual master. Rather, he powerfully demonstrated by his practical deeds a unique and inconceivably marvelous ability to cause the message of Lord Caitanya to go forth into the world and to bring the Western countries across the material ocean to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Today at sunset, as I returned home from my evening walk after chanting the Gāyatrī mantra, which he gave to me, I reflected upon the relationship of his bold command to us in his garden and these words of his *praṇāma* mantras. It became more profoundly clear to me that we can fulfill Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire that we do something wonderful only by surrendering ever more sincerely and completely to his lotus feet. For it is Śrīla Prabhupāda who possesses *śrī,* full success in the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, it is Śrīla Prabhupāda who most perfectly sends forth Gaurāṅga’s message, and it is Śrīla Prabhupāda who will bring these Western countries, and the rest of the planet with them, across the material ocean to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

I might also state here that just as Śrīla Prabhupāda expected his American disciples to do something wonderful, it is safe to say that each loyal follower of Śrīla Prabhupāda should hear Śrīla Prabhupāda saying to him or her: “What is the use of your taking birth in, or preaching in, Africa, or Europe, or South America, or Australasia, or India, or East Asia, or Southeast Asia, or Canada, or any other place, unless you do something wonderful in that place for the glory of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu?” And then we should all remember that our ability to do something wonderful depends directly and completely on our ability to surrender ourselves at the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda. And so glorious and merciful is Śrīla Prabhupāda, that even when our efforts to surrender are inadequate, as they almost always are, he will bestow his causeless mercy and aid, enhance and complete our surrender so that we may share in the bliss and the glory of his pure love and pure service for Lord Caitanya.

Falling at the feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda in a sincere attempt to find shelter there,

His servant,  
Hridayānanda das Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear spiritual father Śrīla Oṁ Viṣṇupāda A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda Ṭhākura,

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, my beloved spiritual master! Your Divine Grace has instructed how a disciple should take the instructions of his spiritual master as more dear then his own life. We have read and studied your spiritual master’s final instruction to his disciples. To see how you have diligently carried out all of his instructions demonstrates your sublime greatness. This gives us great inspiration.

Seeing how you have fulfilled His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s final written instructions to his disciples, translated into English, is a clear testimonial to your dedication to fulfilling the instructions of your spiritual master, and is presented below. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura wrote:

Please do Navadwip mandal Parikrama as quickly as possible. This activity gives one pure devotion for Lord Kṛṣṇa! Please try to serve the holy dham of Sri Mayapur and create some permanent developments for the dham; carefully see that Sri Mayapur’s opulence, beauty, fame and far-reaching influence and glory expand further. Establish a printing press, and organize devotional literatures, sastras, etc. to be distributed, and spread the nama hatta preaching so that everyone can serve the holy abode of Sridham Mayapur in a beautiful manner...That is my heartfelt keen desire. Try to organize these things attentively.

**To develop the Navadvīpa Dhāma Parikrama:**

So, this instruction has been a source of inspiration. Your Divine Grace took us on Navadvīpa Dhāma *parikrama* and asked us to develop it. On your instructions the Navadvīpa dhāma *parikrama* has been performed since 1972, and for the past seven or more years now the thousands of devotees on *parikrama* have walked to all nine islands, camping out at night. These *parikramas* have brought us closer to the holy *dhāma*. Some of the *gupta-tīrthas* (hidden holy places) have been located by your causeless mercy. In the future, with your blessings, the Navadvīpa Dhāma *parikrama* will certainly be further developed.

The Bhaktivedanta Swami Charity Trust, with the help of the various trustees and the Sārasvata Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava Association members, has also been helping in the development of the Navadvīpa Dhāma *parikrama* for your pleasure and the pleasure of the previous *ācāryas*.

**To Develop Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma**

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura talked about developing Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma. Your Divine Grace explained to us how the birthplace of Lord Caitanya was discovered by Jagannātha dāsa Bābājī; how Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura developed the birthplace and begged door to door to build the first temple; how Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābājī was deeply devoted to the holy *dhāma* of Navadvīpa—Māyāpur *dhāma*—and how Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura personally developed various places in the *dhāma,* including building the *yoga-pīṭha* (birthplace) temple and the Caitanya Maṭha. Your Divine Grace, our beloved spiritual master, mentioned in your humble way that you also tried to follow and please your spiritual master and all the previous *ācāryas* by fulfilling this instruction to develop Māyāpur Dhāma. So therefore the Śrī Māyāpur Chandrodaya Mandir has been established, and we are in the process of further developing the preaching activities and creating a *karma-sthāna* of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma. We want your blessings to build the wonderful temple for Caitanya Mahāprabhu in the future, to spread Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s glories all over the world.

**To Publish Kṛṣṇa Conscious Literature**

Your Divine Grace translated and published so many different books in many languages of the world, and your instruction is to publish these transcendental literatures in all the languages of the world. All of Kṛṣṇa’s teachings—the *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta—*should be published in this way. About 450 million literatures have been published and distributed so far, in about 100 languages, with 3.5 million books being distributed in 1998. We beg for the blessings of Your Divine Grace for completing all the remaining books in all the remaining countries and languages of the world.

**Developing the Nāma Haṭṭa**

Your Divine Grace’s instructions to develop small groups and Nāma Haṭṭa groups has also inspired us very much to develop the congregational preaching so that everyone can engage in Kṛṣṇa conscious activities at home and serve Māyāpur Dhāma. Here in India, Nāma Haṭṭa is rapidly expanding, with one thousand Nāma Haṭṭa *saṅghas* and five hundred Śraddhā Kuṭīrs throughout Bengal, Assam, and Orissa. Similarly, in almost all countries of the world, including Australia, Malaysia, England, South America, South Africa, the United States, Russia, and those in the Middle East, congregational preaching is developing nicely. In this way the Nāma Haṭṭa *bhakti-vṛkṣa* is spreading its branches and growing all around the world.
