We pray at the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace to be blessed that we may expand the Nāma Haṭṭa unlimitedly for your pleasure and the pleasure of all the previous *ācāryas*.

**To Work Cooperatively Under a Governing Body Commission**

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura instructed his followers to work cooperatively by forming and working under a Governing Body Commission. That isn’t happening throughout the Gauḍīya Maṭh, but Your Divine Grace, ISKCON’s beloved founder-*ācārya*, established this system in ISKCON for the satisfaction of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Your Divine Grace explained that everything you did in ISKCON is an offering of ecstasy at the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace’s spiritual master. Therefore Your Divine Grace also told us to follow this instruction of cooperating under a GBC. In ISKCON you personally established the Governing Body Commission for fulfilling your instructions and protecting the purity of ISKCON.

Although we have almost one hundred spiritual masters, everyone—spiritual masters, *sannyāsīs, brāhmaṇas,* preachers, and all other devotees—are working cooperatively according to *sādhu, śāstra,* and guru under the coordination and spiritual direction of the Governing Body Commission. Please bless us that we in ISKCON can continue to work cooperatively and follow this instruction to work under the Governing Body Commission system.

Your Divine Grace personally established the Bhaktivedanta Swami Charity Trust, which has many purposes, one of which is to unite the Sārasvata Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava disciplic family. To fulfill your instructions, the Sārasvata Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava Association was organized and is functioning. We beg for your blessings that in the future all of the disciplic descendants from Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura can learn to work together cooperatively, encouraging one another in the service of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. With your causeless mercy we are hopeful that one day there will be a united greater “Sārasvata” family better equipped to spread the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu widely and more effectively all over the planet. In this way all the *apa-sampradāya* influences will be eradicated and the love of Lord Kṛṣṇa given by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu can flood all three worlds, delivering the conditioned souls from their suffering and despair. Most of the disciplic followers of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura are offering a joint worship for Your Divine Grace on your Vyāsa-pūjā Day in the evening in Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma, and this is a step toward our working in a united way as you desired.

Hundreds and hundreds of times I offer you my obeisances, prostrated at your lotus feet. I cannot say that I have exhaustively written all the ways you have fulfilled your beloved spiritual masters instructions. The most important was to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the Western world, which was something personally given to you. Only because you fulfilled that instruction am I so fortunate today to have gotten the opportunity to be your disciple and be in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Everything you have done is a living testimony to your dedication to the principle of following the order of the spiritual master. Please bless me to be able to also follow in your footsteps in this and all regards.

Ever dependent on your causeless mercy,  
Your insignificant servitor,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I am writing this at the ISKCON Arogya Ashram, near the house of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. About six hundred devotees from more than eighty countries are performing Navadvīpa *dhāma parikrama,* as per the wish of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. The devotees are chanting the *mahā-mantra* and dancing in ecstasy while being absorbed in the dust of the holy *dhāma*.

Seeing so many young and enthusiastic Vaiṣṇavas is giving us great faith in the future as we hear about the desire of the previous *ācāryas* and begin to understand what a great contribution you made to fulfilling the all-merciful mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

The *dhāma-vāsīs* are fascinated by your “dancing white elephants.” I am reminded of Lakṣmī-devī performing *tapasya* to get the blessings of the *vrajavāsīs,* and pray that I can become humble enough to get the mercy of the *dhāma-vāsīs*.

If I can get just a drop of your mercy, my life will be successful. But I am like an alkaline field, where nothing can grow. Still you continue to shower your mercy, and you never succumb to the pessimism that abounds in this modern society, which is so much under the influence of Kali-yuga.

Your unflinching faith in the order of your Guru Mahārāja produces many miracles. As we remember your enthusiasm, determination, and patience, we are inspired to carry on.

Please let me always remember your optimistic determination, and let me try to assist those who are working so hard to follow your orders.

Your mercy is all that I am made of.

The aspiring servant of your servants,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Thank you for giving me the possibility of still being here writing a Vyāsa-pūjā offering for Your Divine Grace.

Gone are the times when I thought that the conquest of the world in eighteen days would be an easy job, along with my elevation to pure devotional service. A tiny realization of the unfathomable depths of the ocean of ignorance has brought me down to earth. Its huge waves of pride and self-deceit are tossing me constantly to the material side of the time factor, holding me captive in the petrified forest of desire and hate. And this oppressive feeling of impotence is a condition that seems to never end.

You are my only, only, only hold, hope, and refuge.

In a letter to Madhudvīśa you wrote:

So this dreaming condition is called non-liberated life, and this is just like a dream. Although in this material calculation it is a long, long period, as soon as we come to Krsna Consciousness then this period is considered as a second.

When I was a three-year-old child I lost my mother, of whom I have kept in my mind one very clear image: she is in the dining room smiling at me while the doctor takes a sample of her blood. The cancer took her away shortly after that. I missed her a lot when I was a child, day after day being permeated by a kind of sadness I could not explain. I used to go to sleep thinking that tomorrow my mother would come and wake me up from this bad dream haunted by her absence, and in one instant everything would be all right. In due course of time I resigned myself to the hard facts of life, and grew disillusioned.

Than something happened.

I obtained your association, and relishing the sweetness of your affection, I realized that you are my real parent. With you I felt a relationship that originates beyond the limits of time and space, so deep and fulfilling. Nothing can compare to the greatness of your love for this poor living entity, and I have given up the presumptuousness of trying to find a reason for it or its extension. I just know it is there with all its splendor.

I am very sad, though, because I am missing you thousands of times more than my material mother, and I need your loving protection.

Therefore I submit this plea at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda: Although I do not and will never deserve it, please wake me up from this bad dream. Take me back to your lotus feet, to my real family, back home, back to Godhead. Let this endless life of obtuseness be reduced to an instant.

*kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāro,*  
*tomāra śakati āche*

Should you deem my request improper, please at least give me the patience and intelligence to wait for you at the rendezvous with death without my keeping you at a distance by my offensive attitude.

Grabbing your lotus feet, I remain,  
Your humble servant,  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

**Meditating on Śrīla Prabhupāda**

My dearest spiritual master, savior of the three worlds, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humbly offered obeisances at your lotus feet.

Along with the appearance of the four Kumāras at the palace of King Pṛthu came the transcendental presence of Lord Viṣṇu, as well as the Kumāras’ younger brother, the venerable Lord Śiva.

In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.22.8, you explain that the *brāhmaṇas* and Vaiṣṇavas are the “bearers of Lord Viṣṇu,” whom they carry out of their extreme love for Him. Although the Lord is present in the hearts of all living entities, it is only the Vaiṣṇavas and *brāhmaṇas* who actually perceive and see Him, “always in ecstasy.” This appearance of the Lord along with His devotees is actually very natural. This conclusion is sustained by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura: *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ* By pleasing the spiritual master, who is “both *brāhmaṇa* and Vaiṣṇava,” one pleases the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

By this divine arrangement, in practical application, we can understand that Lord Kṛṣṇa accompanied you all over the world. And in a further display of transcendental love, sometimes He remained behind in His *arcā-vigraha* form while simultaneously going along with you to the next destination! Thus, the footprints of the pure devotee accompanied by those of his most loving Lord encircle this lowly planet earth, as well as our very own hearts.

On this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I beg to be allowed to remain, a small speck of nothing, serving your most blessed lotus feet.

Your fallen daughter,  
Mālatī-devī dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

**To the Master of the Written Word**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The written word has altered history.  
It’s not just the rhyme that tells the story,  
Or crusty tombs old and hoary,  
But brave new words that signaled glory.

The wind blows paper without return,  
But the words have made hearts burn.

Words gave humanity hope  
For truth,  
As through tunnels we grope.

Ink on paper that goes up in smoke  
Yet moves mountains; what it can evoke  
Has the strongest smote.

Written words can make us groan  
And ache for a presence, evoke a moan.

Words on paper,  
Like a personal face,  
Give vision to a dying race.

Words on a leaf of palm  
Stirred the God of Harinām.

And later on,  
Like water falling off a leaf,  
Time has robbed us like a thief.

Some words are remembered for all time.  
I will make some of them mine.

“Arrive PAA 121, 14 Dec (1967)”  
A telegram that changed earth to heaven.

Gossamer pieces of paper  
Have generated groundswells,  
Changed landscapes,  
Pushed buttons and rung bells.

A foothold you carved where there wasn’t a blip;  
Masses today are boarding your ship.

Words changed the world  
For Christians, for Communists,  
For sentimentalists, for nihilists.

And words can be books.

\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*

Like everything else in the material world, books have only transitory value. The great classics will one day become footnotes and then be lost in oblivion. Scripture, however, seems to endure.

But your works stand alone. Few accept your assertion that they’ll be the law books for our future. But let’s look at some facts.

Your writings now contribute to the world’s philosophical and theological body of knowledge; they enrich the literary heritage of the planet; they provide academics with a reference point to understand the true nature of the Vedic way; they bring personal fulfillment and satisfaction; they help convince us that ultimate knowledge dwells within the hearts and minds of the great sages; they act as an instruction manual by which one can operate in the material world; they present solutions to contemporary problems; they fulfill the desire of spiritual predecessors to spread love of God; they provide an alternative to materialism; they clearly explain the nature of material existence; they answer the most probing philosophical questions, such as “Who am I?”, “Who is God?”, “What is time, nature, and destiny?”, and “Where am I going?”

They systematically propagate spiritual knowledge to society at large and educate all peoples in the techniques of spiritual life, help check the imbalance of values in life, and move us toward real unity and peace. (Adopted from the first of your “Seven Purposes of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness \[ISKCON\]”)

Introduced in the 1980s in many leading universities, your books are current in many of them, having found a home in a large sector of academia.

Most members of the ISKCON commit themselves to the philosophy, practice, and spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness because of knowledge gained from your books.

You called it the “Hare Krishna movement” because, within the timeless wisdom of your words on paper, there emerged a grand scheme, an agenda for massive social change.

You took on the world, intellectually and philosophically, challenging and exposing scientists and psychologists, as well as political and religious leaders, the cognoscenti, the literati, the liberals, the conservatives, and the herd of independent minds.

With the written word you educated; made your movement grow from grass roots to groundswells. Eventually they’ll be mountains.

Your servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear eternal father, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your appearance I would like to speak about gratitude. I just came from Jagannātha Purī, where I prayed to Lord Jagannātha to give me inspiration to write my offering to you. Then I had the inspiration to write about gratitude.

Gratitude is one of the most important sentiments that maintain one’s spiritual life and inspire one to go on in one’s spiritual practices and devotional service to the guru. Various passages in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Bhagavad-gītā,* and other Vaiṣṇava literatures describe how Kṛṣṇa and his devotees exchange feelings of gratitude.

Gratitude is something practical and tangible. Recently I was lamenting because due to much travel my bodily functions had become completely disturbed. Then I remembered you—how much you traveled everywhere just to help the devotees and the general mass of people develop attraction for devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, how you tirelessly visited your temples and farm communities to train your disciples, solve their problems, inspire them to go out and preach and print books, increase their attachment to Kṛṣṇa by improving their *śravaṇa* and *kīrtana*.... All these things and much more you did out of love and compassion. ISKCON is the result of your sacrifice and austerities. Your knew that unless an organized society was established with temples, etc., humain society could not benefit from the precious gem of devotional service. Despite all problems and difficulties, therefore, your society must be kept alive, for it is the fruit of your devotion and dedication to your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Ṭhākura.

It is the duty of your disciples, grand-disciples, and future generations of your followers to be careful and not spoil what you built with such great endeavor. This will be a sign of our gratitude to you. But to do this, we must keep our spiritual lives healthy by regularly hearing and chanting the holy names of Kṛṣṇa, reading your books, and serving the devotees. This will be our greatest expression of gratitude to you.

Therefore, my dear master, I pray that I may one day be blessed to develop such gratitude to you, my eternal savior and only shelter.

Your eternal servant,  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! I am presently in Vṛndāvana, so this is a nice opportunity to meditate on your pastimes here.

Vṛndāvana *dhāma* on this planet obviously occupied a central position in your life, as it is nondifferent from the eternal abode of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual realm. Kṛṣṇa is always present here in His thousands of temples, and His holy name is always on the lips of the *vraja-vāsīs,* who greet each other by calling “Rādhe Śyām!” It was here that you lived for so many years in the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, meditating on the order of your spiritual master to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, and preparing yourself for that monumental task by translating the First Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* as well as writing other articles and books.

After you went to the West and started the movement, Vṛndāvana was always in your thoughts. When your health was poor, this was the place where you came to recuperate before returning to the Western preaching fronts. So you obviously considered it your home. You confirmed that in 1977 when you wrote to a disciple:

I was staying in Risikesh hoping to improve my health but instead I have become a little weaker. Now I have come back to my home, Vrndavana. If anything should go wrong, at least I will be here in Vrndavana. Vrndavana is for residence, Bombay is office for organization, and Mayapur is for worship of the Supreme Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

When you first brought a group of Western devotees to India for preaching, you soon designated some of them to start a center here. Although at that time ISKCON in India was just in the beginning stages, you had a definite plan to establish an international temple and guesthouse here so that people from all countries could come and savor the mood of Vṛndāvana and thus develop their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Due to your spiritual potency, clear vision, organizational skills, and, of course, Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, it only took a few years for your plan to become a reality. You personally oversaw every stage of the construction, and it was a great triumph for you and all of ISKCON when you personally inaugurated the Śrī Śrī Krishna-Balarām Mandir in 1975. This temple quickly became the most popular and dynamic in Vṛndāvana, and remains so till this day.

You started the annual Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana festival and invited the devotees from all over the world to attend. I had the privilege to first come here in 1974 while the temple was still under construction. You gave lectures every day at Fogel Ashram to inspire your army of disciples to increase the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Although your house was incomplete, you were living there, giving directions for the development of the project. I can remember being present in your room that year when one of the senior *sannyāsīs* was leaving for his preaching field overseas. You told him, “You are a perfect leader.”

He replied, “I am just trying to follow you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

“Yes, of course,” you said. “Unless one follows, he cannot be a leader.”

You were always a staunch and dedicated follower of your Guru Mahārāja. On one occasion I heard you say with genuine humility, “My only qualification is that I tried to follow my Guru Mahārāja.” And because of your faithfulness and dedication in following the *paramparā,* Kṛṣṇa empowered you to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

After the opening of the Krishna-Balarām Mandir, you came here regularly to preach, as you always considered your temples primarily as preaching projects and not just places to eat and sleep by making a business of showing the Deity (although you always stressed the importance of purity, punctuality, opulence, and above all devotion in the worship of the Deity, who is Kṛṣṇa Himself). Despite poor health, you preached vigorously here in 1977 at the Vṛndāvana portion of the Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana festival. In July of that year I came here to report to you about our preaching in Bangladesh and also to ask your permission to go and open a center in Nepal. You encouraged me by telling me to preach with the “courage of an Englishman and the heart of a Bengali mother,” and were so kind as to sanction regular financial support for our fledgling preaching efforts from your *guru-dakṣiṇā,* which you kept in the Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana Trust. You were always ready to spend money liberally for preaching, as you considered it to be the best use for money. Indeed, preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness was your life and soul, and you preached here right up to the final moment of your pastimes on this planet.

Visvambhar—Bhagatji, your old friend—was always fond of relating how you once told him that you wanted to be like a soldier on the battlefield up to the last moment. Even though the soldier’s head is cut off, his arm holding the sword may still move as if trying to continue to fight. In that way you desired to leave your body in the most glorious way, preaching and fighting for Kṛṣṇa till the last moment. And in that you certainly succeeded, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You went on dictating your Bhaktivedanta purports, absorbed in meditation on your Lord right up to the time when Kṛṣṇa gently took you away from your mortal frame to His spiritual realm. You departed at the most spiritually auspicious time—during the month of Kārttika—from the most spiritually auspicious place—Śrī Śrī Krishna-Balarām Mandir, in Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma—*in the most spiritually auspicious circumstances—surrounded by loving devotees chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. In this way, just as you preached to us and taught us by your living example, so you also preached to us and taught us by the example of your departure—how to lead a perfect life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and how to bring it to a perfect conclusion. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I humbly pray that I may always follow in your footsteps.

Your unworthy servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Praghoṣa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Because of your ability to see through insincerity and hypocrisy, this is the one time of the year that I have difficulty expressing myself. Your incomparable qualities are innumerable, so there is no shortage of subject matter by which to glorify you. But I think I know you enough to understand that simple glorification by words is not what is most dear to you. Rather, my genuine attempts at becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and then sincerely trying to give others Kṛṣṇa consciousness, without any hint of attachment for self-aggrandizement, is what I understand will truly please you.

There are certainly many wonderful examples of devotees who are dedicated to this course of action, and I pray for your mercy so that I will be guided by their sober example.

I also pray for your special mercy to understand how best to respond to the current difficulties facing your beloved ISKCON. On the one hand, I don’t want to be tricked by Māyā into overreacting to criticisms and complaints that are in effect just Trojan horses designed to destabilize and undermine what you worked so hard to establish. On the other hand, I don’t want to neglect whatever my responsibilities may be in helping to highlight and ultimately rectify the sincere and genuine concerns expressed by your loyal followers—concerns regarding your beloved ISKCON slipping further away from the vision that you created for it all those years ago. We are all in need of shelter and security, which you provided in so much abundance when you were physically with us. I have no doubt that you expect us to continue to give that shelter and security through the medium of your ISKCON, and for us to fail to do so will mean that we have failed you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Whatever is the best course of action to take, one thing is sure: we all need your help and guidance as much as, if not more than, ever. Indeed, it seems that the more time that passes since your physical disappearance, the more we can appreciate how much we need you and your transcendental expertise. In the not-to-distant future, there will only be servants like myself who never personally met you, but if we can continue to realize that our need for you is *nitya-vardhanam*—ever-increasing—that in itself will be a glorious testament to your eternal greatness. May I always be blessed with the need for your shelter and guidance, and may I always remain worthy of receiving the same.

Your servant,  
Praghoṣa dāsa

## Prithu dāsa Adhikārī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Since your spent your life up to the very last moments preaching, you are most dear to Śrī Kṛṣṇa—*na ca tasmān manuṣyeṣu kaścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ*. *Yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa—*that was your life.

You gave everything you had to accomplish Kṛṣṇa’s mission, since you carried Him wherever you went in your beautiful heart:

*sādhavo hṛdayaṁ mahyaṁ sādhūnāṁ hṛdayaṁ tv aham*  
*mad-anyat te na jānanti nāhaṁ tebhyo manāg api*

**“The devotees are always in My heart, and I am always in the hearts of the devotees. The devotee does not know anything beyond Me, and I also cannot forget the devotee. There is a very intimate relationship between Me and the pure devotees. Pure devotees in full knowledge are never out of spiritual touch, and therefore they are very much dear to Me.” \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **9.4.68\]**

I feel wasted and useless seeing your great example, barely able to walk in your footsteps. However, as a great touchstone turns iron to gold, please kindly touch my lowly heart and cause just one more miracle, the way you miraculously reformed so many of my Godbrothers and -sisters into pure devotees of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya.

I have no personal qualifications and have no good qualities. Kindly pick up poor Prithu dāsa and drag him home, to His lotus feet.

Unless you pray for me, what hope have I?

Your servant,  
Prithu dāsa Adhikārī

## Rādhānātha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

From the sacred pages of Śrī *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* we have read the pastime of Lord Caitanya cleansing the Guṇḍicā Temple. In your sacred commentary you have quoted the explanation given by your beloved Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. We must thoroughly and sincerely cleanse our hearts of all exploitative desires through the process of hearing and chanting the names and glories of the Lord. In the association of dedicated devotees this divine process will prepare the heart to be a suitable place for the Lord to reside.

My heart is like a dark pit filled with numerous varieties of snakes and scorpions. Your causeless mercy is incomprehensible, for you have taken residence in this heart. From Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma you have descended to release from captivity the hearts of the poor, fallen souls imprisoned by the chains of Kali. Who could understand the fathomless ocean of your compassion, save and except one who is a recipient of your love? I pray that the ultimate aspiration of my life will be to please you. In sincerely reciprocating with your love there is hope even for a sinner like myself. Wholeheartedly serving your desire makes the most confidential mysteries of Goloka-Vraja accessible even to the most fallen. You have perfectly presented the conclusions of the great *ācāryas* of our Gauḍīya-sampradāya and garlanded the whole world with the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

I am desperately unqualified and utterly insignificant, yet you have ordered me to preach by representing your words. I can only pray to be the humble servant of the servant of your servants. I pray that the great Vaiṣṇavas, including my Godbrothers and Godsisters, may accept me as their servant by keeping me as the dust at their lotus feet. I pray that those under my care may accept me as their servant by allowing me to assist them in serving you. I am helplessly praying for your mercy, for it can fulfill all spiritual aspirations.

This year we are again confronted with challenges that appear insurmountable. I have come to believe that our only hope will be to sincerely strive to purify our hearts through chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa in a spirit of humble servitude. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are my life and soul. Please shower your mercy upon me.

Your servant,  
Rādhānātha Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories unto you.

*yadā mukundo bhagavān imāṁ mahīṁ*  
*jahau sva-tanvā śravaṇīya-sat-kathaḥ*  
*tadāhar evāpratibuddha-cetasām*  
*abhadra-hetuḥ kalir anvavartata*

**“When the Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, left this earthly planet in His selfsame form, from that very day Kali, who had already partially appeared, became fully manifest to create inauspicious conditions for those who are endowed with a poor fund of knowledge.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are constantly praying for your mercy, guidance, and protection. Kali-yuga is moving swiftly, and Kali is wasting no time implementing his nefarious arrangements. Only if we seek complete shelter at your lotus feet can we feel immune from his ever-increasing attacks. Kali is performing his service very well, and we have even seen formerly stalwart devotees become his victims. His web is far-reaching and has been crafted in both gross and subtle ways. It is extremely difficult for anyone to escape. What can anyone do except totally and continuously surrender unto you?

We are indeed very fortunate that you so kindly came to this world to save us fallen souls. Except for you, who else could be our savior? Our formerly spiritual qualities had long been contaminated and corrupted by the three modes of material nature, especially the modes of passion and ignorance. Now we are trying to follow all your instructions, no matter how small. You once said that this was the sign of sincerity. But sometimes it is not so easy. We have been brought up in very materialistic cultures and have many bad habits from our past. They are like witches and ghosts that continually come back to haunt us. However, even these condemned creatures are purified and liberated by just a moment of your merciful association.

On a morning walk a disciple said that many people say our principles are too difficult to follow. You stopped and asked, “What is that difficulty?”

He said, “Like getting up early in the morning.”

You replied, “No, that is good for you, what is that? Early to bed, early to rise, makes a man healthy, wealthy, and wise.”

Of course, on another occasion a reporter said, “Your Grace, it seems that your movement is growing very rapidly.”

You cut him off and replied, “Yes it is growing, but only slowly because we have principles. People nowadays do not want to follow any principles.”

So on one hand it appears that Kṛṣṇa conscious principles are good and easy to follow, and on the other they are difficult. How do we understand this apparent contradiction? In my opinion the answer lies in strictly following the predecessor *ācāryas’* instructions and example. It is especially important to follow one’s own guru. If we deviate even slightly, then the easiest things become difficult, but if we remain fixed, then the most difficult things become easy. This is the secret of success for any sincere and serious disciple. As you often told us, “Don’t try to become more than your guru; just try to be humble and serve him. In this way you will please your guru, and simultaneously the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, will also become pleased.” My hope, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that somehow I can please you, because I consider that to be the perfection of my life.

Your insignificant servant,  
Rāmāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. You are the giver of ever-increasing pleasure to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, as you continue to bring back many fallen souls to Their feet.

You remain—as ever—our dear master, and we your pupils, now and always. It is true that your school is severe, your rule exacting, your proving of us relentless. You subject us to newer and newer challenges. You test us and then test us again, harder; in this way we receive your mercy.

This school of yours is more than natural. When we become eager and lively students, seeking your instruction ceaselessly, we can see at work how fine, detailed and painstaking is the discipline you use, acting with clockwork precision on the largest as on the tiniest scale. We discover that no moment passes without your test. And each day comes packed with new challenges to our hard-won skills received through your merciful art.

Please bless us, dear master, that we may master the art of surrender that you teach us, the art of receiving your gifts of love and of giving those gifts to the love-bereft world.

You have written:

By the pleasure of the spiritual master, one can get extraordinary power, especially in spiritual advancement. The blessings of the spiritual master are more powerful than one’s personal endeavor for such advancement....Especially for spiritual advancement, one should carry out the bona fide order of the spiritual master. By the *paramparā* system, one can thus be endowed with the original spiritual power coming from the Supreme Personality of Godhead.... (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.15.28, purport)

Therefore, we cry for your blessings so that we may do your will and the will of Śrī-Śrī Gaura-Nitāi now and in the world to come.

I beg to remain,  
Your eternal student,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of all fallen souls, whose mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances. All glories unto Your Divine Grace!

Your divine appearance day celebration is such a joy-filled event! All varieties of nationality, culture, language, service, personality, and all other categories of externals that make up your loving followers’ constituency become united in a grand symphony of single-minded glorification of you, on this day especially. Experiencing the diversities of love and the intensity of love that your numberless devotees are carrying in their hearts for you is so transforming and uplifting!

One of the most striking self-evident manifestations of your greatness is how your mere presence in your disciples’ lives uplifted them completely to the spiritual platform. Anyone who has accepted responsibility for the spiritual well-being of others, in relationships ranging from a parent to temple president, or teacher, or a dear friend, certainly knows what a challenge one encounters in providing for another’s spiritual well-being. The task is awesome!

Very quickly one learns that providing opportunity for spiritual well-being for others is challenged by a host of other requirements, such as social needs, physical needs, and psychological needs. With notable exceptions, most conditioned souls look to these needs’ fulfillment as a fundamental function of their lives; without the support of inner spiritual strength, in the absence of the maturity of one’s faith in guru and Kṛṣṇa, these needs will easily consume one’s whole energy. Discrepancies and unfulfilled expectations in these areas of life create formidable, if not insurmountable, obstacles on the path of spiritual well-being.

Those of us who are trying to act as your faithful servants, carrying out your mission according to our personal capacity, vividly know from our direct experience how unending and how complex is the service before us. How did you do it, Śrīla Prabhupāda?! How did you uplift thousands of souls to the spiritual platform, just by your sound vibrations? Souls were sent to you by Kṛṣṇa from all walks of life, from all backgrounds of education, piety and impiety, all cultures and all value systems. You provided us all with the absolute confidence that a life of pure *bhakti* can carry us to the other side of the ocean of material existence. You gave us hope and love. How did you do all this, with such a wide array of followers?

There is only one plausible answer: Kṛṣṇa’s personally empowering you made all this possible: *kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*.

You instructed us by *pracāra* and by *ācāra*, that “Preaching is the essence. Purity is the force.” As strong as was your call for preaching, we were equally guided in the ways of purity:

Our unique asset is our purity. No one anywhere can match it. That will be noticed eventually and appreciated, as long as we do not diminish or neglect the highest standard of purity in performing our routine work. Our pure standard is enough. Let us stand on that basis. \[Letter to Yogeśvara dāsa, 28 December 1971\]

Even if we strayed or wavered from the path, your purity and your powerful instructions brought us back to the proper focus, both in our goals and our methods.

When we place our faith in carrying out your preaching mission with genuine purity, taking care as you did, with great compassion toward each soul sincerely seeking shelter at your lotus feet, in this way expanding the network of the same loving relationship by which you captured our hearts, then inconceivable results are naturally ordained to follow.

*‘śraddhā’-śabde—viśvāsa kahe sudṛḍha niścaya*  
*kṛṣṇe bhakti kaile sarva-karma kṛta haya*

You awakened faith in us—faith in preaching with purity of heart. This faith carries us beyond dualities and beyond our personal frailties. This faith gives us strength and courage and resourcefulness to carry out our endless service to you. The absence of such faith steals away all enthusiasm, shrouding even the best of souls in a cover of gloom and despair.

We place our full faith in you and in your teachings. We place our full faith in your capacity to empower your sincere followers to come to the standard of pure devotion you desired for each of us. We eagerly embrace those activities that are supportive of pure devotion, knowing all of these to be part of your primary gifts of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*.

Kindly help us overcome each of the numberless obstacles that face us as we attempt to build a strong spiritual foundation for all the infrastructure-building work that awaits us. We want to render this service to you, along with all of our duties, in a mood of complete gratitude and love.

We hold your lotus feet tightly within our hearts. Your satisfaction is all we live for.

Your aspiring servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Prabhupāda

my teacher, my  
spiritual father

a newcomer spoke of  
me, “Your lingering  
love for he who is  
unarguably the most important  
person in your life.”

You have left.  
But your vani is present.  
You always said that  
was most important.

Prabhupāda’s teachings.

Waa! Waa! Cry  
and thump.  
Look at me.  
Give me some attention  
and love. Throw me a cracker.  
a cookie, I am the  
quacking duck.

Prabhupāda, do you know what’s  
happening? Of course you do.  
Why doesn’t Krsna eliminate our  
enemies or our wrongs?

You answer, “He will, He is,  
it is happening.”

Will I be able to write  
another homage, honestly?  
We do it each year.

We still have sweets,  
they say. We still have good times.  
Maybe bad times  
too, some infamy.

Anyway, I follow you.  
You are the eternal resident of Vṛndāvana.  
We wish to serve you  
in an original  
form—approved  
and revealed  
by you.

I don’t know what’s  
to come, so I must rely  
on you. I stake my  
life and death on that.  
Me and all your followers  
down until the end.

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Śeṣa dāsa Adhikārī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept my humble obeisances. I submit the following poem for your glorification and pleasure.

**Yoga**

Yoga lessons now at fifty  
Physical exercise, and more  
Obeisance, your lotus feet.

Yoga practice, learn to breathe  
Perception clearing, but unchanged  
Obeisance, your lotus feet.

Yoga *āsana,* performance peak  
Tension free, best applied  
Obeisance, your lotus feet.

“Yoga Journal,” took a peek  
Whew, It’s a jungle out there!  
Obeisance, your lotus feet.

Śeṣa dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Today is your Vyāsa-pūjā day, but for me, every minute, of every hour, of every day is an attempt to offer similar expression of love to you. In fulfilling your orders to be Kṛṣṇa conscious and give the same to others, I am constantly meditating on you by way of your *vāṇī*. It is only such service that mitigates the pain of separation from your *vapu*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the tenure of my service I have seen many Vaiṣṇavas, had the opportunity to meet some, and even heard discourses from a few. There is no one like you! No one!

No Vaiṣṇava equals your learning, your kindness, your humor, your empowerment, or your devotion to Kṛṣṇa and guru.

Last night, as I was returning from the temple accompanied by some devotees, I looked up at the clear night sky of New Vraja Dhāma. It was an impressive panorama of twinkling lights representing the external potency. I stopped and looked, absorbed in thoughts of you. Then I said aloud, “In recent memory I have not been to any of the innumerable stars, planets, or galaxies up there. But I am sure that of all the devotees who are wandering in all those places for the welfare of others, there is none to match Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

As soon as I said that, I heard a wonderful voice resonate within my heart, “There is none to match Śrīla Prabhupāda!” Simultaneously, all the devotees responded at once, “No one equals Śrīla Prabhupāda!” Here was Kṛṣṇa and the Vaiṣṇavas confirming my proposition.

Retiring to my room, I opened the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and began to read Sanātana-śikṣā. In *Madhya-līlā,* Chapter 20, verse 61, I saw the following verse and took it as conclusive confirmation:

*akṣṇoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-darśanaṁ hi*  
*tanoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-gātra-saṅgaḥ*  
*jihvā-phalaṁ tvādṛśa-kīrtanaṁ hi*  
*su-durlabhā bhāgavatā hi loke*

**“O devotee of the Lord, the purpose of the visual sense is fulfilled simply by seeing you, and to touch your body is the fulfillment of bodily touch. The tongue is meant for glorifying your qualities because in this world a pure devotee of the Lord is very difficult to find.” (Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, a devotee like you is very, very difficult to find. That I had the good fortune to see you, to touch you, to hear from you, and to share your mission seems inexplicable. The only reasoning I can consider is yours: “I have created your good fortune!”

As I continue to wander here and there in your service, I know that I will meet none to match your shadow. I simply pray that I will develop the good sense and spiritual insight to appreciate the uniqueness of Your Divine Grace, as it continues to expand before my eyes and within my heart.

Your lowly servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Suhotra Swami

**Amazing**

“Amazing” seems not word enough  
To sketch all that you do,  
But since “amazing” sings the *Gītā* of  
The pure soul, so do I of you.

All the more that you are Kṛṣṇa’s tool  
To save us from our plight—  
You lift us, drowners, from the sucking pool  
And lead us to His side.

Amazing is Your Divine Grace  
That keeps us in its ken,  
Even though we from your shelter race  
To dive in *māyā* again.

Amazing is your loving heart  
In which Kṛṣṇa, the blackish sun,  
Shines forth, all darkness to depart,  
Revealing the true sense of One:

Not One without devotion,  
But One in tender flow  
Of purified emotion  
That only *premānandīs* know.

Amazing are your books sublime  
That spring forth from your love,  
Exposing there’s no reason or rhyme  
To materialistic *bhāv*.

Amazing is the holy name  
You sowed everywhere as did Gaura,  
Immersing the world in Kṛṣṇa’s fame  
And opening up the door:

From *saṁsāra’s* woes to the Lord’s abode  
Where you welcome us in glory—  
Amazing, Srila Prabhupada, O golden lode,  
From where I mine this story!

Your servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Gurus and Crackpots, All Have Their Place or Who Speaks for Prabhupāda?**

During Vyāsa-pūjā devotees set aside their differences for that singular activity upon which all can agree: glorifying Śrīla Prabhupāda. As the offerings are read we appreciate not only Prabhupāda’s multi-faceted personality and accomplishments but one another as well. Our diversity contributes to and reveals Prabhupāda’s greatness. He is the one person who could unite us all and help us understand our strength lay in our differences.

Recognizing these differences, Prabhupāda said that our love for him would be tested by our ability to cooperate. Have we succeeded? I cannot claim to have. Considering Prabhupāda’s high expectations, my vagaries of thought and action are an embarrassment. So too my self-righteousness. I have pontificated and judged on matters and persons too numerous to mention. I beg the forgiveness of all I disappointed or offended, including Śrīla Prabhupāda.

If others share this sense of failure, then perhaps we should ask why Prabhupāda appealed for cooperation. Was it a prophetic warning of potential dissolution? Or just friendly advice, freighted with no doom or destiny attached? If the latter, then what about his other words, and his teachings in general? Are they all still relevant? And if so, how much weight are they each to be given? There is no intended disrespect here. Anyone aware of ISKCON’s problems may wonder. Our movement is challenged by a plethora of issues few of us could have imagined during Prabhupāda’s physical presence. We are forced to consider the rights of women, the education of children, the sanctity of marriage, levels of commitment, institutionalization, the role of the guru, and a host of other topics.

In the past, Prabhupāda’s availability made comprehension easy; in fact, his mere presence banished most doubts. Today it is not so simple. Though we have nearly every word he spoke, and though he spoke on nearly every subject, we do not seem to be able to agree precisely on what he meant. The fact that we continue to seek solutions by consulting Prabhupāda’s teachings indicates that we believe they are relevant. That he himself said (in my presence) that they would continue to be the law books for the next ten thousand years is as strong a warrant as we could wish. Yet we remain divided as to their meaning. Perhaps, therefore, the question should be, How are Prabhupāda’s teachings best understood? Or, stated another way, Who speaks for Prabhupāda?

In a surprising statement, Prabhupāda indicated that his purports were more important than their texts. While we would not say that the explanations to his purports are more important still, their present meanings are hotly debated. Though clarification based on the authority of guru, *sādhu,* and *śāstra* is held in the highest esteem, the speaker’s own credentials are also a consideration, as are the audience, time, and place. Nor can it be assumed that these are localized; retransmission of any message can considerably widen the audience, time, and place, entirely reshaping meanings. Add to this all the social, cultural, and historical influences that affect us whether or not we are conscious of them, both from within our tradition and without, and we are face to face with our original problem of authority: Who speaks for Prabhupāda?

No two explanations are ever identical. When Prabhupāda’s statements are filtered through ever-changing circumstances, their meanings are inevitably nuanced. Is it possible, then, to understand what Prabhupāda’s statements mean? It is, but comprehension, just as our devotion for Kṛṣṇa, will always be unique and evolving. Though the seeming loss of certainty caused by competing interpretations may not be reassuring, there may be no escaping the dilemma. Rather than ignore, condemn, or banish alternative explanations, post-Prabhupāda reality dictates that the truth may be a many-headed hydra able to generate heads as quickly as those unwanted are lopped off. We are not Kṛṣṇa, nor are we Prabhupāda; if we judge too severely, among the decapitated heads may be our own. Unity in diversity is not an empty slogan but the constitutional nature of a society of the One seeking to avoid becoming a society of none.

A story with a parallel meaning recently came my way (I apologize for not knowing its original source):

A water bearer in India had two large pots. Each hung on the end of a pole that he carried across his neck. While one pot was perfect and always delivered a full portion of water at the end of the long walk from the stream to the master’s house, the other had a crack in it and arrived only half full.

For a full two years this went on daily, with the bearer delivering only one and a half pots full of water to his master’s house. Of course, the perfect pot was proud of its accomplishments, perfect to the end for which it was made. But the poor cracked pot was ashamed of its own imperfection, and miserable that it was able to accomplish only half of what it had been made to do. After two years of what it perceived to be a bitter failure, it spoke to the water bearer one day by the stream: “I am ashamed of myself, and I want to apologize to you.”

“Why?” asked the bearer. “What are you ashamed of?”

“I have been able, for these past two years, to deliver only half my load because this crack in my side causes water to leak out all the way back to your master’s house. Because of my flaws, you have to do all of this work and you don’t get full value from your efforts,” the pot said.

The water bearer felt sorry for the old cracked pot, and in his compassion he said, “As we return to the master’s house, I want you to notice the beautiful flowers along the path.” Indeed, as they went up the hill, the old cracked pot took notice of the sun warming the beautiful wild flowers on the side of the path, and this cheered it some. But at the end of the trail, it still felt bad because it had leaked out half its load, and so again it apologized to the bearer for its failure. The bearer said to the pot, “Did you notice that there were flowers only on your side of the path, but not on the other pot’s side? That’s because I have always known about your flaw, and I took advantage of it. I planted flower seeds on your side of the path, and every day while we walk back from the stream, you’ve watered them. For two years I have been able to pick these beautiful flowers to decorate my master’s table. Without your being just the way you are, he would not have this beauty to grace his house.”

The moral of this story: For our Vyāsa-pūjā glorification to please Prabhupāda, each of our voices should be appreciated and included, no matter its seeming insignificance or faults. Let our voices, therefore, in melodious multiplicities rejoice: *Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda!

Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet, those feet which are the shelter of countless fallen souls in this miserable material world, those feet which walked the earth and established the *yuga-dharma* worldwide.

As the years go on, more and more fallen souls are getting the great opportunity to hear the holy names, receive one of your books, get *prasādam,* and offer service in some way or other. Your movement is becoming more and more widely accepted by people from all walks of life. The more I see this and experience meeting more and more people who are now accepting Kṛṣṇa consciousness, in one way or other, the more enthusiastic and encouraged I feel, and the more I want to see this great movement spread everywhere.

I had the nice experience of going to Kazakhstan this year. Very encouraging indeed! His Holiness Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Mahārāja is working very hard and making many devotees and extending the congregation very quickly. I had the opportunity of giving some lectures on *gaura-līlā* there, and thought to ask the question, “How many of you sitting here now have come from Muslim backgrounds?” To my great pleasure and surprise, over 50% of the attendees put their hands up. All glories to the Śrī Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana* movement, which has spread throughout the world! All glories to BB Govinda Mahārāja, who is receiving your mercy and giving it also. The work there is going on nicely. A farm has been started and is growing nicely. The cows are being looked after, the Deities are being worshiped devotionally, and the devotees and guests are very enthusiastic on *harināma saṅkīrtana*. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you spread the rays of the benediction moon everywhere! Thank you for giving me the pleasure of being involved in such preaching, and thank you for the association of that enthusiastic soul called BB Govinda Swami.

Another one of your dear sons is working very hard to please you in Hungary. His holiness Śivarāma Swami is preaching up a storm there and spreading your mission throughout that land so quickly and positively. All your books have been translated into the Hungarian language and are being distributed throughout the country in quantity. Preaching centers have been established throughout the country, and big new temples are planned for the coming years. The congregation members are enthusiastic and are giving funds regularly for the maintenance of the existing projects and for the planned new buildings. A big temple has been planned for the center of Budapest. The plans are wonderful: this temple will be the only one of its kind in the Western world, especially in a major city. This will be a temple that the whole world will see and thereby recognize the greatness of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, its founder-*ācārya*.

You are obviously pouring your mercy upon Śivarāma Mahārāja, and he is taking that mercy deep into his heart. Please keep pouring your mercy upon him and the Hungarian devotees and people in general; things are really happening there.

Thank you for the association of that wonderful soul called Śivarāma Swami, who is a great example of a very hard-working and dedicated disciple.

Mahādyuti Prabhu is sticking it out in London, and things are coming together there. The financial situation there is the best we can remember in the history of the temple. Book distribution has increased greatly over the past few years under the dedicated direction of Rādhā Ramaṇa Prabhu. Mahādyuti Prabhu is a good example of the philosophy of “Just stick it out in the one place and remain dedicated and everything will work.” Of course, he is trying to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and that is why things are working, even in a very difficult situation like London. Please keep pouring your mercy upon Mahādyuti Prabhu and the servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara, and give them the ability to accept your mercy and act upon it fully. The London temple is one of the most important preaching centers in the world, and it is never easy for anyone to live in that situation for any length of time. Please give them your special mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for their great example of determination and patience; it is great to see.

Thank you for the association of other great souls who have enthused me over the years. Some of them are as follows: Bhakti Vidyā Pūrṇa Swami, who has always encouraged me in service and has a great fund of knowledge. Tamal Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja, whom I had the pleasure of being with for a few days last year. He enthused me very much by telling me many of your pastimes and direct instructions. Kāliyaphaṇi Prabhu, Janānanda Prabhu, Jaya Śilā Prabhu, and Rāma dāsa Prabhu, whom I met just before he left his body. That association was sweet, just sitting for a few hours with him sharing the nectar of Deity worship and remembering the different mercy programs that you have given us all. It was wonderful to just sit with Rāma dāsa Prabhu and just appreciate your mercy, your movement, and your devotees. Indradyumna Mahārāja. He is Mr. Festivals, always enthusiastic to preach and share Kṛṣṇa consciousness with others, always ready to give the Holy Name to others. Gopāl Krishna Mahārāja. I got his association for a short time as he was rushing here and there at full speed as usual, working hard to try to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Bhakti Caitanya Mahārāja. He was taking groups of devotees round Vraja, showing them the Lord’s pastime places and narrating those sweet pastimes—the places and pastimes you revealed to the world Śrīla Prabhupāda. Dīna Bandhu Prabhu. Always happy when I see him, always telling stories about Kṛṣṇa and his devotees, always revealing the nectar of Śrī Vraja-dhāma to the devotees, always catching malaria. But it has never stopped him from continuing to stay in Vraja and India in general. This son of yours has a real taste for the nectar of *kṛṣṇa-kathā* and *seva*. He is receiving your mercy and giving it to others.

Thank you for the many other souls of yours whose association I have been greatly fortunate to receive; they are the great gems in the transcendental treasury of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. So much mercy do you have, so much mercy have you given. My prayer is that I will take your mercy also and become one of the bright shining little gems in the Bhaktivedanta Swami transcendental treasury. You are the transcendental touchstone who has built a great storehouse of transcendental gems that have become lovers and servants of the Lord and his devotees. The great process you have given us has changed so many souls into gems of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that are shining brightly in the darkness of Kali-yuga. What need is there for anything else. I just have to accept what you have given and perfection will be there. It is for all!!

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I beg forgiveness for my offenses, stupidity, and laziness.

Your insignificant servant, the worthless  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I am writing this year’s offering from your new village community in Indonesia, which we have named Gītā Nāgarī Baru (New Gītā Nāgarī). You will be pleased to know that a group of dedicated devotees here on the island of Sumatra are trying their level best to make one of your dreams come true, that of establishing self-sufficient rural communities.

By your kind mercy and inspiration, we now have thirty-five devotees living here at Gītā Nāgarī Baru. Already eight families have settled here, building their homes in a very simple and modest manner with no electricity and the minimum of facilities. We have altogether eight wells on the property, eight homes built by the devotees themselves, and five other buildings.

For your pleasure, we are now very busy finishing the construction of our new temple. Our plan is to hold the official opening of the temple on Gaura Pūrṇimā itself. Some devotees are now in Vṛndāvana doing the last-minute preparations to bring our new Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Madana-Gopāla, Śrī Śrī Lalitā-Viśākhā and Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, who will be installed on Gaura Pūrṇimā itself. We have already installed Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi.

We have very much to learn, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have given us many things in your books. You have always been the ideal teacher by your own example of selfless surrender and service to the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The majority of our devotees are young couples. It is very inspiring to see them attend both *maṅgala-ārati* and *gaura-ārati* with their children. We now have fifteen children in our small community, and they are all so fortunate to hear and chant and dance every day. We have two cows, Gopī and Lakṣmī. Yesterday our bull Dharma left his body. He had been sick for the last few months. In his last few days he was hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* accepting *caraṇāmṛta,* and receiving a lot of personal care from the devotees.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there are many things to say, but time is short. Please continue to bless and inspire us so that we can follow in your footsteps. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for this wonderful gift of devotional service you have given us. Please allow us to remain your servants eternally.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at you lotus feet.

It is over a hundred years since the day of your auspicious appearance in this world and now we are in the twenty-first century. There have been countless changes over these years, and practically none of them are significant since they are all meant to increase the so-called comfort of material life.

At the time of your birth you witnessed India under British rule. When you were a student, some of your professors were British missionaries who would test your faith in Vedic wisdom. *Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta* describes this as follows:

One of the professors criticized the Vedic teaching of *karma* and transmigration of the soul. In a court of law one cannot be persecuted for a crime unless there is a witness. Similarly, he argued, although according the Hindus the soul suffers in his present life for the misdeeds of his past life, where is the witness to the misdeeds? Abhay was displeased to hear this criticism and he knew how to refute it, but being only a student he had remained silent. (*A Lifetime in Preparation,* pp. 23–24)

After graduation you met your eternal spiritual master and then took initiation from him. You told us that everything you learnt from your guru had already been taught to you by you own father, except for one thing—book production. You eventually accepted the *sannyāsa* order of life and established your preaching mission on a worldwide scale.

In 1972, while visiting your London center, you accepted an invitation to visit a preaching center in Glasgow, Scotland. It required more strenuous traveling and the weather was miserable, but you wanted to reward your followers. At that time you also remembered your Scottish professors, who had given you their mundane knowledge. Now it was time for you to give them and their countrymen the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If someone gives charity to a pure devotee, it is returned hundreds of thousands of times. The value of the mundane education given by the Scottish professors was only a few rupees, but you repaid them by giving the priceless jewel of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

At present there are still some Christian missionaries in India, but they are not as conspicuous as your disciples and grand-disciples, both from India and abroad (even from Scotland). The foreign devotees go there simply to take part in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servant,  
Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me to acquire the right attitude. Attitude is one of the most important elements of Kṛṣṇa consciousness because, with the right attitude, one can overcome any obstacle and develop any good quality. With the right attitude, one can step over any personal or interpersonal negativity. Small drops wear away the stone. If there are overwhelming obstacles, it does no good to become fearful, despondent, or cynical. If there are monumental personal goals to reach, it does no good to become faint-hearted or lethargic. Most of what we really face is the internal struggle for our own purification. As Prahlāda Mahārāja says in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, “There is no enemy but the mind.” Our real battle is within, yet it must be played out on the social, interpersonal level. Learning to be of service to others is very difficult for me, but I want to go forward. Honestly wanting the best for everyone else is an attitude that I don’t find natural to my character. But I think about that quality frequently. I want to have that quality.

In times like these, when there is a creeping cynicism and hopelessness in our Society, I find it is important just to keep firmly fixed on the idea that I really am trying to help others—though for many, the only way I can help them is to pray for them. Often I try to help, only to find that what I have done or said is ineffective or worse, counter-effective. I am baffled about what to do next. So I pray for others. I wait to see if Kṛṣṇa reveals anything else. Often there arise feelings of frustration and resentment. I am working to try to rid myself of these unwanted emotional aftereffects. Please help me to remember. I am fighting the tendency to feel pessimistic about the progress of others. I am fighting the tendency to feel embattled, the tendency to feel self-righteous and self-important. All these are wrong attitudes. There is also the tendency to just “Let it be,” which is also not exactly what you wanted for the devotees. We do have to maintain standards and uphold the true understandings of our Gauḍīya-Vaiṣṇava tradition. The wisdom to know how much to attempt to do, and when and where, is very crucial these days. Please grant me that wisdom. I don’t want to tilt at windmills or watch everything brown out in a gradual loss of all standards.

Please help me to balance my *sādhana* with my daily duties so that I can attend to important tasks and yet keep my chanting and reading in place. The attitude that I can make it without proper *sādhana* is a wrong attitude. The attitude that I have no important deadlines to meet or duties to perform is also a wrong attitude. Let me not fall into a brain-dead robot attitude in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Keep me always stay fresh with an attitude of trying to make today better than yesterday.

Most of all, please help me to make steady progress in preaching to devotees as well as outsiders. That is where there is true life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. So many obstacles may be there, but there is always hope. In preaching everything can be accomplished with steady determination and prayer. Please help me to take the Holy Name more deeply, not as a ritual but as a daily renewal, a daily audience with the Supreme Lord. With the Holy Name, all the purification I need will quickly manifest. If I really want to help others, here is where I should start.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Nitya-siddha* without a doubt,  
Kali’s influence meant to rout,  
Śrī Rādhārāṇī’s pride and joy,  
Gour Mohan’s precious little boy.

From double pram to *darśan prem,*  
Rādhā-Govinda’s beauty came.  
Jagannātha parade, the child made,  
Rolling cart, and the *kīrtana* played.

*Mṛdaṅga* lessons caref’ly took,  
Observed *mātā* and learned to cook,  
Traded *kācam* for *divya-ratna,*  
Left “Mahatma,” found “Siddhānta.”

Order taken upon the head:  
“Preach in English,” that’s what was said.  
Searching to find the place and time,  
Till years had passed—-numbr’ing sixty-nine.

*Jīva* dispatched wondrous mission,  
Swamiji’s great expedition.  
Heart attacks twice, if thrice, the end—  
Gopāl’s rescue, the dearmost friend.

Calm needle enters Boston port,  
Bearing Bal’rām’s plow, soon to sport.  
None knew who filled those rubber shoes—  
No one less than *jagat-guru.*

The Bow’ry is where he would teach,  
Extend his mercy to furthest reach,  
*Bhagavad-gītā*alive and well,  
’Mericans’ *bhakti* starts to swell.

*Samosās, laḍḍus* he cooked for all,  
And after the feast, cleaned the hall,  
Engaged youths in Kṛṣṇa’s service,  
Made some elders mighty nervous.

Around the world in eighty ways,  
Countless disciples to amaze,  
Writing in the still of the night,  
Making ev’ryone’s future bright.

No one else came out to save us.  
Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa no one gave us.  
My duty’s clear: I am his hound,  
Most happy he keeps me around.

Dānavīr Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet.

Almost two and a half decades have passed since your departure, and almost all of your disciples are passing into the sunset of this life’s last phase.

You caught most of us in our youth, and, looking back now, certainly we can see that our greenness and brashness led to procedural and psychological mistakes in executing your divine will.

Yet, the wonder of the *saṅkīrtana* sacrifice consumed us. Your brilliance in leading many of us to amazing levels of dedication and self-abnegation still mystifies us to this day. Somehow, in spite of ourselves, you swung wide open the doors of Vaikuṇṭha and stampeded us in. “For this effort you’ll go back home, back to Godhead,” was your magnificent dispensation, bestowed in exchange for our tossing youth and young adulthood into the fire of the supreme *yajña*.

Actually, only the most mature and wise should have assisted the founder-*ācārya* in blazing the trail of Hare Kṛṣṇa into the wilderness of Kali-yuga. Your mission to transplant *bhāgavata-dharma* from Indian soil to the jungles abroad required a staff equipped with the kind of depth and sagacity acquired by years of missionary and life experience. Yet, we were all novices. And we were all you had.

We cringe, reflecting back upon our youthful excesses and fanaticisms. Lust, envy, and impersonalism certainly pounded us, as we traded in what a materialist would consider the best years of life, just to fulfill your impossible goals.

Now we know better. Or do we? In our attempts to please you better, our sincere self-castigation should not curdle into a neurosis that overshadows the sun. What still shines on is the *acintya* Bhaktivedanta miracle. How did you get us to do all that we did? You kept raising the bar and we loved to jump higher and higher, eager for the mercy particles of dust decorating your lotus feet. Oh how we rejoice at the extraordinary opportunity for absorption and sacrifice that you made so easily available!

You once gave us a masterly lesson in transcendental management psychology. Pop motivational experts such as Covey and Peck do well at their level, but you soared far beyond them. Instructing that this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement should always remain a great challenge, you insisted that it is the job of the leaders to induce a spirit of sacrifice. Otherwise, you predicted, no one will become purified and everything will grind to a halt.

We’ve all heard it before, but how to apply it? We revere these brilliant axioms as much as we long for the spiritual abilities to wield them:

Krishna Consciousness Movement is for training men to be independently thoughtful and competent in all types of departments of knowledge and action, not for making bureaucracy. Once there is bureaucracy the whole thing will be spoiled. There must be always individual striving and work and responsibility, competitive spirit, not that one shall dominate and distribute benefits to the others and they do nothing but beg from you and you provide. No.

*Bhakti-yoga* is a process. Are we cooking with fire—or wasting time cultivating smoke? Significantly, even in the boom year of 1972, your sharp eye uncovered decline:

I am little observing now, especially in your country \[the USA\], that our men are losing their enthusiasm for spreading on our programmes of Krishna Consciousness movement. Otherwise, why so many letters of problems are coming, dissatisfied? That is not a very good sign. The whole problem is they are not following the regulative principles, that I can detect. Without this, enthusiasm will be lacking.

Interestingly, you then proceed to cite the value of even mechanical devotional service—often minimized today. Currently, some demand, “Give me *rāga* now, or bust! If it can’t be spontaneous *bhakti,* then forget it.” But you obviously had solid faith in your basic program of how to rise gradually and surely, from the ground level up.

Even mechanically following, and if he gets gradually understanding from the class, he will come to the point of spontaneous enthusiasm. This spontaneous loving devotional service is not so easy matter, but if one simply sticks strictly to the rules and regulations, like rising early, chanting 16 rounds, chanting gayatri, keeping always clean—then his enthusiasm will grow more and more, and if there is also patience and determination, one day he will come to the platform of spontaneous devotion, then his life will be perfect. All of this I have told you in *Nectar of Devotion*. So I do not think the leaders are themselves following, nor they are seeing the others are following strictly. That must be rectified at once.

Next you gave us a warning, and a subtle appraisal of the leadership at that time.

Our leaders shall be careful not to kill the spirit of enthusiastic service, which is individual and spontaneous and voluntary. They should try always to generate some atmosphere of fresh challenge to the devotees, so that they will agree enthusiastically to rise and meet it. That is the art of management: to draw out spontaneous loving spirit of sacrificing some energy for Krishna. But where are so many expert managers?

Concluding with a clarion call, you exhorted:

All of us should become expert managers and preachers. We should not be very much after comforts and become complacent or self-contented. There must be always some *tapasya,* strictly observing the regulative principles—Krishna Consciousness movement must be always a challenge, a great achievement to be gained by voluntary desire to do it, and that will keep it healthy. So you big managers now try to train up more and more some competent preachers and managers like yourselves.

It may be that here the road out of stagnation is clearly laid by you. How to create a sustainable devotional economy of spiritual growth, where many devotees are eager to invest “venture capital,” that is, the energies of at least part of their lives? We’ve seen the excesses of periods characterized by exuberant, reckless development. And now we have seen the pendulum swing the other way, to the excesses of periods dominated by stagflation, negativity, fratricide, and other symptoms of sacrificial inactivity in the spiritual marketplace. “Nothing ventured, nothing gained.”

As a notably inept servant, who am I to say anything? Pressing my nose at your holy feet, I beg for some intelligence, expertise, forbearance, and compassion,

Struggling to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Gaṇapati dasa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you are the manifested representation of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu, please allow me to direct Lord Caitanya’s personal glorification of Lord Nityānanda toward Your Divine Grace:

You are really *nityānanda,* eternal bliss. You are the reservoir of all the Vaiṣṇavas. All ornaments decorating you are surely incarnations of *prema-bhakti*. Your causeless mercy will liberate all wretched sinners. Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is glorified by the *Vedas,* is your property. You can sell Him to anyone. Who has the power to understand your glories? You are ecstatic Kṛṣṇa-prema personified. When you do *nāma-saṅkīrtana*, you lose yourself in rapture. You glorify Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental qualities day and night. Kṛṣṇacandra always resides in your heart. Your body is the abode of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. If anyone offers love and respect to you, Kṛṣṇa never ever rejects that person. (*Śrī Nityānanda Caritāmṛta*)

You are a *paramahaṁsa ṭhākura,* Lord Caitanya’s empowered agent for reclaiming all fallen souls in this Age of Kali. You are replete with all twenty-six qualities of a Vaiṣṇava. You are well aware that I had and still have no good qualities, but if I can at least remember my fallen condition, there is a chance that I can be delivered by your causeless mercy, for you have personally avowed that there is no limit to the mercy of Lord Nityānanda. This is my only hope.

Your Guru Mahārāja has nicely articulated this principle of hope against hope thusly:

The root of it all is humility or due realization of one’s ineligibility. If the conviction of our unfitness for devotional service is revealed to us either automatically or by the instructions of others, we are then in a position to appreciate the beauty of the lotus feet of God’s devotees. (*A Ray of Viṣṇu*)

Śrīla Kṛṣṇadasa Kavirāja Gosvāmī compared the mercy of Lord Caitanya to a river that flows downstream. In your purport, you comment:

The more fallen one is, the greater one’s claim to the favor of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. One must only be very sincere and serious. Despite being contaminated by all the bad qualities of this Kali-yuga, if one surrenders unto the lotus feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the Lord will surely and certainly deliver him. The best example is Jagāi and Mādhāi. In this Age of Kali practically everyone is like Jagāi and Mādhāi, but the *saṅkīrtana* movement inaugurated by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is still flowing like a great river, inundating the entire world, and thus the International Society for Krishna Consciousness is successfully claiming all fallen souls to free them from contamination. (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta Ādi* 16.1)
