How fortunate I am to be one of those fallen souls of Kali-yuga born at just the right instant! How fortunate I am to have come within the swath of your reclamation harvest! But O, how unfortunate I am that I do not always appreciate your benevolence. In my obstinacy, I even sometimes “bite the hand” that tries to nourish me. But you have so liberally pledged, “This is the principle. If you offer your respectful obeisances to Guru, he becomes pleased. Anyone, even one is offender, if he comes and offers obeisances to the superior guru, then even there was offense, he forgets.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture)

What else must I do besides acknowledging my ineligibility, offering you homage, and begging for your forgiveness? I can prove my resolution by humbly assisting you in your service to Lord Caitanya and your Guru Mahārāja. You have explained,

The members of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness cannot even call themselves *brahma-bandhus*. Therefore our only means for satisfying Kṛṣṇa is to pursue the injunctions of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who says:

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

**“Whomever you meet, instruct him on the teachings of Kṛṣṇa. In this way, on My order, become a spiritual master and deliver the people of this country.”** ***(Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*****7.128)**

Simply trying to follow the orders of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, we speak to the people of the world about *Bhagavad-gītā* As It Is. This will make us qualified to satisfy the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. *\[Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi* 17.78\]

Please give me the strength and enthusiasm to execute this order successfully. By your grace, you have endowed me with some small realization of the supreme shelter of *kṛṣṇa-kathā* in the association of devotees. Now let me share that shelter with all those I come in contact with on my journey out of this material world.

Begging for your mercy,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on this most auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in this world.

Before you came to us in the 60’s, Śrīla Prabhupāda, “Peace and Love” were our by-words and our goal, but although we sang about peace and love all the time, nobody really knew what those words meant. We thought something “peace and love” should be something like no war, no hassles, and lots of “higher pleasures” and ephemeral joy. In spite of all the external fanfare, though, the reality was that we were completely miserable and confused inside. We were finding neither peace nor love.

Then you landed on the scene, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and started singing *your* song of peace and love: You chanted *nitāi pada-kamala, koṭī-candra suśītala:*

If we take shelter of Nityānanda Prabhu, then we get peace.... Just like in daytime, especially in the summer season, we become very much exasperated. But at night, in the evening, as soon as there is moonshine we become very much satisfied.... The whole world is suffering, but if you want peace, then you take the shelter of Nityānanda Prabhu. \[Lecture, Bhubaneswar, 2 February 1977\]

You not only talked of that peace attained through Lord Nityānanda’s shelter, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but you personally emanated it. And you lovingly showed us by your *saṅkīrtana* of the holy names how we could also attain it for ourselves. At last the real message of peace and love had arrived! It was the relief of lifetimes! It was not at all what we had expected, but it was certainly what we wanted. And you gave it to us freely, pouring it into our hearts, thus creating within us a genuine taste for loving and serving Kṛṣṇa. It took you “gallons of blood,” as you said, to do so, But just as Lord Nityānanda shed blood to save Jagāi and Mādhāi, you were prepared to do the same for us.

For the twelve years that you were with us, you continued to fully immerse yourself in that transcendental loving mood of Lord Nityānanda. And because you wanted us to dive deeply into that mood as well, you never tired of preaching it:

By following His footsteps, you can approach Nityananda Prabhu. Nityananda Prabhu approached Jagai and Madhai at the risk of being personally injured and still He definitely delivered them. The world is full of Jagais and Madhais; namely drunkards, women-hunters, meat eaters and gamblers, and we will have to approach them at the risk of insult, injury and similar other rewards. To face such reverse conditions of life and to suffer thereby the results of actions is considered as the greatest penance and austerity in the matter of spiritual advancement of life. \[Letter to Gajendra dāsa, 27 January 1970\]

So this is Nityānanda. Nityānanda-vaṁśa means one who takes the risk of preaching work, he can claim Nityānanda-vaṁśa. Not that easy-going. The preaching work is not easy-going. There are so many difficulties. \[Lecture, Vṛndāvana, 16 October 1972\]

You have to take this botheration. On this principle, at the age of seventy years, I took all the botheration—“All right.” The other friends were thinking that “This man is going to die, and he is going to preach.” \[laughing\] They said like that. And “All right. I shall die, I shall die for Kṛṣṇa’s cause. \[Conversation, Bhubaneswar, 23 January 1977\]

If I would not have lectured, how you would have come? So as far as possible I am taking care of my health, but Krishna’s service must be executed, even at the risk of life. A living entity gets millions of opportunities to get a type of body, but hardly he gets opportunity to serve Krishna. The service of Krishna must be executed at all risks. \[Letter to Madhusūdana dasa, 1968\]

So when you go to preach, you know the preachers are sometimes attacked. Just like Nityānanda Prabhu was attacked by Jagai-Madhai.... Therefore Kṛṣṇa says, “These field workers who are engaged in preaching this *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is, they are very, very dear to Me....Therefore if we want to please Kṛṣṇa, we have to take this risk. Kṛṣṇa, guru. My spiritual master took this risk, preaching work, and he inspired us also to do that preaching work. And we are also imploring you to take this preaching work. \[Lecture, New Vrindavan, 24 May 1969\]

You are imploring us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. How can we ever refuse you after all you have sacrificed for us? The power of your love has made it impossible to refuse you. And that power forces me to keep pushing on in spite of all the pains and allurements that come. No one else could have performed such a miracle on this sinful soul. I am sure that is why Kṛṣṇa so mercifully sent you, for as Lord Nityānanda had to be the one to save Jagāi and Mādhāi, only you could have saved me.

On this day of your divine appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my prayer is that I don’t remain lower than Jagāi and Mādhāi, who, unlike myself, fully accepted the cooling shelter of Lord Nityānanda’s lotus feet. I pray that one day I can accept your message heart and soul and thus also receive the full shelter of Lord Nityānanda. Then at last I will understand the true meaning of “Peace and Love.”

Your most unworthy servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpresaka Swami

*Jaya Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, Prabhu Nityānanda, Śrī Advaita, Gadādhara, Śrīvāsa-ādi-gaura-bhakta-vṛnda!*

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

We bow down, fall down, at the feet of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Prabhupāda, whose glorification and illumination are all-auspicious for the whole world. By continuously improving our attitude in glorification of him, we can understand Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, and in turn all of the *ācāryas* in our chain of disciplic succession. Thus we can approach Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, the dynamic source of all life.

\* \* \*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are living forever in your books. By hearing your *kīrtana* therein, we become more and more purified. Your books are like an ocean of knowledge. One goes on reading over and over again, hundreds of pages, and finds new details and progressive realization of essential facts. Just by our reading your Bhaktivedanta Purports, the basic principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the essential verses of the *Gītā, Upaniṣads,* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* are impressed upon our hearts over and over and over again. Eventually, like drunken, intoxicated fellows, we begin to realize, “Oh! Ah! Śrīla Prabhupāda has cited *Bhagavad-gītā* 9.10twenty-five times in the last few hundred pages! Oh! Ah! Maybe this is an important verse? Maybe I should read the word-for-word meaning in the *Gītā*. Maybe I should memorize it. Maybe I should scrutinizingly study the purport. Maybe...Maybe...Maybe.”

Most of the problems we face arise from the fact that we have not *realized* the basic principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness profoundly: We are not these bodies. But what is this body anyway? We are not the Supreme Controller. But neither are we zero. So, where does our responsibility for situations begin and end?

It sometimes seems boring that you are repeating the same basic lessons over and over again in your books, but them we think, “This seems boring because we are so stupid that we think we are intelligent. We are so stupid that we are not even continuously afraid of death.” Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for going over these basic points again and again and again until we can make some progress.

Personally, I do not want to die. It seems like a terrifying prospect. Old age, senility, degeneration of the body all begin to seem like ghastly futures. We are hoping to really progress in our purification and re-awaken our natural dormant fascination with Kṛṣṇa and all His fantastic friends and playmates. Please, please forgive our enormous insolence, conceit, selfishness, and stupidity. We pray and pray to Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, that we will eliminate these qualities more and more and stop putting such a strain on your mercy. We pray to be pleasing and useful to the devotees. We pray to get your association through your other disciples and admirers who also read and realize your instructions.

Your words are changing the whole world, little, by little, by little, everywhere. May this go on more and more! May we fill the forthcoming year with book distribution, book distribution, book distribution, and more book distribution.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!!

Your humble servant,  
Hanumatpresaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Today is the celebration of your appearance within this world, and it is a time of great rejoicing. However, my own desire to celebrate such an auspicious event is mixed with feelings of lamentation, for in reality we live in constant separation from Your Divine Grace. In my mind, your appearance was the most significant day in the history of the human race, and conversely your departure was the greatest tragedy for all mankind. The only reconciliation is that today our hearts are full of precious memories of your divine association, and with such remembrance our love for you increases. Hopefully, one day, when we leave these mortal frames, that carefully nurtured love will once again take us to the shelter of your lotus feet, far beyond this world of birth and death.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, meeting you was the turning point in my long sojourn through material existence. How fortunate I was to have your brief association in this life! I first met you through the transcendental medium of your disciples. I vividly recall Viṣṇujana Mahārāja saying, “Come sit, young man, and I will tell you of my spiritual master!” His love for you was contagious, and within moments, by the power of his words, I too was your disciple—ready to fulfill all your desires.

Several months later I saw you in person. The day you arrived at our temple I felt I had known you for lifetimes. Could it be that that had indeed been my good fortune? If not, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that from this point on, wherever you appear in your preaching mission, I may have the good fortune to take birth as your menial servant.

By the time you left the temple you had stolen my heart. For the first time in my life I had experienced unconditional love: *dadāti pratigṛhṇāti guhyam ākhyāti pṛcchati.* You had shared with us the precious jewels of your realizations—the relevant truths of this temporary world and the absolute truths of transcendental world. I also learned for the first time the meaning of spiritual separation. Apprehensive about your departure, I quickly scribbled a note, put $10 in an envelope, and placed it in your lotus hand as you left for the airport.

Two weeks later you appeared again to solidify my faith in you. The postman delivered your letter. But as far as I was concerned, it came from the spiritual sky:

My Dear Bhakta Brian,  
Please accept my blessings  
I thank you for your donation to my newly created book fund. I have information from your authorities that you are doing well in your first months of devotional service. My request is that you always follow in the footsteps of advanced devotees and know for sure that in a very short time I will gladly accept you as my duly initiated disciple.  
I sincerely appreciate the feelings of separation you have expressed in your letter. I also feel separation from my guru maharaja. But I always feel that he is watching over and protecting me.  
Your ever well-wisher, \|  
A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami

I will always be grateful, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that despite your many responsibilities in this world (and the next) you took several moments of your precious time to reach out to a conditioned soul like me. I will never forget this fact, and in return I pledge myself forever to your mission. With confidence, you may send me to heaven or hell; I will never leave your movement—I will never leave your service.

*evaṁ janaṁ nipatitaṁ prabhavāhi-kūpe*  
*kāmābhikāmam anu yaḥ prapatan prasaṅgāt*  
*kṛtvātmasāt surarṣiṇā bhagavan gṛhītaḥ*  
*so ’haṁ kathaṁ nu visṛje tava bhṛtya-sevām*

**“My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But Your servant, Nārada Muni, kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I ever leave his service?” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.9.28)**

Several months later you called me to your room in New York to encourage me in the service in Europe you had recently entrusted to me. Handing me a piece of your own cloth, you once again watered the growing creeper of my attachment to you with these words:

“Preach boldly—and have faith in the holy names!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, if I could tear open my chest like Hanumān, the whole world would see those words forever etched upon my heart. May I truly imbibe those instructions and preach the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa to my final breath.

On the plane to London the next day, you gave me your own *prasādam,* thus enabling me to share the realizations of that great sage Nārada Muni, who upon taking remnants from the plates of great sages experienced the very nature of transcendence.

And what mercy you gave me at the temple in London! While slapping me on the back in firm appreciation for my imperfect service, you said:

“So much endeavor in this material world, but when I take you home, back to Godhead, everything will be easy and sublime!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how many times have I repeated these words both within my mind and in public?! They are the be-all and end-all of my existence. They are the pride of this disciple, who, by the strength of those words finds the ability to go on year after year in the mission of Your Divine Grace. I have faith that when all is said and done, you will honor your promise and take this lowly soul to the Lord’s own transcendental abode.

I am anxious to go, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As I grow older, your words of wisdom continuously prove true. You once quoted Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura:

“Upon the arrival of old age, material happiness becomes impossible.”

But I can wait to go home, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I know there is service to be done. My only request is that from time to time you somehow appear to encourage your spiritual son. It has been a long time since I have seen you. When you were present you won my heart with our brief exchanges. How difficult it has been to live in separation! Please come to me again through the transcendental medium to assure me that I am doing everything as you desire and that you are pleased with my service. Chastise me if you must, but please appear, as you did in the infancy of my spiritual life. I’m still a spiritual child, Śrīla Prabhupāda, attempting to accomplish service reserved for realized devotees. I need your mercy.

Your servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I offer my humble obeisances to my spiritual master. Not to a symbol, an institution, a placeholder, an image. Not an “official Prabhupāda,” or some agreed-upon idea of who he was.

Rather, a person. The person who was kind to me, who knew me, who tolerated me, who helped me, who awed me, who inspired me, who was present before me. The person who saved me.

I was a minor disciple, a rank-and-filer. An incompetent, truth be told, a material failure, and spiritually no great shakes. But he had a place for me, some service for me, a way to make use of a pretty worthless boy, an American atheistic-Jewish kid pickled in psychedelics and proud of his imaginary smarts.

We met, sometimes. He sometimes recognized me. “Did you have any personal association with Prabhupāda?” Sometimes. Now and then. It accrued, over the years.

But our real association, all along, was different. It grew through the mail, through dictaphone tapes sent in re-used envelopes folded and stapled, tapes with little handwritten tags on them—his handwriting—that read, for example, “Kṛṣṇa Ch 87” and started, when you played them, with something like “Kṛṣṇa, Chapter 87 continued.” That association grew through typing and manuscript pages, and editorial scratchings, and meditation—“What does he mean?”—and through appreciation (mine for him), and through trying to do what he wanted.

When he visited Los Angeles and lectured from the canto he was working on, then still not published, our relationship grew as every day I edited and polished the next verse—the text and purport—for the next day’s class, and he would listen attentively as it was read. And he never said anything, but he listened.

And our relationship grew because I respected him, because I trusted him, because I was afraid of him, afraid I would offend him or take him for granted and then my life would be finished. It grew because I would look down at his feet.

It grew because of his kindness and his sternness, his jolliness and his sobriety, his effulgence, his instructions, his example.

It grew because I tried to follow him, to serve him, to do what he wanted me to do. And it was held back my own weaknesses, my own material desires, my own speculations, my stupidity, my unwillingness to do more, to give more, to commit more, to surrender fully, truly surrender.

And our association grew because of his Society, his devotees, his disciples. They were my friends, maybe some of them superficially my enemies, but all of them connected with Prabhupāda, serving Prabhupāda, reminding me of Prabhupāda, creating and sustaining a world in which serving him and pleasing him mattered more than anything else. A world that was for Kṛṣṇa.

Kṛṣṇa’s world, the world of the holy name, of the books, of the Deities and Their *prasādam,* the world of devotional exchanges between Kṛṣṇa and His devotees. That world, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s world, visible the more we can let go of our illusions and choose to enter it through purity and dedication, through sacrifice, through Kṛṣṇa consciousness, through the path of the *ācāryas,* Śrīla Prabhupāda’s path.

He one time, early on, touched me on the head while I was bowed down, and said to me, “Very good boy.” He one time told me I was a fool for burning some vegetable. He several other times disclosed that I was a fool. He sometimes praised me.

I’m still around—is it a great accomplishment, merely that? And I’m still trying to serve him. And if I am anything, at least if I’m still out of the gutter, it is only by his kindness, by the strength that comes from remembering him and trying to follow him and do what he wanted, by the pleasure that comes from sometimes doing something I’m certain would please him.

“And if even one man becomes a pure devotee of the Lord....” it will be by his causeless mercy.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

*namo brahmaṇya-devāya go-brāhmaṇa-hitāya ca*  
*jagad-dhitāya kṛṣṇāya govindāya namo namaḥ*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this last year proved to be a very disturbing year for me after learning that there are thousands of slaughterhouses in the holy land of Bharat, where hundreds and thousands of sacred cows are being slaughtered every day for their hides. All this is happening under the very noses of the authorities, who choose to look away. King Parīkṣit could not tolerate Kali’s abuse of the cow. That was just one cow. But now it is millions of cows being massacred for financial gain, and the authorities simply look on. We made protests to the present government, but to no avail. To our deep frustration, the authorities simply chose to ignore us, doing nothing to curb this horrible practice. The sanctity of Bharat is in ruins because of this one single brutal practice. Lord Kṛṣṇa, the protector of the *brāhmaṇas* and the sacred cows, must be angered by this barbarous action.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wanted us to protect the cows. Therefore you inspired us to build farm communities and *gośālās* where the cows will be looked after. Please empower us now, your disciples and grand-disciples, to do the needful. I earnestly pray to Your Divine Grace to give me the strength to counter this abuse of the cows. The sins of the world are increasing day by day, and the reaction due to this is visible by the increase in catastrophes all around the world—and yet the leaders take no notice. *Gomātā* is calling for help, and we feel we are unable to give this help. It is very frustrating, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Realizing that this world is full of misery, we continue to do our daily *bhajana* and glorify Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Despite my illness, I am hoping to do more this year in serving your wonderful movement. Now and then, we sincerely try to clear in-house misunderstandings so we can expend our energies in the right direction in expanding your movement. Sometimes situations stack up against us and make it difficult for us to preach peacefully. Sometimes the very credibility of ISKCON comes into question, such as in the case of the child abuse allegations and court cases related to the “ritviks.” So much energy, time, and money is then channeled away from the main goals. We are praying, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that sooner or later we will stop being a liability to your movement and instead become an asset. Thank you once again for being a towering inspiration, a shining beacon in this age of dense darkness. Haribol!

Your humble servant,  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This year I missed the Gaura Pūrṇimā festival for the first time in twenty-eight years. Just before leaving Māyāpur, I spoke with Jayapatāka Mahārāja. He asked, “How can you leave, Mahārāja? How have you become so stone-hearted?” This is his thirty-first year at the Gaura Pūrṇimā festival. He has imbibed your spirit of participation in the festival. Your spirit is embodied in the reply you made to Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami, who asked you whether you weren’t tired after so many busy days conducting the 1976 Māyāpur festival. You said, “What ‘tired’? So many people are coming and hearing about Kṛṣṇa. When you’re preaching, you feel refreshed.”

While riding away from Māyāpur after the GBC meetings, I felt bad about leaving early. My only consolation was that I was on my way to Jagannātha Purī to make the final revisions on my new book, *Festivals,* which is subtitled “Prabhupāda at the Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana Festivals.” This book glorifies the festivals you established and attended. I interviewed about a hundred devotees to collect remembrances of those festivals. This book is coming out today, on your Vyāsa-pūjā day, as my offering to your lotus feet.

While working on the book in Purī, I saw a number of my Godbrothers there, also absent from the Māyāpur festival. One of them, on his way to the Māyāpur festival, said to me, “I see so many devotees here. Isn’t the festival enlivening anymore?” Pañcaratna Prabhu also felt that our participation in the festivals is below standard. I quote him in my book as saying, “Śrīla Prabhupāda set the standard for his leaders to follow by his regular attendance at the Māyāpur festivals. He went to great lengths to enliven and inspire the devotees. In Māyāpur he made himself available to the devotees—and not just for a few days. He would be there the whole time, going on walks, giving classes and *darśanas,* and overseeing the GBC meetings.”

These festivals are one of your legacies, and we senior devotees and leaders cannot ignore it. You used to be the first to arrive at the festivals and the last to leave. Dropping whatever you were doing, you would travel to Māyāpur from wherever you were in the world.

Attending the Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana festivals is a principle of *bhajana* and *sādhana*. You integrated this *bhajana* into the ISKCON devotees’ yearly *sādhana,* but somehow we have become deviated or distracted from attending the festivals. As a result, we do not derive the benefits, and the movement is the loser. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura said about the Vaiṣṇava, “He lives forever through his divine instructions, and his followers live with him.” Because we are not following your instructions regarding the festivals, we are not getting the benefit of associating with your instructions. What we need to do is revive the festivals and keep them going the way you wanted. Working on my book in Purī was like revisiting those days with you at the festivals. This topic has been a meditation for me over the past few years, as I sorted through the interviews and the other materials to paint a picture of those festival times. We have fallen short of the spirit of those days when you were present, and it hurts to see how the festivals have deteriorated. The Vṛndāvana festival is even weaker than the Māyāpur festival because more leaders skip it, though the devotees are still into it. We are not doing it the way you did it. To put my book together I interviewed dozens and dozens of devotees, and they all concluded there was a really high spirit in those days. Everyone practically fought to get to the festivals. No one wanted to miss that once-in-a-year opportunity.

Most of our second-generation devotees do not know what they are missing. They did not experience the early festivals. So your disciples who lead the movement have an obligation to maintain and re-enact the festivals. “Do as I did,” you often said. You also said that the leaders should think of the world first and their local areas second. The Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana festival is an opportunity to put this instruction into practice. Devotees from all over the world come to India, where we can serve one another, associate with one another, and inspire one another. The ISKCON body will get healthier by following this principle.

Still, in many ways things have improved. The primitive accommodations and substandard *prasādam* are things of the past. During your centennial year, two thousand devotees attended the festivals. Annual *parikramas* are now being conducted in Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana by the traditional method—walking—just as your spiritual master did them. The Māyāpur Institute of Higher Education (MIHE) conducts seminars to facilitate the hearing, chanting, and education of the devotees.

On this auspicious occasion, I dedicate my book to you, and pray that it may create a higher awareness of the significance of these festivals. I hope it educates and inspires our leaders and readers in general to attend the festivals and thereby preserve and renew these festivals, which you personally developed.

Your eternal servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Today is Lord Nityānanda’s appearance day. I am now visiting the Soho St. Temple, in London. My responsibility is for our ISKCON projects in Nepal, Bangladesh, and India. I feel especially close to you here because it was here in London that I first joined your movement, in January 1971. I first saw you coming in the door at 7 Bury Place, just near here by the British Museum, on August 4, 1971. Devotees often ask when I first saw you, and that event is always sweet to remember and talk about, as fresh realizations appear every time.

As an enthusiastic new *bhakta* and your aspiring disciple, I was still very curious to see you in the flesh and personally evaluate you, the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement’s famous guru—the one who all the devotees were so happy to welcome. I was also happy, but I wondered if I would find some fault in you. When you entered the door as Kṛṣṇa’s representative on earth, you were strikingly normal, self-composed, and modest. When you lectured, you simply and boldly gave the same message as you gave in your books. I had heard it before! There was no inconsistency, bluff, or show off. I was disarmed. It was a confirmation. I could find no fault.

But I had accepted you in my heart nine months before this meeting. It was in December 1970 that I first read your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* in the abridged Collier-Macmillan edition. I was living at Canterbury, Kent, UK. I had started to chant the *mahā-mantra* and had bought that book from the devotees at the London temple. Before reading it I had butterflies in my stomach out of expectancy. It was the culmination of a confused but most fortunate person’s search for understanding God. It was as though the reactions of the past were standing still and I was entering a new future. Through your disciples, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave your mercy to one great proud fool.

In retrospect, meeting you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* was a simple, almost standard thing that fortunate people are doing every day. But it was momentous for me. Your authoritative presentation of Kṛṣṇa made you the transparent via medium. Through your merciful touch I could realize—at least partially—that I was Kṛṣṇa’s eternal servant. For the first time in my twenty-six years I could hear the voice of someone who actually had true knowledge and realization of God. You were therefore the first person who I could surrender to wholeheartedly. You easily and naturally answered those questions nobody had ever answered before. You gave me such an unexpected and inconceivable gift.

Your gift was the most precious one possible. It was Kṛṣṇa—Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. You gave me access to begin to understand the very personality of God—who He actually was. By your mercy I began to grasp that five thousand years ago God Almighty had appeared in His original transcendental form as Pārtha-sārathi, the chariot driver and friend of Arjuna. His body, though human-sized, was completely spiritual, and it was the source of all energies, incarnations, and infinite universes. We tiny souls were His eternal fragmental parts and parcels. You revealed to me who God was and who I was. Śrīla Prabhupāda, through your *vāṇī* you showed me clearly that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and I am His eternal servant. I recognized you as Kṛṣṇa’s representative. I wanted to become your disciple. Therefore I came to join the temple here in London. Therefore I had my first *darśana* of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā -Londonīśvara.

This *sambandha-jñāna* was certainly all your credit, Śrīla Prabhupāda—not mine. It was only because I was already chanting the *mahā-mantra—*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—that I was blessed to understand your words in the introduction to *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. Before I chanted, I had no entrance to them. That mantra was delivered to me by your faithful disciples in London. Your words then revealed Kṛṣṇa. Our meeting was supervised by Kṛṣṇa. The faithful servant—you—brought your master’s lost son (me) back to the presence of your master (Kṛṣṇa) again.

I was changed so much because I was in ignorance and you brought divine knowledge. *Cakṣu-dāna dila yei.* You opened my eyes. On my own I could never have conceived that God was actually the Supreme Person with a transcendental form of eternity, bliss, and knowledge—*sac-cid-ānanda-vigraha*. Before your mercy rained, my senses and heart were closed up to the possibility of such a wonderful person as Kṛṣṇa, the Absolute Truth Personality of Godhead.

Your gift of Kṛṣṇa is for everyone—*ahaṁ bīja-pradaḥ pitā*. He is everyone’s seed-giving father coming in His holy name!! You are Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi’s agent empowered to give love of Kṛṣṇa to everyone! By your mercy the flood of Kṛṣṇa consciousness inexorably spreads by the loyal service of your surrendered servants, who are always adoring your lotus feet.

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

You have sent us boys out as spiritual masters—seemingly frail warriors—to fight with Māyā at the command of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the master at whose lotus feet many other masters sit.

You are the *senapati bhakta,* the great Commander in Chief devotee predicted by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura in his *Caitanya-maṅgala*. We are your chosen forces to wage war on the ignorance that enslaves the miserable people in this age. Even though many of your generals soldiers have already fallen in the battle to save the world, you have taught us there is no loss or diminution spiritually. We may even fall, but we will still try to sacrifice our lives for you. We will take the risk for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Why? Because you gave us life! You gave us the gift of our own lost lives. So what is there to lose in your service? You are giving new life to the deadened condemned souls of this Age of Kali.

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am still with you—at least partially, according to my meager degree of surrender. I am still your struggling, feeble spiritual child. Please accept my gratitude on this, your divine appearance day.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for Their sweet Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara and Śrī Girirāja Mahārāja! Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for Their powerful Lordships Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā! Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for Their all-merciful Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi!

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for Their Lordships mercy, *mahā-prasādam*! Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your books, tapes, and videos! Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all the devotees of ISKCON, the GBC, the ministries, the farms, the *gurukulas,* restaurants, the Ratha-yātrā...Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything!

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, most of all for the wonderful *mahā-mantra* to chant—which is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa.

Your humble servant,  
Mahāviṣṇu Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In your transcendental books and instructions you often talk about the bona fide guru. Thus the topic of who is a bona fide guru must be a very important one. When we refer to guru, we refer to three main types of gurus. As you write in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 8.128, purport): “The word guru is equally applicable to the *vartma-pradarśaka-guru, śikṣā-guru,* and *dīkṣā-guru.*” Due to the problems some of our ISKCON gurus have had, one may wonder, If only one who is very advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness can be a bona fide guru, how can others less exalted also qualify?

In your instructions you seem to emphasize two qualifications of bona fide gurus. One is that they have learned Lord Kṛṣṇa’s teachings from their superior guru in His disciplic succession. The second is that under the guidance of their superiors they repeat His teachings and work on His behalf.

Let us see what you have said about bona fide gurus in your *Nectar of Devotion* lectures in Vṛndāvana:

**Where is the difficulty in finding a bona fide guru?**

“It is the fortunate person who comes in contact with a bona fide spiritual master.” This question, we were just discussing before coming to the class, how to select a bona fide spiritual master. So that is not very difficult. Just like in our ordinary business life, we accept somebody as representative of the firm who is actually come, canvassing for the benefit of the firm. He’s representative. Suppose he’s representing some bookseller, publisher, so he should canvass for selling the books published by his firm, not for anything else. Suppose he has taken the advantage of becoming representative of a business firm, but he’s doing his own business. He’s not bona fide. He’s not bona fide. So real guru is Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa....Kṛṣṇa existed before the creation. Then He made His representative, Brahmā. *Tene brahma hṛdā ādi-kavaye*. He instructed the original guru, Brahmā....So therefore, the original guru is Kṛṣṇa. The same guru, Kṛṣṇa, is instructing Arjuna also. Kṛṣṇa became guru of Arjuna. Arjuna accepted Him guru: *śiṣyas te ’ham*. Arjuna said, “Now I am not talking with You as friend, but I accept You as my guru.”...Anyone is accepting the authority of *Bhagavad-gītā,* he’s accepting, imperceptibly, Kṛṣṇa as guru. Therefore, bona fide spiritual master means who is representing Kṛṣṇa....

So to find out a bona fide spiritual master is not a very difficult job, because if one is representing guru, Kṛṣṇa, then he must speak of Kṛṣṇa, canvass for Kṛṣṇa....

So that is bona fide guru. Where is the difficulty to find out a bona fide guru? Just like Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, *yāre dekha tāre kaha kṛṣṇa upadeśa* \[Cc. *Madhya* 7.128\]. Caitanya Mahāprabhu says that “You preach the words of Kṛṣṇa.” Therefore He’s bona fide. Similarly, anyone who is representing Kṛṣṇa and canvassing for Kṛṣṇa, he’s bona fide guru. Where is the difficulty? Is there any difficulty?

Anyone can understand that if Kṛṣṇa is the original guru, and if somebody’s canvassing for Kṛṣṇa, he’s bona fide guru. If somebody’s canvassing for himself, he’s not bona fide guru. So there is no difficulty to find out a bona fide guru, provided I am serious to find out a bona fide guru. But if I want something else...Because *hṛdyantaḥ stho hy abhadrāṇi.*If in my heart there is something else, then I must be cheated by some...somebody else who is not a bona fide guru. *Ye yathā māṁ prapadyante tāṁs tathaiva bhajāmy aham* \[Bg. 4.11\].

\[Lecture on *The Nectar of Devotion,* Vṛndāvana, 31 October 1972\]

**The bona fide guru presents the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is**

So to become a bona fide spiritual master, there is no difficulty, if you, simply, if we carry the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is, without any adulteration. So we must find out a person who is actually bona fide spiritual master by this test, that he’s not, I mean to say, adulterating Kṛṣṇa’s message. He’s not playing havoc with the message of Kṛṣṇa in order to introduce himself, his person. He’s simply presenting the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is. Then he’s spiritual master. Nobody else.

\[Lecture on *The Nectar of Devotion,* Vṛndāvana, 31 October 1972\]

**The bona fide guru is in the disciplic succession from Lord Kṛṣṇa**

One has to accept a guru, a spiritual master, who has received knowledge from another perfect spiritual master. Just like Kṛṣṇa is the origin, perfect spiritual master, guru. So Kṛṣṇa, what Kṛṣṇa said, was realized by Arjuna, directly. Therefore if we receive knowledge from Arjuna or his disciplic succession, then our knowledge is perfect. Kṛṣṇa...Arjuna accepted Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Brahman: *paraṁ brahma paraṁ dhāma pavitraṁ paramaṁ bhavān*\[Bg. 10.12\]. So if we accept the version of Arjuna, that Kṛṣṇa is Paraṁ Brahman, He’s the Supreme Person, He’s the origin of everything, then our knowledge is perfect. I may be imperfect, but because I receive knowledge from a perfect person, my knowledge is perfect. This is called paramparā system.

So in order to dissipate our ignorance, we should receive perfect knowledge. Then we can stop our sinful activities.

\[Lecture on *The Nectar of Devotion,* Vṛndāvana, 5 November 1972\]

**Lord Kṛṣṇa is the real friend of the people**

We are becoming leaders, the friend of the people. But we are not friend of the people. Kṛṣṇa is the friend of...Suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānām. If I simply say that “I am your friend in this sense: that I deliver the message to you that ‘Kṛṣṇa is your best friend.’ I do not...I am friend so far I am giving you this information. But actual friend is Kṛṣṇa,” suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānām, so this is friendship. If one preaches Kṛṣṇa consciousness and teaches everyone that Kṛṣṇa is your best friend...He does not say, “I am your best friend.” “I am your best friend in this sense: that I am giving you this information.” Actually, Kṛṣṇa is your best friend. What can I do? I am a teeny living entity. What can I do for you? I be...may become your friend, but when you are in danger, I cannot give you any protection. Kṛṣṇa can give you protection. This is real friendship. He does not take himself. He always carries the message only. Ya imaṁ mad-bhakteṣu abhidāsyati. Simply our business is to carry the message of Kṛṣṇa. Then we are friend. Otherwise we are not friend. We may pose to become friend, but we are not friend because we do not know how to benefit the friend. Sometimes we mislead him.

Therefore our business is to point that “Kṛṣṇa is your friend.” Suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānām. “He’s the proprietor. Don’t claim your proprietorship. You are not proprietor. You are falsely claiming; because what you are claiming as your property, it will be taken away by Kṛṣṇa at the time of your death. Everything will be finished.” Mṛtyuḥ sarva-haraś ca aham, Kṛṣṇa says. Mṛtyu...Kṛṣṇa says, “I am death when I take away everything from the person—his life, property, body, and everything.” Actually, we claim, “This is my property.” This is moha. Ahaṁ mameti \[SB 5.5.8\]. Lokasya janasya moho ’yam. This is friendship, to educate people that “You are not proprietor. Kṛṣṇa is proprietor. You are not enjoyer. Kṛṣṇa is enjoyer. You are seeking out friends to give you protection. Kṛṣṇa is the supreme friend who will give you protection.” If we spread this message all over the world, then I am friend of Kṛṣṇa, I am friend of the persons. Otherwise, I am not friend, because I cannot become friend. I can simply carry...Just like post peon gives you five thousand rupees, delivers. He does not deliver. Somebody else is delivering. He’s simply carrying. That’s all. Similarly, if we simply carry the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is, we become perfect. It is not very difficult. If I simply carry the message of Kṛṣṇa, where is the difficulty? Everything is there. Kṛṣṇa has said everything. Kṛṣṇa says, man-manā mad-bhakto mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru. We carry this message. Just always think of Kṛṣṇa, always offer obeisances to Kṛṣṇa, become a devotee of Kṛṣṇa. Man-manā, think of Kṛṣṇa always. We have to carry this message. Where is the difficulty?

So to become a bona fide spiritual master, there is no difficulty, if you simply, if we carry the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is, without any adulteration. So we must find out a person who is actually bona fide spiritual master by this test, that he’s not, I mean to say, adulterating Kṛṣṇa’s message. He’s not playing havoc with the message of Kṛṣṇa in order to introduce himself, his person. He’s simply presenting the message of Kṛṣṇa as it is. Then he’s spiritual master. Nobody else.

\[Lecture on *The Nectar of Devotion,* Vṛndāvana, 31 October 1972)

Still, aren’t there different levels of gurus? Are some more qualified than others? Sometimes we hear that a guru must be on the topmost level of devotional service, and sometimes that devotees on other levels also can be bona fide gurus.

**The guru must be on the topmost level of devotional service**

The guru must be situated on the topmost platform of devotional service. There are three classes of devotees, and the guru must be accepted from the topmost class. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 24.330\]

**The second class devotees accepts disciples**

The second-class devotees are therefore meant for preaching work, and as referred to in the above verse, they must loudly preach the glories of the Lord. The second-class devotee accepts disciples from the section of third-class devotees or nondevotees. Sometimes the first-class devotee also comes down to the category of the second-class devotee for preaching work. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.3.21, purport\]

**Two types of instructing gurus**

Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī states that the instructing spiritual master is a bona fide representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself teaches us as the instructing spiritual master from within and without. From within He teaches as Paramātmā, our constant companion, and from without He teaches from the *Bhagavad-gītā* as the instructing spiritual master. There are two kinds of instructing spiritual masters. One is the liberated person fully absorbed in meditation in devotional service, and the other is he who invokes the disciple’s spiritual consciousness by means of relevant instructions. Thus the instructions in the science of devotion are differentiated in terms of the objective and subjective ways of understanding. The ācārya in the true sense of the term, who is authorized to deliver Kṛṣṇa, enriches the disciple with full spiritual knowledge and thus awakens him to the activities of devotional service. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 1.47, purport\]

We may conclude that anyone who is sincerely canvassing on behalf of Kṛṣṇa and is strictly following the process of devotional service received in disciplic succession is a bona fide representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa. However, there are different levels of realization among those who are bona fide, and we should always be prepared to serve all those who are more advanced than we are so that we can also become advanced. Mangoes are all mangoes, but some may be more ripe and sweeter than others.

Even if there are some defects in some devotees, if they are more advanced than we are in devotional service, they must be considered our gurus and we should try to see and appreciate how they are engaged in devotional service. Everyone should try to see in this way. Devotees may be in the position of being gurus to others; however, for them to continue be bona fide gurus they must remain servants to their own gurus.

If we consider the bodily defects of a Vaiṣṇava, we should understand that we are committing an offense at the lotus feet of the Vaiṣṇava. An offense at the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava is very serious. Indeed, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has described this offense as hātī mātā, the mad elephant offense. A mad elephant can create a disaster, especially when it enters into a nicely trimmed garden. One should therefore be very careful not to commit any offense against a Vaiṣṇava. Every devotee should be ready to take instructions from a superior Vaiṣṇava, and a superior Vaiṣṇava must be ready to help an inferior Vaiṣṇava in all respects. One is superior or inferior according to his spiritual development in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One is forbidden to observe the activities of a pure Vaiṣṇava from a material point of view. For the neophyte especially, considering a pure devotee from a material point of view is very injurious. One should therefore avoid observing a pure devotee externally, but should try to see the internal features and understand how he is engaged in the transcendental loving service of the Lord. In this way one can avoid seeing the pure devotee from a material point of view, and thus one can gradually become a purified devotee himself. \[The Nectar of Instruction 6, purport\]

We have to learn to discriminate among the different level of devotees—*kaniṣṭha, madhyama,* and *uttama*—so we can properly honor and respect them. Although some devotees, by their example and instructions, may help us more than others to advance in spiritual life, still, we can and should consider as our bona fide and worshipable guru any devotee who is following an authorized devotee strictly engaged in devotional service and Kṛṣṇa consciousness and who is more advanced than we are.

Aspiring for your service,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this most blissful and auspicious day, I want to thank you again and again for the opportunity that you are granting to me in performing devotional service. Especially, I want to thank you for keeping me alive and involved with your book distribution.

I pray that you always allow me to be an instrument of your desire to flood the universe with your transcendental words.

Your most insignificant servant,  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**A Heart to Heart Relationship**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

On this auspicious day of your divine appearance, I am praying helplessly in front of you to obtain your mercy, so that I can always see clearly your divine direction and actually please you.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you describe your own sweet relationship to your spiritual master:

I think that His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura is always seeing my activities and guiding me within my heart by his words. As it is said in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, tene brahma hṛdā ya ādi-kavaye.* Spiritual inspiration comes from within the heart, wherein the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in His Paramātmā feature, is always sitting with all His devotees and associates. \[Concluding Words, *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*\]

My urgent desire is to develop a similar relationship with you, a relationship where you will be always present in my heart.

I remember the days when you were personally visiting and guiding us. By the potency of your divine association, you lifted all of us beyond the three modes. Kṛṣṇa is in the heart of His pure devotee, and everyone who came in contact with you could feel that “special energy” emanating from you. Just by being in your presence, we found ourselves in a Kṛṣṇa conscious state where we spontaneously asked questions like: “How can we become more Kṛṣṇa conscious? What do you expect each one of us to do?” You nourished this willingness in your disciples by liberally sharing your exalted Kṛṣṇa consciousness with us—in fact, by delivering Kṛṣṇa to us. Everyone could experience it.

On this day I meditate on the question of whether the situation is forever gone with your physical disappearance from us in 1977, or whether you can again appear in my heart. If this reappearance is possible, what would I have to do from my side to make it possible? For your pleasure I will try to offer my thoughts on this subject to you.

Please do correct me if there is need, and please help me to expand my understanding.

**Real Association**

Real association always takes place in the heart, which is the seat of the mind. At one time you said, “Devotee means the mind!”

Externally I may act and present myself as your disciple, but I am really only your disciple if I bring my mind in tune with your instructions and intentions. Ultimately all my thinking, feeling, and willing should be made harmonious with yours. At such a time I will enter into your heart and you into mine: one in interest. We have often heard the example of iron that becomes fire in the association of fire. We glorify Rūpa Gosvāmī because he understood the intentions of Caitanya Mahāprabhu and knew how to execute them.

For the disciple to reach such a high level of communication, it is certainly indispensable for him to come to the platform where he has purified his mind. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has explained that one starts devotional service by engaging one’s external senses, in other words the body. At that stage the uncontrolled mind may still run about in the world and in fact be out of tune with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. After this stage, the devotee should carefully control the mind and bring it slowly to the stage of absorption.

The *ācārya* describes that this is done in three consecutive ways:

First comes *abhyāsa,* that is, practicing control of the mind. Wherever the mind goes, one has to bring it back into the realm of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The second stage is *manana,* or reflection, in which a devotee thinks of the instructions from all angles of view, and finally comes *smaraṇam,* or constant remembrance.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura has said about this remembrance:

If I do not remember the lotus feet of Śrī Guru at the beginning of every new year, every new month, every new day, and every new moment, then I am sure to fall into far greater inconveniences. If I do not do so, I will want to dress myself in the garb of the guru, I will become liable to the bad desire of seeking to be worshiped by other people as guru. It is this which constitutes addiction to things other than the truth. \[“My Guru Pūjā,” Sajjana Toṣaṇī, March 1932\].

By your mercy I already have some little experience of how connecting oneself in the mind with you is not just an imaginative exercise but a powerful way of association, through which you give inspiration, empowerment, and guidance from within the heart.

As human beings, we all have a wonderful “satellite station” inside of us—the heart. Unfortunately, it has gone in disrepair, but once it is repaired by the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we can certainly receive spiritual messages from you and from the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

**Meeting You Again**

In our glorious line, the means and the goal are the same. Kṛṣṇa, the *sādhya,* is identical with the first *sādhana,* that is, the chanting of the holy name.

When the guru gives his disciple Kṛṣṇa, he gives him the holy name. This point is explained wonderfully by your enlightening purport to one of the verses in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.2.18) describing the extraordinary appearance of the Supreme Personality of Godhead in this world:

TEXT: Thereafter, accompanied by plenary expansions, the fully opulent Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is all-auspicious for the entire universe, was transferred from the mind of Vasudeva to the mind of Devakī. Devakī, having thus been initiated by Vasudeva, became beautiful by carrying Lord Kṛṣṇa, the original consciousness for everyone, the cause of all causes, within the core of her heart, just as the east becomes beautiful by carrying the rising moon.

PURPORT: As indicated here by the word *manastaḥ,* the Supreme Personality of Godhead was transferred from the core of Vasudeva’s mind or heart to the core of the heart of Devakī. We should note carefully that the Lord was transferred to Devakī not by the ordinary way for a human being, but by *dīkṣā,* initiation. Thus the importance of initiation is mentioned here. Unless one is initiated by the right person, who always carries within his heart the Supreme Personality of Godhead, one cannot acquire the power to carry the Supreme Godhead within the core of one’s own heart.

After having received Kṛṣṇa in the form of the holy names, the disciple has the duty to nourish this divine gift. In his instructions to Rūpa Gosvāmī, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu compared the gift of the spiritual master to a seed, which needs to be taken care of in order to properly grow into a tree and deliver the desired fruit. The disciple must become a conscientious gardener, protecting his spiritual life from *aparādha* and nourishing it through *śravaṇam* and *kīrtanam*. In this way, what he has been given will produce the desired fruit of love of Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you once said that of all the instructions of the spiritual master, the instruction to chant one’s rounds is most important. As our eternal well-wisher, you wish that we make use of your divine gift and realize Kṛṣṇa in His holy name by careful *sādhana*. By following your instructions on chanting, we will automatically develop great affection for you and eventually meet you with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in our heart. Chanting means to keep Kṛṣṇa always “within your heart,” you once explained. And I am certain: “Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, there you are, and wherever you are, there is Kṛṣṇa.”

sādhavo hṛdayaṁ mahyaṁ sādhūnām hṛdayaṁ tv aham  
mad-anyat te na jānanti nāhaṁ tebhyo manāg api

\[The Lord said:\] The pure devotee is always within the core of My heart, and I am always in the heart of the pure devotee. My devotees do not know anything else but Me, and I do not know anyone else but them. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*9.4.68\]

I feel that now you wish to bring me to the level of being close to you—and I thank you from the depths of my heart. With great shame I am standing before you, always your ignorant child, fully dependent on your mercy. What I have written about are deep subjects—the full realizations of which are still beyond me. To come to this level of experiencing your presence and guidance in the heart is difficult for someone like me. It means that I have to do my homework, and it means to always be conscious of you in my life. Ultimately, it means to bring the restless mind fully under the control of Your Divine Grace. I wish to come to that stage as soon as possible; I do not wish to waste any more time. From my experience with you during your visits to Germany and England, I know that this state is a very happy state in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. To surrender one’s independence to the lotus feet of you is extremely relieving and refreshing. Only then can one enter into the kingdom of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please continue to shower your mercy on me, and do not lose patience, although I am such a slow disciple.

Always begging for your mercy,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Without a doubt your mercy is all that I am made of.

On this auspicious day of your appearance anniversary we, your disciples and grand-disciples, remember you. We try to praise you and enumerate your glories. You are the most important spiritual teacher to appear in the twentieth century. You presented a practical and easy method for everyone to attain spiritual perfection and get free from suffering in the cycle of birth and death. Most amazing is how you struggled with every drop of your lifeblood to achieve this.

At the advanced age of sixty-nine you traveled to the West alone, as the only passenger on a steamship. You survived two death-threatening heart attacks on the boat and another after a year and a half in America. You were so determined to serve the order of your Guru Mahārāja that your life was extended.

You astonished the world by translating more books on *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* than anyone else before you. You initiated thousands of disciples in a hostile world, where you were the pioneer of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism in the west. You gave us a blue print for happiness in this world and then personally sacrificed everything to teach us by your example how to achieve the goal. You didn’t stop teaching us and others until your last breath, when you returned to your beloved Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

All these things I know. I was there to see much of it, and I have read more every year since your disappearance. Now, after seeing the world in my traveling, I am feeling only slightly satisfied. I don’t think many others except your disciples remember you. I don’t think you are yet appreciated the way you, as God’s incarnation as guru, should be worshiped and praised.

Before I came in contact with devotees, I was aware of the world and suicidal. I was practically dead. No direction. No meaning. My mind was reeling. I faced only desolation in my life.

It was out of your extreme kindness that you breathed life into me by your ambrosial words and saintly association. I was dead and you gave me life. And then you left me alone.

Again I am dead (almost more than metaphorically).

In the beginning of 2000 I came out of a coma caused by this devil dragon in my blood. My defenses were down. I had suffered massive hepatic encephalopathy (ammonia on the brain), and I had no control. I became very emotional. I was crying much of the day, thinking “I have not served my spiritual master. He has saved me and I have done nothing to repay him.” Some of my Godbrothers encouraged me to go to Vṛndāvana and chant. It was good advice. But I couldn’t digest it.

I think of you as a great *ācārya* for sure, but also as a warrior teaching us to preach Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu’s mission till the end. So I determined that that is what I will attempt in a small way, to begin some repayment, which I fear I will never complete. It is just fine, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I am happy living this way. I want people to know you. It is the only thing that gives me pleasure. When someone begins to realize that millions and millions of years of journeying is at an end when they meet you, I get unbounded happiness. You are such a person.

So now I carry on, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I flash back to my childhood, when there was a series of movies called *Carry on Nursing, Carry on Teaching,* etc. So I will carry on preaching your glories, and nothing will stop me. I know others are doing so as well, and I pray more will do so. There is so much to do.

You may trample me down with your feet or put me through any test. I am yours to do as you like. I was dead and you gave me life. Now it is all yours. Such a man are you. Cāṇakya Paṇḍita has said, *lālane bahavo doṣās tāḍane bahavo guṇāḥ:* “Pampering increases faulty habits, while chastisement increases good qualities.” *Tasmāt putraṁ ca śiṣyaṁ ca tāḍayen na tu lālayet:* “Therefore, you should always chastise your sons and disciples. Never give them lenience.” A little lenience and the faults will grow.

Please embrace me or chastise me, but always keep me as your foot dust, as your dog, in any way you desire. I need you so much. Please allow me to preach on your behalf for as long as possible.

Prabhupāda’s dog,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Subhāga Swami

On this auspicious day of your appearance, my dear Srila Prabhupada, I offer my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Please be kind to us. You may be watching us from the spiritual world, and you may be observing who is sincere, serious, enthusiastic, to carry out your orders.

Please guide us in times of confusion and bewilderment, as you have been doing all the time. Devotees, devotional service, and the spiritual world are eternal. Despite the fact that we are in the material world, you were very kind to us and gave us the most precious thing: love of Godhead.

If we follow your instructions sincerely and seriously, then we can qualify ourselves. It is our hope that one day we can please you as you pleased your spiritual master. Prahlāda Mahārāja, the greatest devotee, instructed that one must bathe in the dust of the lotus feet of the pure devotee—then he can start devotional service. *Pāda-rajaḥ abhiṣekam* (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.5.32)

Caitanya Mahāprabhu taught us:

*tṛṇād api su-nīcena taror iva sahiṣṇunā*  
*amāninā māna-dena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*

He also said:

*ayi nanda-tanuja kiṅkaraṁ*  
*patitaṁ māṁ viṣame bhavāmbudhau*  
*kṛpayā tava pāda-paṅkaja-*  
*sthita-dhūlī-sadṛśaṁ vicintaya*

You and all the previous *ācāryas* are observing us. We are always being tested—both by the higher authorities and by the Lord Himself. Your teachings and those of all the previous *ācāryas’* are guiding us to solve the various problems in our lives. It is in your books that we find these instructions and this guidance on how to solve our problems.

With our body, mind, and heart we should try to follow your instructions and try to please you. You said that without being self-controlled, obedient, and disciplined, one can’t carry out the order of the spiritual master, and without carrying out all the instructions of the spiritual master, one cannot to go back to Godhead. This is our goal, to go back to Godhead, so please inspire us, give us the strength, give us the hope and faith, so that we can be obedient, controlled, and disciplined and thus easily go back to Godhead. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Caitanya Mahāprabhu predicted that in every town and village people would chant the holy name of God, that is, the name of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, of Kṛṣṇa and Rāma. You took the instructions of your Guru Mahārāja seriously and tried to fulfill them to the best of your capacity. Thus you were blessed by your Guru Mahārāja and the previous *ācāryas* to fulfill their orders.

Therefore, we should also take seriously your instructions and try to carry them out with all our sincerity and seriousness. We must give the best of all that belongs to us and dedicate ourselves completely at your lotus feet. In this way we will surely receive your blessings.

We can also give the blessings to the people of this world. There is so much to do in order to make available to the people of the world the *kṛpā* you are giving. If we simply try to fulfill your desires and try our best to propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness by working cooperatively, unitedly, without creating factions in the Society, I’m sure we can work nicely and please you. We can receive your blessings and the blessings of the previous *ācāryas*.

Those who are born in India have no responsibility save and accept the responsibility to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. You reminded those who are born in India that they have got this special responsibility. Please give us the strength and power so that according to our capacity we can strive to fulfill this responsibility.

Many of your followers are still doing wonderful service and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness by making many devotees. It is my humble prayer to you on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, that you also empower me so I can work together with my Godbrothers and inspire others to bring the ignorant nondevotees from material consciousness to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We wish to make them devotees of the Lord and offer them at the lotus feet of the pure devotee. Kṛṣṇa said this is the greatest service. The success of preaching is to make more people devotees of the Lord. It’s not easy to become a devotee. A devotee belongs to the spiritual world. He must be decorated with all the wonderful qualities of the Vaikuṇṭhalokas.

To fulfill your instructions and desires requires great dedication and sacrifice; only in this way can we pass the test of the guru and the *ācāryas*. Please, Prabhupāda, bless us and give us the faith and strength so we can pass this test. We wish to become pure, completely surrendered, and to dedicate everything so that we can develop the qualities of the pure devotee.

We wish to become a Vaiṣṇava in the pure sense and make others Vaiṣṇavas. As many as we can. Even if we can make only one person a good devotee, I am sure you will be pleased, the *ācāryas* will be pleased, Kṛṣṇa will be pleased, Caitanya Mahāprabhu will be pleased, and Nityānanda Prabhu will be pleased. This will be the perfection of our lives.

*Tāndera caraṇa sevi-bhakta-sane vāsa*  
*janame janame hoy ei abhilāṣa.*

Your humble servant,  
Subhāga Swami

## Trivikram Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You know me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I am your lowly servant who, in all these years, has yet to make any substantial contribution of assistance to you in your mission. This is not spoken in humility but is the clear reality for everyone to see. The unusual thing is that I still, somehow or other, have a hope that I might be of some real value to you someday. This hope is keeping me alive.

On the other hand, my continued attempts to assist you in a meaningful way force me relentlessly and repeatedly to face my severe limitations. Thus I am again and again obliged to see the reality of my personal disqualifications and lackluster abilities. How can such a person ever be useful to you? This stark realization brings me to the very brink of hopelessness.

Then by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy I remember you and hope floods back and washes away my self-deprecation, leaving me humbled, feeling small and insignificant, but somehow happy...and hopeful. Not a “hope against hope,” but a hope based on the most consistent, deep, and authorized philosophy, which you have so kindly presented to us at the cost of such great personal endeavor.

I don’t know what you have in store for me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I only beg that you don’t kick me away from your lotus feet. Please continue to allow me to attempt to assist you in pushing on this wonderful movement of Lord Caitanya, so that even if I don’t succeed in being of much help to you, I will at least be saving myself by this attempt.

Your insignificant servant,  
Trivikram Swami

## Umāpati Swami

**The Diamond**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda looked across his writing table at me. “This will be good for you,” he said.

I had just received my first assignment in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I was to take notes during Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lectures and then write them up in essay form. “Yes,” I said, “I know.”

It seemed like the right thing to say, but looking back, I can see that I had no idea just how good it would be. I had no idea what Kṛṣṇa consciousness was. I was only looking for a convenient way to enjoy my mind. The years passed, and I learned that no matter how much I thought I knew about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, there was always more.

And while I was wondering what would be next, Lord Kṛṣṇa came in the form of time, the destroyer, and took away my youth and my robust health.

It is said that when Lord Kṛṣṇa wants to show special mercy to a devotee, He takes everything away from him. One’s youth and health are certainly the most precious bodily advantages one has, but losing them is not the special mercy given only to devotees. It happens to everyone, but only the devotee knows that it is mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda writes:

A person in ignorance takes it as the usual course that days come, and after the days, the nights come. This is the law of material nature. But a man in ignorance does not know that when the sun rises early in the morning it begins to take away the balance of his life. Thus day after day the span of one’s life is reduced.... \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*4.27.3\]

For the devotee, getting older is a warning that this body is not eternal, a reminder that he has to prepare for death. Śrīla Prabhupāda says:

We are counting where, that day when we have to give up this body. Just like I am now seventy years old, so...I am now seventy years old. So my days are counted. So I have to give up this body. The warning is already there. So we have to prepare ourselves. Just like when going from outer station, from New York to California, if you wanted to go, you have to make your preparation, say, fortnight before, reserving the seat and making all arrangement. Similarly, we must know that we have to leave this body and we must prepare for that. Unless we don’t prepare for that, all of a sudden, if death comes, then our whole life is spoiled. \[Lecture, New York, July 25, 1966\]

The decline of the material body has given me a new appreciation for what Śrīla Prabhupāda has done. As I become more and more aware of impending death, I look over my life, and I gradually understand that Śrīla Prabhupāda gave me the chance to do the greatest thing possible with it and to give the greatest of all gifts to others.

Besides devotees, who can say he has had the greatest life possible and has done something wonderful for others? And not only has Śrīla Prabhupāda given me a perfect life, but he has also given me the chance for a perfect death. Śrīla Prabhupāda says:

Foolish people say, “You devotees are dying, and the nondevotees are also dying, so what is the difference?” There is a difference. A cat catches her kitten in its mouth, and it also catches the mouse in its mouth. Superficially we may see that the cat has caught both the mouse and the kitten in the same way. But there are differences of catching. The kitten is feeling pleasure: “Oh, my mother is carrying me.” And the mouse is feeling death: “Oh, now I’m going to die.” This is the difference. So, although both devotees and nondevotees die, there is a difference of feeling at the time of death—just like the kitten and the mouse. Don’t think that both of them are dying in the same way. The bodily process may be the same, but the mental situation is different. \[The Journey of Self-Discovery\]

Some may say that I am speaking prematurely of my death, that I have had no immediate warning, but I have only just started to understand what I should have known all along. Śrīla Prabhupāda says that we have to start preparing from the day we are born.

However you may try to keep your body fit, how long you shall keep your body fit? As soon as you are born, the death is also born along with you. Suppose one man is twenty-five years old. That means he has already died twenty-five years. So you cannot protect yourself from death, however you may become very strong, stout. \[Lecture on Teachings of Lord Caitanya, 25 September 1968\]

I did not know what I wanted or needed when I first came to Śrīla Prabhupāda. I was looking for a little mental pleasure, and by the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda I found a perfect life and the chance for a perfect death. It reminds me of the words of Dhruva Mahārāja:

But when he \[Dhruva Mahārāja\] saw God, he says, “My dear Lord, I do not want anything. Now I am fully satisfied.” Svāmin kṛtārtho ’smi varaṁ na yāce: “I came here for something which is just like broken pieces of glass, but I have got the diamond.” \[Lecture, Boston, 5 May 1969\]

I offer my respectful obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda, my spiritual master, who has given me something better than I could ever have asked for. His mercy is still beyond anything that I can understand, but maybe now I have learned a little more.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

A most insignificant servant,  
Umāpati Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most prostrated obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this auspicious day of your divine appearance, please accept my humble appreciation of your humility (dependence on Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa) in your magnanimous heart. The reason for these thoughts is naturally the feeling of pain caused by trying circumstances in your movement, but I am attempting to convert this feeling into tolerance, following in your footsteps. I share the hope of your movement’s bright future with others who are sincerely trying to uplift the spirits of some disappointed devotees and infuse them with enthusiasm, which is a serious business in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, according to you. You knew the hard facts of your disciples all along, yet you chose to be their eternal guide. They caused you so much disappointment, and even discouragement at times, while you were with us in your *vapu* form, yet your desire to carry out the mission of your Guru Mahārāja superseded such inconveniences. You even thanked them, telling them that your Guru Mahārāja had sent them to help you in your service to him. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I wonder, how should I understand with my feeble heart the vastness of the magnanimity contained in a seemingly small heart occupying your small-framed *vapu*?

It is by providence that your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.61came to my rescue today:

TRANSLATION

\[Lord Śiva prays:\] My dear Lord, You have manifold energies, and these energies are manifested in manifold forms. With such energies You have also created this cosmic manifestation, and although You maintain it as if it were permanent, You ultimately annihilate it. Although You are never disturbed by such changes and alterations, the living entities are disturbed by them, and therefore they find the cosmic manifestation to be different or separated from You. My Lord, You are always independent, and I can clearly see this fact.

PURPORT (excerpt)

Being always in distress, the conditioned soul considers the material energy to be very much disturbing. This fact is explained by a Vaiṣṇava kavi, or poet:

*kṛṣṇa bhuli’ sei jīva anādi-bahirmukha*  
*ataeva māyā tāre deya saṁsāra-duḥkha*  
*\[Cc. Madhya20.117\]*

When the living entity forgets the Supreme Lord and wants to enjoy himself independently, imitating the Supreme Lord, he is captured by the false notion that he is the enjoyer and is separated from the Supreme Lord. This material energy is therefore very much troublesome to the spiritual energy, the living entity, but the material energy is never troublesome to the Supreme Lord. Indeed, for the Supreme Lord, both material and spiritual energy are the same. In this verse Lord Śiva explains that the material energy is never troublesome to the Supreme Lord. The Supreme Lord is always independent, but because the living entities are not independent—due to their false idea of becoming independently happy—the material energy is troublesome. Consequently the material energy creates differentiation.

Because the Māyāvādī philosophers cannot understand this, they want to be relieved from the material energy. However, because a Vaiṣṇava philosopher is in full knowledge of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, he finds no disturbance even in the material energy.
