On this most auspicious day, we pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: if you so desire, kindly bless us so that we can bring all the people of the San Francisco Bay Area to your lotus feet to take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously. You are the guarantee that they will be blessed both materially and spiritually.

Falling in the dust of your lotus feet, we remain  
Your servants at ISKCON’s New Jagannātha Purī dhāma, Berkeley, California, USA.

## New Navadvīpa, Honolulu, Hawaii, USA

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.You are certainly an ocean of mercy and our dearmost friend. There is no way we can ever repay you for the gift you have freely given us, though, as you have said, we can try to repay you by strictly obeying your instructions.

*ei-bāro karuṇā koro vaiṣṇava gosāi*  
*patita-pāvana tomā bine keho nāi*

*jāhāra nikaṭe gele pāpa dūre jāy*  
*emona doyāla prabhu kebā kothā pāy*

*gaṅgāra paraśa hoile paścate pāvan*  
*darśane pavitra koro—ei tomāra guṇ*

(1) O Vaiṣṇava Gosvāmī, please be merciful to me now. There is no one except you who can purify the fallen souls.

(2) Where does anyone find such a merciful personality, by whose mere audience all sins go far away?

(3) After bathing in the waters of the sacred Ganges many times, one becomes purified, but just by the sight of you, the fallen souls are purified. This is your great power.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you sacrificed every second of your life just to benefit suffering humanity. But alas, we all lament because at times we are self-absorbed, inattentive, and very dull-headed. Nevertheless, we here in New Navadvīpa are trying to rise up to the standard you expect of us. It is a struggle, but as you said, “A diamond is not cheap.” Here in Hawaii there are many of your disciples and grand-disciples living in and outside of the temple. But in all circumstances, we cannot forget you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All your words of encouragement and chastisement result in the same mercy. You have affected our lives so much—they are changed forever. We can never forget you.

Now we are going forward with a plan to become strong in our service. We understand that we are nothing but what your mercy makes of us, and that our success therefore depends 100% on really becoming your disciples. On one of your visits here at the Hawaii *yātrā,* during *guru-pūjā* you said, “You have to enter into the meaning of *śrī-guru-vandana*.” It is a very deep instruction still, and we are praying that by obeying you in all circumstances you will kindly help us in this endeavor. Now that our faith in your instructions is growing stronger, so too is our confidence in achieving success.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please don’t kick us away. We have no good qualities; our taste for devotional life is very flickering; our thoughts are focused on impure things; we are so absorbed in material consciousness. Therefore we qualify for your merciful glance. All of us are praying to acquire sincerity and a serious attitude about taking up and pushing forward your mission. We are enthusiastically aware that if we continue in this way you will be pleased and all will be revealed.

We remain the very fallen servants of your servant,  
Your devotees in New Navadvīpa-dhāma, Honolulu, Hawaii, USA.

## New Nīlācala, Guadalajara, Mexico

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accepts our most humbles obeisances on this auspicious day, the celebration of your appearance, your Vyāsa-pūjā.

When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu met with the learned scholar Vallabha Bhaṭṭa in Jagannātha Purī, Vallabha recited various verses glorifying Lord Gaurāṅga, including this one:

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in the Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the saṅkīrtana movement.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya*** **7.1)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you have been empowered by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you have propagated His holy name everywhere. Actually, today practically everyone in the world has heard the names “Kṛṣṇa,” “Caitanya,” and also your name “Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, your beloved spiritual master, explained that unless one is directly empowered by the causeless mercy of Kṛṣṇa, one cannot become the spiritual master of the entire world, *jagad-guru.* You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a true *ācārya,* have presented Kṛṣṇa to everyone by preaching the holy name of Kṛṣṇa throughout the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your characteristics are exceptional—you are pure, sinless, faultless, spotless in your behavior, free from anger, learned in the *Vedas* and in the *bhakti-śāstras,* beginning with *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and you destroy all the doubts in your followers and fill them with faith.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is because of your potency that ISKCON is living, this greatest spiritual society in the world. We are very grateful to you for this matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

All glories to your great *saṅkīrtana* movement.

We beg to remain,  
Your humble servants at New Nīlācala Mandir, Guadalajara, Mexico.  
(written by Premānanda dāsa)

## New Pānihāṭi, Atlanta, Georgia, USA

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on this most glories day of your divine appearance in this world, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

Your followers here at New Pānihāṭi-dhāma are ever grateful to you for giving us the opportunity to serve you by preserving the memory of your visit here in 1975. As you were giving your arrival address in the temple (your usual practice), you began to describe the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Nityānanda Prabhu. Suddenly you were overcome with ecstatic emotions and you had to compose yourself. At this point you tried to continue by stressing how Lord Kṛṣṇa demands that we first of all surrender, but as you mentioned the name of Lord Caitanya and said how He makes no demands, you were again overwhelmed with emotion and could not continue. So you asked us to “take shelter of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and be happy.”

This was probably the shortest arrival address in the history of the movement. Even to this day, whenever we think of this event we feel extreme joy, satisfaction, and pride in realizing that it somehow took place here at New Pānihāṭi-dhāma. We will be forever grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bestowing this blessing and responsibility upon us. I say responsibility because such an event is a rare occurrence. Generally advanced devotees such as yourself do not express deep spiritual emotions in public. So it must have been something truly wonderful that caused you to display such transcendental feelings openly before your astonished disciples. Your tears have been enshrined in the hearts of all the devotees who were fortunate enough to be here in 1975, and we have taken a vow to enshrine those tears, by way of continued discussions and remembrances, for all future generations. By your tears you have made this place a holy *dhāma* worthy of our most valiant efforts to maintain and expand its influence.

“The test of your love for me will be in how well you cooperate.” This statement by Your Divine Grace is the key to unlimited potency in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have to always consider our programs in light of this statement. The mission to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness is an awesome responsibility, as well as a great and distinguished honor bestowed upon only the most fortunate souls. We have been blessed by you with such an honor here in Atlanta, and we pray to you for the strength and courage that will enable us to continue and increase our cooperative efforts in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, consider our fallen condition and give us your special blessings, because we sincerely want to assist you in your service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Just as when you first came to America you prayed to Lord Kṛṣṇa to give the people of this land the ability to understand your message, so we need your special favor to continue developing your project here at New Pānihāṭi-dhāma. Without your mercy and your blessings, we cannot possibly achieve such an exalted goal. Such activities as preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness are generally performed by the most advanced souls, who have pure devotion to guru and Kṛṣṇa, not by persons such as ourselves, who where born in the darkest ignorance of the Kali-yuga. But we are confident that with your blessings we can do the impossible, which is to give all souls the opportunity to hear about Kṛṣṇa.

So on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we reaffirm our commitment to your lotus feet and to fulfilling your vision of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone. And we pray that we may work cooperatively with your sincere followers all over the world in realizing your vision of a truly Kṛṣṇa conscious society.

Your humble servants at ISKCON’s New Pānihāṭi-dhāma, in Atlanta, Georgia, USA.

## New Ramaṇ Reti, Alachua, Florida, USA

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, our eternal master, father, and friend!

Here it is, February 28, 2001, the deadline for submitting our offerings to be included in your Vyāsa-pūjā book for this year, and I’m still trying to finish this offering on behalf of the devotees here in Alachua. It seems, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that there is always so much to do, and just not enough time to do it. Everyone says this, no matter what their situation is in life. Everyone is so busy with their day-to-day activities that many people cannot find the time to do the important things in life. You have shown us what the most important things in life are: to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and to engage in devotional service. But too often we get caught up with our daily activities, and these most important things are minimized, or sometimes forgotten.

Thirty years ago I wrote you a letter saying that because there was so much work to do at ISKCON Press in New York, many times it was very difficult for me to attend the full morning program and to chant my rounds in the morning. You replied that it was OK to work first and chant later, but that without fail I had to complete sixteen rounds daily. Why do I remember that you said it was OK to work first and chant later, but I forget that you stressed that I had to complete sixteen rounds every day?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we all want to thank you. We want to thank you for all that you have given us. You have given us everything, but most of all you have given us eternal spiritual life, something that we are all searching for, whether we know it or not, yet something that, somehow or other, seems to evade the vast majority of people in this world.

Here in your Alachua New Ramaṇ Reti community, we are trying to serve you in our daily lives. We are trying to be good examples, true examples, for our friends and families and for others we come in contact with. We want to share with others that which you have so kindly given us. So many of us here have been full-time members of your ISKCON Society for many years. Collectively, the members of your community here have offered thousands of years of service to Your Divine Grace and, under your guidance, to the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. We pray that we may be able to offer thousands of years more of devotional service to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it has been twenty-four years since you departed from our vision. That is almost a quarter century. The time you have not been physically present is twice as long as the time you were physically present with us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, that twenty-four years seems like more than a lifetime. While you were present with us, most of us thought those days would never end. Even though we gradually began to understand that those days would end, your departure was a shock to us all. I think that to this day, we are all still in shock. I regret so much that the devotees who were present at that time did not have the opportunity to collectively take the time, as much time as necessary, to reflect, contemplate, understand, and appreciate who you really are and what you have done for us and to understand the far-reaching impact of what life would be like without you physically present with us. Perhaps it’s not too late. Perhaps we can all step back from our present positions, situations, and understandings to collectively reflect on our relationships with you and to adjust our lives appropriately, according to the realizations we would gain through such introspection.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, during your final days with us you mentioned that only half your mission was complete. You mentioned that the unfinished half of your mission was establishing *varṇāśrama-dharma*. Despite what others may say about the members of the Alachua community, or even what we may say about ourselves, deep in our hearts we are trying, albeit feebly, to establish ourselves in this most perfect *varṇāśrama* system and to show this as an example for others to follow. Yes, some of us are struggling with the daily chanting of our sixteen rounds, or with following all four regulative principles, but we *are* struggling. I don’t know anyone here who is satisfied with their present level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Everyone knows they could and should improve. No one is satisfied with their shortcomings. Everyone knows they have a greater responsibility to you to follow those instructions you’ve given us and that you will give us the ability to make the grade.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, even though we are so unqualified and even though we were so scared, Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa have come to Alachua. We know the reason They have come to is not because of our qualifications or even our desires, but They have come to us because you have asked Them to come, to accept our service and to purify us. You used to say that Deity worship is for the householders and that by engaging in Deity worship we would become purified. We see that our lives have changed since the arrival of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara. So often, after a big festival, life “goes back to normal.” However, after the arrival of Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, our lives have changed. Our lives will never be the same. We are so very grateful for all you have taught us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and for the feelings you have instilled in us to worship Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and for the opportunity you have given us to render service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, so often we say, “If it weren’t for Śrīla Prabhupāda, who knows where I’d be today.” We are all living proof of your mercy. You have given us more than we can ever repay you for, no matter how much service we do, no matter how much money we contribute, and no matter how Kṛṣṇa conscious we become. We have an eternal debt to repay you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And, even though we can never repay that debt, we should always keep trying. We can’t declare bankruptcy with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We can’t erase that debt. That debt always remains. It’s not like a material debt, where we may be so deep in debt that we think there’s no hope of ever paying it off so we give up trying. We must never give up hope of being able to repay you, and we must never stop trying.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you used to give us the example that one stick standing alone can be broken very easily but a bundle of sticks wrapped together become so strong that they cannot be broken. This is what we are trying to do in Alachua. We are trying to group together to become strong in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and strong in our devotion and dedication to you. There are so many distractions to deter us in our dedication to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It seems that those distractions can come with either material or spiritual coverings. We pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we will not be deterred from our service to you and that our commitment to you will not be compromised. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are all we have. If it were not for you, in most of our cases, we would know nothing of the higher values of spiritual life. You have truly opened our eyes with the shining lamp of knowledge while we were standing in the darkness of ignorance. We pray that our eyes always remain open and that we will not lose sight of the goal of our lives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please always remind us of our most important work, to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. It is work. You told me once in Los Angeles, “Nothing good comes without a struggle.” So how can we ever think that we’re just going to coast along and that along the way we’re going to become Kṛṣṇa conscious? We’ve got to endeavor for this most valuable treasure. Please always help us and guide us so that we do no stray from this path. And, if we do stray from the path, please always show us the way back, so that we will always remain engaged in your service and in the association of your followers.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have been so kind to all of us and to all the people of the world by establishing the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and all the positive influence that comes along with it. You have established your temples for us to come together and to become enlivened and encouraged in devotional service. By your causeless mercy, not by our desires for retirement, so many of your devotees have gathered here in Alachua, Florida, “such a remote part of the world, so far from the birthplace of Lord Caitanya,” as you have said about this area. We have come here to become enlivened in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to recharge our spiritual batteries, and to have the association of one another. None of us are getting any younger. Time is passing us by. Most of us didn’t realize this when we were younger, no matter how many times you told us. But now as we’re approaching fifty years old or having recently passed that age, we are understanding that death is approaching and that whatever time we have left, no matter how little or how much, we must use for devotional service, in some way or other, and for becoming truly Kṛṣṇa conscious. In the past two years we have seen some of those we have grown up with in Kṛṣṇa consciousness leave their bodies. It has been a stark realization for us all—a realization that it’s going to happen to all of us one day and that when that day comes we had better be prepared for that ultimate test by preparing while we have the chance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was requested by our temple president here in Alachua to write this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā offering on behalf of all the devotees here. I was very honored by this privilege to write on behalf of so many devotees who have served you for so many years. But only after beginning to write did I realize how difficult it is for me to represent the many deep and different realizations of you and feelings for you that your servants here in Alachua have. We are all so different, but we are simultaneously so similar. You have brought us all together, Śrīla Prabhupāda, from so many different cultures (or lack thereof) and backgrounds and you have united us in your mission of serving Lord Kṛṣṇa and sharing this knowledge with others. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have created a worldwide family of devotees that we have all become a part of. We all pray that we will become and remain your loyal and devoted sons and daughters, grandsons and granddaughters. We pray that we will become worthy of being called your followers and that we will always be recipients of your causeless mercy. And we pray that you will instill in us the ability to appreciate one another for who we are and what we are striving to become, and that we may all work together to serve and please you eternally.

Your servants in New Ramaṇ Reti, ISKCON’s rural community in Alachua, Florida, USA.  
(written by Kīrtirāja dāsa)

## New Remuṇā, Toronto, Canada

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Gurudeva, the savior of the most fallen!!

You are a pure lover of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and a true representative of the desires of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Having also appeared within the vision of the residents of this earth planet for the real well-being of all without restrictions, your influence knows no limitations. Surely this is of the nature of causeless mercy.

In you we find endless waves of all-good qualities that ever adorn the infallible, those who are residents of the spiritual plane of perfect enlightenment, the spiritual world. In your time here we find the ever-fresh dynamic wonder that constitutes a divine life in contact with this material world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, by the love that you bear for the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you have inspired, and continue to inspire, countless souls on the truly beneficial platform: a transcendental platform, above self-centered considerations of material exploits, both gross and subtle.

You illuminate the true path of life for the soul, for all souls. And what is that life? The soul’s life ever moves in a deep joyous celebration of intimate service connection with the name, form, qualities, associates, pastimes, abodes, and paraphernalia of the divine couple, Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all who would see, you have come to show the life that lies beyond our petty struggle for existence here in the world of weal and woe—in other words, spiritual life—and in keeping with the immeasurable magnanimity of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you freely bestow the highest conception in pure love of Godhead, as did your beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and as did all the previous spiritual masters in our Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya, in a verifiably authorized way in full accord with scripture and all saintly persons (the *mahājanas*).

Though now we rise and fall among the insurmountable waves of the ocean of success and failure while depending on our own imperfect attempts (even—and especially—while ostensibly engaged in your service), still, by example and inspiration (through thoughts, word, and deeds), you kindly intercede to revoke our exploitive tendency and invoke our innate dormant loving propensity and thus rescue us from drowning; indeed, without our even asking, you reduce that vast, tumultuous ocean to the bit of the water contained in a calf’s hoofprint so that we can easily cross over all such problems and thus reach the land of ultimate success, the land of pure devotional service. In this way you lead us from a dreary dream full of varieties of miseries to a waking state full of ever-fresh and wonderful varieties of true loving exchanges. All hail to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Though by your divine grace we have experienced, and continue to experience, many remarkable successes in the progressive advancement of spiritual life, still, it is often painfully clear just how far we fall short of the goal of fulfilling your desires here in New Remuṇā-dhāma. We submit ourselves at the lotus feet of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Kṣīracora-Gopīnātha with the hope of associating with the pure desires of Their beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda in our heart of hearts.

You left so many books of mature realization about the *siddhānta,* or perfectional conclusion, regarding life’s ultimate goal and how to realize it, even in this life—books that are just appropriate for the upliftment of we Kali-yugans. Out of your great mercy you took up the task of widely publishing and distributing these sublime instructions. You continue to guide all to the fulfillment of such lofty realization in unlimited ways.

Let our prayer be this: may we ever seek to more fully understand how to properly serve you here in Toronto, Śrīla Prabhupāda; to somehow assist in the fulfillment of your mission and instructions to the world in general and in harmony with the Vaiṣṇava community at large; to ever expand your glories in a way that shall please your dearmost Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we whom you have accepted out of causeless loving kindness, we who approach you and huddle expectantly in your shelter, we beg to submit our unstinted desire to reaffirm our commitment to you: You are our Vyāsadeva!! Let us hear well.

Your servants at ISKCON’s New Remuṇā-dhāma, in Toronto, Canada.

## New Seva-kuñja-dhāma, Chicago, Illinois, USA

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The following words from one of the most elevated conversations of all time aptly describe the experience of our connection with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Lord Caitanya then inquired \[from Rāmānanda Rāya\]: “What is the most profitable thing in the world, the essence of all auspicious events?” Rāmānanda Rāya replied that there is nothing as profitable as the association of pure devotees....

When Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya what is considered to be the most painful existence, Rāmānanda Rāya replied that separation from a pure devotee constitutes the most painful existence....In the Fifth Canto of *Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta* (5.44) it is stated that the association of a pure devotee is more desirable than life itself and that in separation from him one cannot even pass a second happily. \[*Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* Chapter 32\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your association has given us what nearly everyone and everything here in the material world has promised yet failed to deliver—happiness. Even at these very early stages in devotional life, we are appreciating a profound sense of spiritual happiness. To paraphrase some of your own words, true happiness can be found only on the spiritual platform. This happiness is love. Love is enjoyment, or the real meaning of life, and enjoyment is service rendered devotionally to the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

You have brought to us the pure, unbroken understanding that devotional service is the only path by which one can achieve the Supreme Person. And you have single-handedly made practical devotional service readily available to everyone. Under your most expert guidance, anyone who follows your directions can immediately become engaged in the conclusion of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. That conclusion, which is the essence of all spiritual understanding, is to surrender unto the Supreme Lord. One can do this simply by participating in your mission. Whoever does so will remain continuously in your association and will never have to endure the most painful existence of separation from you. You have written in *The Science of Self-Realization* that it is the business of the guru to see that no human being suffers in the material world. We are certain that your association ends all suffering. Now we must convince the rest of the world.

Here at ISKCON Chicago, Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī Mandir, you have arranged for us an atmosphere that is very conducive for turning our initial faith in the science of devotion into *niṣṭhā,* or steadiness. Just as the calm yet unrelenting repetition of ocean waves lures one to the shore, here the conchshells resound, the bells ring, the curtains close and open, and the *kīrtanas* start up with a regularity that similarly persists in encouraging us to become steady, or “fixed-up.” Why do we resist? “Fixed-up” means happiness, and that is all that you are concerned with and requesting from us—that we be happy.

The material body is often considered to be just like a temple; so this temple building is much like a body itself in that it is a warm, familiar place to be. At the core, Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā are enshrined here just like the Paramātmā within the heart of the body, awaiting our worship and love. While we daily perform our service we are constantly reminded that Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the speaker of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* stands before us. He also sits within our hearts, patiently waiting for us to take up the message He has given us through Your Divine Grace.

You are His most glorious representative. Your life is the essence of all auspicious events. And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the most profitable association for the entire world. May we have that divine association of yours life after life. We can think of no greater boon.

Your menial servants at ISKCON’s New Seva-kuñja-dhāma, home of Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī, in Chicago, Illinois, USA.

## New Śāntipura, Khasadeśa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your excellent disciples!

Just as Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda carried out the will of his Lord, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and just as Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura carried out the will of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, similarly you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have continued to carry out the will of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda by spreading the message of love for Kṛṣṇa to the Western world. And now, after years of hard effort by your excellent representatives, Kṛṣṇa consciousness has taken root in this remote capital of the East—Śāntipura. This is like the morning sun of the new century. It shines upon thousands and millions of poor beings and brings new hope into their lives.

Five thousand years ago, for the utmost welfare of all human beings, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared personally in order to establish the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* movement. This is the only medicine that can save people from all the miseries of this Age of Kali. It can dissipate the darkness of passion and ignorance in the hearts of all living entities. Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, out of limitless compassion you have spread this *saṅkīrtana* movement to every corner of this world so that we can all benefit from it. With the blessings of Kṛṣṇa and guru and all the deeply concerned Vaiṣṇavas, the devotees in Śāntipura are all growing prosperously in devotional life. Receiving the special mercy from performing the *saṅkīrtana-yajña*, the devotees will surely worship and serve the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, more blissfully and distribute this special mercy more widely and with more enthusiasm.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the sage among the sages. You are the life and soul of our Śāntipura *yātrā*. We beg you to bestow upon us eternal shelter and blessings. We cannot bear the idea of being separated from you even for one moment, our dear grandfather. Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your grandchildren at New Śāntipura, Khasadeśa.

## New Tālavana, Mulberry, Tennessee, USA

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this 105th anniversary of your divine appearance, we humbly thank you for the ministry you spread around the world. By its mercy we have been delivered from material existence and given the opportunity to engage in *bhakti-yoga*. On the strength of our *sampradāya,* we stand tall and straight and sing the same divine message you so graciously spoke to us. By its potency all men, women, and children can be relieved of *māyā* and revive their blissful life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Oh how we long to please you despite the faults and troubles that weigh us down and attempt to drown us. Yet we know, by your divine grace, that Śrī Kṛṣṇa will help us overcome these obstacles and empower us to continue singing your song divine.

What a beautiful song it is, composed of the ecstatic expressions of your vivid realizations of the Absolute Truth, contemporary society, and life in general. Oh, it was you who taught the song of God to all, and it was you who so mercifully gave your life to teach all people these facts of life.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, based on our connection with you, we are continuing the *līlā* the Lord so mercifully engaged you in. As you delivered us, may we deliver our fellow man from the darkness that surrounds them. As you inspired us to preach on your strength, may we gain the purity to continue your inspiration and see your work further unfold the mission of Lord Gaurāṅga.

As the Ganges inundates West Bengal, may your love inundate our hearts so that our preaching will teach only the truth, the same truth that anyone can hear from you. May the duality that attempts to divide us find it increasingly difficult to separate us from the core of your teachings. Then may we sing Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s song strong and true for your satisfaction.

Please, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, do not let us fall away. Make us stay and become more seasoned in the drama of your mission. This mission is our heart, and may you lead us in carrying it on. We need it, the world needs it. We pray that you will empower us to sacrifice our lives for your goals and pleasure.

May we build up the soil of immortality and grow strong and tall like you, may we spring forth more and more branches for humanity to climb home, back to Godhead. By Lord Kṛṣṇa’s grace may we please you with success in the preaching field, through the proper cultivation of the seeds you planted so long ago.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for engaging us eternally in the divine service of Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga. May we never, ever fall down from your lotus feet, and may we always hear your command to go out and preach to every man, women, and child.

Sincerely,  
Your loving servants at ISKCON’s New Tālavana rural community, in Mulberry, Tennessee, USA.

## New Uddhava-kuṇḍa, Khasadeśa

Dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept our humble obeisances! All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi!

Dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your Vyāsa-pūjā day, and devotees from all over the world are singing and dancing to celebrate this grand day and offer you their endless yearnings and devotion. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Although we devotees in Khasadeśa are living in a special environment, we can still feel the excitement and bliss of such a wonderful event. We know that the gospel brought to us by Kṛṣṇa consciousness can save us from this miserable material ocean and allow us to taste nectarean spiritual bliss. It is because of the mercy emanating from you, through your books, our gurus, and other devotees, that we can become your grandchildren.

Due to your causeless mercy, now our greatest desire is to get the association of devotees and live with them; we feel our hearts purified when we approach the noble swanlike sages; we want to hear the transcendental knowledge that scatters away the ignorance accumulated in so many lives and that enables us to gradually taste the sweetness of devotional service and to realize the eternal position of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, that cowherd boy who performed pastimes in the forest of Vṛndāvana. Now we know that the Absolute Truth is Lord Kṛṣṇa, that He is the lover of all the *gopīs* and the enjoyer of the residents of Vraja, and that He spoke the *Bhagavad-gītā* to Arjuna on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra to rid him of his material confusion, to impart to him the confidential knowledge of devotional service, and to give spiritual guidance to people in the Age of Kali. All this knowledge that we know possess is due to your great mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a great devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is you who brought this transcendental knowledge about Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West, where materialism and nihilism were prevalent. It is you who wrote your wonderful purports in the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* so that we can understand this perfect knowledge easily through your pure devotion to your guru and Lord Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of your disciples, we devotees in Khasadeśa have become so fortunate to receive this transcendental information and have the opportunity to take shelter at your pure lotus feet.

It is our real obligation to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every corner of this country, to all its towns and villages, so that this transcendental knowledge will inspire the many souls still suffering here to blissfully chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu told Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī, “\[My spiritual master said to Me,\] ‘You are a fool. You are not qualified to study Vedānta philosophy, and therefore You must always chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. This is the essence of all mantras, or Vedic hymns.’” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 7.72) Lord Caitanya taught us how to taste spiritual bliss and perfection through chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy name only. He is the incarnation of mercy, who bestowed Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everybody by teaching us to chant the holy name of the Lord. To whomever he met He would say, “Please chant the holy name! Please chant!” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the mercy incarnation of Lord Caitanya, and we are all the fortunate recipients of your mercy, dear grandfather. Without your mercy we would not be here glorifying you.

We offer our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

The servants of your servants at Uddhava-kuṇḍa, Khasadeśa.

## New Varṣāṇā, Auckland, New Zealand

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

In approaching you on this day of your most divine appearance, I beg the mercy of my own spiritual master, that I might glorify you, the world *ācārya,* the *patita-pāvana,* savior of the most fallen.

You gave your life for us, giving us your perfect example to follow so that we might have true guidance in our search to reestablish our eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa.

Although I never met you personally, I know that you are present throughout your society, ISKCON. I recently had the benediction and great fortune to take part in your transcendental book marathon. I found that whenever books are being distributed, so many things are accomplished at the same time: Kṛṣṇa is glorified, His holy names are chanted, and the conditioned souls are given the opportunity to engage in devotional service. And when the conditioned souls engage in devotional service, they are saved from the hellish life in repeated births and deaths. Also, Lakṣmī is liberated, and temples, preaching, and devotees are maintained. In addition, the whole disciple succession is pleased, the devotees who are distributing become purified and make rapid advancement, and all the devotees become enlivened and shower their blessings. I can see why you said, “If you want to please me, then distribute my books.”

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! When times get tough, as they always seem to do, I know that I can approach you and bow down before you. Seeking your presence in my heart, I almost feel your hand upon my head and your deep kind voice saying, “That’s all right.” I feel so wretched but so fortunate.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, may you always be manifested in my heart. May I always serve you and distribute your books.

Your servants at New Varṣāṇā, Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Gaura-Rāya dāsa)

## New Vraja-dhāma, Hungary

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most blessed day of your divine appearance!

In 1965 you set out to the West to fulfill the desire of your Guru Mahārāja. You did not know first-hand the country you were heading to, but you were aware that due to the godless habits of its barbarian population, the life of the living entities there could be compared to only hell. You were not even sure you would be able to find food suitable to maintain your body. It is said that when the King of Jaipur visited London some two hundred years ago, he carried water with him in huge silver pots because he was not willing to accept even water from such a place.

What you found upon your arrival in the USA was what you expected. The cathedrals of Christianity, timber-lined with the wood of religious sentiments and fear of the world beyond, could not abide the test of time. In the throbbing bluster of questions left unanswered due to lack of knowledge of the Absolute, the decaying pillars of Christian ethics collapsed. On these ruins only voidism and nihilism were dancing madly in order to impregnate with the spirit of Freudianism and Darwinism such demons who, by the middle of the last century, had made the whole world into a slaughterhouse where living entities were no longer secure even in their mothers’ wombs.

On seeing this situation, you asked, “How will these people, deluded by the modes of passion and ignorance, be able to understand the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa?” Due to your unlimited compassion and mercy, you were empowered by the Lord to work wonders in this accursed place and save the hopeless souls.

By the touch of your lotus feet you sanctified that land and, by chanting the holy names of Lord Kṛṣṇa, you purified the atmosphere. You created a new world suitable for living a life dedicated to the service of God for those who accepted the message of Vāsudeva in their hearts by taking shelter of your instructions.

But you were not satisfied just to spread a devotional religion or to enlighten people with a personal philosophy. You started a cultural revolution, through which you wanted to fundamentally change the world. The appearance of Lord Caitanya had brought about the beginning of a golden age in Kali-yuga, and you came to give the law books for the countless generations for the next ten thousand years.

In contrast to the two-legged animals and professional exploiters who populate modern so-called civilization, you defined for us the outlines of a society the basic principle of which is the service of the servants of the devoted servants of the Lord. The divine essence of this social system—*daiva-varṇāśrama*—is to educate its members on how to use their God-given abilities in the service of Kṛṣṇa so that they can develop their selfless love for Him. You wanted to manifest a society the members of which live in perfect harmony, respecting one another and understanding that their prime duty is to help one another become purified and attain perfect Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you kindly left us the task to make this spiritual culture a reality. Countless Vaiṣṇavas are trying to understand the relevant principles and their practical application. There are some who do not understand what you said. There are some who understand but are skeptical about implementing it. Since they themselves are not applying the elements of Vaiṣṇava culture in their lives, they try to adapt Western models in the field of Kṛṣṇa conscious social development.

Here in New Vraja-dhāma we would like to assist those sincere followers of yours who have understood what you wanted to introduce and are determined to do everything possible to make it a reality on the level of the whole society. Please mercifully help us to become purified from the conception of I and mine. By your mercy, may our society be free from self-interest, which is the main impediment in fulfilling your divine desire, and may we always keep Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in the center of our lives.

May we witness your divine plans for rescuing the suffering souls gradually unfold here in New Vraja-dhāma and all over the world.

Your servants in New Vraja-dhāma, Hungary.  
(written by Gaura-śakti dāsa)

## New Vraja-dhāma, Khasadeśa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet! All glories to your auspicious appearance day!

Most of us came to know you by reading your books published in our language while we were still trapped by *nirvāṇa, śūnyavāda,* or atheism, but we thought you were the same as the so-called saints from ordinary groups. We did not know the importance of your appearance in this material world until we met your disciples, our future gurus, whom you had sent especially to our country for the people here.

We are grateful to you for sending them here. Through them we have begun to understand you and your books; through them we have begun to understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness; through them we have begun to understand the real meaning of Truth and God; and through them we have begun to know the true way of serving the Lord and His devotees.

We are grateful that you set up ISKCON as a great temple for people all over the world. Now, due to your causeless mercy, we have met our beloved gurus and can have the sweet and pure association of *sādhus* and pure devotees in the place where we live. And by their association we can see you with our own eyes in the pure disciplic succession. You are the savior sent directly by Lord Kṛṣṇa for us, a group of fallen souls. ISKCON is the spiritual property and shelter left by you for us. To serve ISKCON is to serve you, and to follow the guru from ISKCON is to follow your instructions. We have full faith in ISKCON and our gurus, even though there may be discordant and jarring sounds outside. We know that by pleasing our guru we can please you.

Thank you again and again for your causeless mercy.

Your servants in New Vraja, Khasadeśa.  
(written by Yaśodā Kumāra dāsa)

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*jāhāra nikaṭe gele pāpa dūre jāy*  
*emona doyāla prabhu kebā kothā pāy*

Where does anyone find such a merciful personality by whose mere audience all sins go far away?

*gaṅgāra paraśa hoile paścate pāvan*  
*darśane pavitra koro—ei tomāra guṇ*

**After bathing in the waters of the sacred Ganges many times, one becomes purified, but just by the sight of you, the fallen souls are purified. This is your great power.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In 1973 I had the very good fortune to meet one of your devoted disciples at a concert. Śrīla Prabhupāda, he was distributing your books. I was wandering around the field when I came upon this seeming Socratic sage seated in a wheelchair, with incense smoke billowing up around him. He had a pleasant smile and a shaved head and was wearing saffron robes, with his forehead marked with the symbol of a Vaiṣṇava. I sat next to him for a few minutes, sensing I was now with some modern day wise man, a saintly messenger. He gave me a magazine and I glanced at it: *Back to Godhead* was the name on the cover. I opened to the first page and noticed a full-page photo of Your Divine Grace, but I had no idea who you were, although I had been studying your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* since 1972. Being inquisitive, I asked the sage, “Who is this person in the photo?”

The *sādhu* answered, “That’s Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

I asked, “Who is Śrīla Prabhupāda?”

The devotee answered, “Śrīla Prabhupāda is our father; everyone loves Śrīla Prabhupāda.” Words of wisdom from a saintly surrendered servant, your sincere disciple, Śrīla Prabhupāda. That disciple loved you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and certainly Your Divine Grace is the *ācārya* and spiritual father for everyone who displays such love for you by rendering similar favorable devotional service at your lotus feet.

In 1999 a simple and humble old Indian gentleman who regularly visits Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira was telling me of the unfortunate plight of a local leader of a small Hindu center in Queens, New York. He told me how the swami was ill-advised into a surgery that cost him the use of his legs. He said this swami was a great leader of many Hindus in Queens and was highly educated, a big scholar in Vedānta. As the old gentleman proclaimed the man’s glories, he pointed toward a large painting of Your Divine Grace hanging in the lobby of the *mandira* and said, “The leader in Queens was a big Ph.D., a big scholar just like your Swamiji.” At that I took issue with him and told him that “Śrīla Prabhupāda never received a Ph.D. from a mundane university, but he had the greatest and most important Ph.D..” I told him, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you had “a unique Ph.D. in love of God,” *kṛṣṇa-prema,* a Ph.D. that no one else presently has, and therefore Your Divine Grace was the most qualified to teach others how to love Kṛṣṇa. Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Your Divine Grace is certainly the *ācārya* of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* the true Doctor of Love who can prescribe the cure of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* to end our disease of *avidyā*. With your own wise words you shine the light to guide us, your insignificant students, on the path back home, back to Godhead:

So to understand this knowledge one has to approach *ācārya*. *Ācārya* means one who knows the purpose of Vedic literature, *śāstra*. *Āśṛṇoti yaḥ śāstram*. And practices and teaches his disciple. He knows. *Ācārya* means knows. One who knows the purports of Vedic literature, he practices in his life, and he teaches his disciple. He is called *ācārya*. So *ācārya upāsanam*. Before worshiping the Lord...Just like here Kṛṣṇa said in the beginning, *ācāryopāsanam,* and in the middle he says, *mayi cānanya-yogena bhaktir avyabhicāriṇī*. So before engaging yourself in devotional service to the Lord, you must first of all worship *ācārya, ācāryopāsanam*. One cannot become a devotee personally. Just like some rascals say, “What is the use of accepting guru?” Of course, they have got very bad experience.

But who is *ācārya*? *Ācārya* means one who has received the Vedic knowledge through the *paramparā* system. Evaṁ *paramparā-prāptam imaṁ rājarṣayo viduḥ*. He is *ācārya*. *Ācārya* cannot be manufactured, self-made. No. He must come down from the disciplic succession. He is *ācārya*. You have to approach such *ācārya* who is coming in disciplic succession. *Ācāryavān puruṣo veda*. One who is under the shelter of *ācārya,* he knows things. So Kṛṣṇa recommends also here that if you actually want to become in knowledge, if you want to have real knowledge, then you must worship *ācārya*.

Therefore we worship Your Divine Grace today and every other day. We worship our eternal father, the father who everyone loves. Possessing the most unique Ph.D. in Love of Kṛṣṇa, an honorary degree received an eternity ago in Goloka Vṛndāvana. You are the Founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, the preeminent *śikṣā-guru,* and the Servant God-*ācārya*. On this most blessed occasion we fall prostrate at your divine lotus feet and feel unfortunate, rotten, and insignificant.

*Hā hā rūpa koro doyā, deho more pada-chāyā:* “Please give me your mercy and the shade of your lotus feet.”

We beg your forgiveness for our past mistakes, and we beg for your loving mercy. Please situate us firmly on the transcendental platform of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*; please don’t kick us away! We pray to remain your menial servants forever.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your servants in ISKCON’s Rādhā-Govinda Mandira, Brooklyn, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## New York (Manhattan)

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet! All glories to Your Divine Grace and to your life and soul, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness!

Thank you for bringing the treasure of pure unalloyed love for Śrī Kṛṣṇa to this most materialistic place, Manhattan Island.

Just the other day I had to spend time with a mother of one of your new followers. She asked for a meeting, and her voice on the phone sounded concerned. She came to realize that her son wasn’t going to quit living the life of a devotee in the near future.

We talked for about two hours and discussed many topics in a very frank and open manner, and the mood was respectful and pleasant. Then I went back to the temple.

Suddenly, on the way to the subway, it hit me!

“Sour-grapes philosophy! This is a perfect example of sour-grapes philosophy!”

After having looked everywhere in the material world for real happiness without any success, the conditioned soul in his or her exhausted state comes to the conclusion: “There ain’t no happiness in this place, and anybody who dares to differ is insane.”

Well, as long as this unfortunate soul stays in the association of similar conditioned souls, this type of philosophy can stand and help to ease the pain, but as soon as the first blissful devotee is in sight, as in the case of our new bhakta and his mother, the agony and frustration start all over again.

So actually the difficulty the mother experienced by seeing her son dedicating his life to devotional service to Śrī Kṛṣṇa was actually subconscious envy of his good fortune.

By now I had almost reached the entrance to the subway, when it hit me again, this time even harder:

I thought, “How merciful you are, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to have come to the West and out of pure compassion taken all the living entities by their hand, like a loving father, and not only freed them from all suffering and pain but introduced them to your most beloved sweet Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and His pure unalloyed devotional service!”

We didn’t ask you to come. As a matter of fact, religion and God was the last thing on our minds. We had convinced ourselves that we were happy and that nobody could ever realize God. I’m very scared to think of what would have been our destiny if you hadn’t come.

Thank you for this wonderful gift, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and please bless us here at 26 Second Avenue so that we may gain an ever-new appreciation of this gift and assist you in making this incomparable gift available to all living entities.

We are very much inspired to have the regular association of your dear disciple His Grace Dayānanda Prabhu and his good wife, Her Grace Nandi Mukhī Mātājī, who are presiding over our gatherings on Tuesdays and Fridays like loving parents. Their deep love for you transforms this storefront into a genuine *mahā-tīrtha* again and again.

All of us who visit this temple regularly want this love, because we had enough sour-grapes philosophy in who-knows-how-many lifetimes, and we are determined to hold on to this opportunity.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, please consider us your most dependent children, who are waiting for your guiding hand to lead us out of the darkness of envy and selfishness onto the path of transcendental unalloyed devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Govinda.

All the devotees and friends of your Matchless Gifts at 26 Second Avenue, Manhattan, New York, USA.  
(written by Yajña-puruṣa dāsa)

## Nueva Goloka Vṛndāvana, Costa Rica

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

In your books, as well as in your recorded classes, in our disciples, and in the many other things you left for us in your big house, we can find whatever we need. You constructed such an estate for mankind with your spiritual guidance! Any place in the world where an ISKCON temple is present, the war against *māyā* you began is successfully continuing.

All over the world, many human beings are becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious through reading your books or seeing your videotapes or relishing Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, as your humble servants, we deeply thank you, a direct representative of Lord Caitanya and Lord Kṛṣṇa. Please help us become unconditional servants of your servants. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive us for our offenses against you, and help us stop making new ones, so that we may become pure disciples. Please help us carry out your instructions. To avoid chaos in our *yātrā,* please help us follow your prescription to read and hear *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the association of devotees. Otherwise we will turn into *gṛhamedhis,* infested with so much envious faultfinding.

You have given us a matchless gift, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and we thank you from the bottom of our hearts, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants in Nueva Goloka Vṛndāvana, Costa Rica.

## Osaka, Japan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.All glories to Your Divine Grace! Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It is only due to your mercy in the form of your transcendental books that preaching activities are still going on in Japan, which is the one of the most powerful fortresses of Māyā. Māyā might be very strong, but Lord Kṛṣṇa is the most powerful. I am sure of that because my Guru Mahārāja’s strong faith in Your Divine Grace comes to me through Guru Mahārāja’s words. Even when we forget Lord Kṛṣṇa, He never forgets whatever little service we have performed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please kindly hear offerings from several congregational members in Osaka.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books in English are translated into Japanese, and through your books now I am connected to Lord Kṛṣṇa. At the moment people might think I am in trouble because I used to own some business and I have lost it. But actually I am as happy as an innocent child because I am chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Absolute Truth—this all the spirit souls should remember all the time. This have you shown me by your personal example.—Bhakta Hatanaka

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have kindly showed me how I can practice *bhakti-yoga,* which is easier than *haṭha-yoga, kuṇḍalinī-yoga,* or *rāja-yoga*. You have kindly helped me get myself established in my spiritual life. Thank you very much.—Bhakta Araki

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have spread the teachings of Lord Caitanya, which are meant for the Age of Kali. Now the teachings have reached even Japan, crossing the ocean. Without you I would have ended my life unfortunately. I can hear your words: “Preach the teachings of Lord Caitanya throughout Japan.” I hope your teachings will keep spreading all over Japan without the slightest deviation.—Bhakta Azuma.

We can glorify you when all devotees cooperate with one another. No matter how Kali-yuga might advance, your glories will spread all over the three worlds.

Your servants in Osaka, Japan.  
(compiled by Satsvarūpa dāsa)

## Pakistan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Although we are most fallen and have no qualification to serve you, we pray to be counted among your eternal servants. We have no other desire than this.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we cannot begin to comprehend your glories. So how can we pretend that we can adequately glorify you? Nevertheless, because it is our duty to try and glorify the spiritual master on this most auspicious occasion, we are presenting this totally inadequate Vyāsa-pūjā offering, like a child reaching for the moon.

How can we say what is in our hearts? Śrīla Prabhupāda, your beloved disciple Bhakti Cāru Swami has written, “As the world becomes increasingly aware of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s inconceivable achievement, it will admit without a doubt that His Divine Grace is the greatest personality of this age.” We completely agree, although we do not know what he means by “this age.” He writes, “Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance is not a chance happening but the culmination of five hundred years of spiritual development on this planet.” But we feel that at least “five thousand years” would be more accurate, for we have been told that five thousand years ago when He was on this planet Lord Kṛṣṇa revealed His plan:

“After five thousand years My *mantra-upāsaka* \[worshiper of the holy name\] will appear and spread the chanting of the holy name everywhere. Not only in India but all over the world people will chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. By this chanting the whole world will become *eka-varṇa* \[one class or designation\], namely, *hari-bhaktas,* devotees of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa” (*Brahma-vaivārta Purāṇa,* quoted in *Sahasra Tīrtha Jala,* p. 17).

But actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it seems to us that it would be still more accurate to say that your appearance is the culmination of hundreds of billions of years of spiritual development, for this day of Brahmā culminates in the unique and sole appearances of the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya, yet their appearances really find fructification only in Your Divine Grace’s appearance.

Indeed, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can only see that this very special universe of the four-headed Brahmā, in which Your Divine Grace appears, must have been created especially for your most auspicious appearance, for we can see no higher purpose for this universe than to display Your Divine Grace’s inconceivably most munificent pastimes.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we may be accused of exaggerating your glories due to being your followers, but we cannot see any other way to try and adequately glorify you. As Bhakti Cāru Maharaja wrote,

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has been glorified as the most munificent incarnation. Therefore how special that personality must be to whom He awarded the credit of achieving something even greater than His own achievement....Owing to the extremely envious condition of this world, the Supreme Personality of Godhead appeared without revealing His identity. If anyone told Him that He was the Supreme Personality of Godhead, He would cover His ears as if He had heard the worst blasphemy. But His real devotees recognized Him and let the world know who He actually was. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda also concealed his real identity. Therefore it is the noble responsibility of all his followers to become aware of his real greatness and broadcast it throughout the world for its ultimate benefit.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are simply trying, though unsuccessfully, to become aware of your real greatness. For us, your activities force us to see you as no less magnanimous than Lord Caitanya or Lord Kṛṣṇa. So how can we try and explain your greatness? Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is Kṛṣṇa Himself appearing as His internal energy, and some of your followers have concluded, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you must be the personification of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s marginal energy, or Nārada Muni. But for us you are far more than Nārada Muni—for Nārada Muni, you told us, comes to this planet to observe in amazement your *saṅkīrtana* activities. This proves you are greater than he, just as Lord Mahā-Viṣṇu’s desire to see Lord Kṛṣṇa shows that Kṛṣṇa is greater than the inconceivably great Lord from whose bodily pores trillions of universes emanate with His every breath (*Kṛṣṇa* book, chapter 89).

So we can only beg forgiveness for not being able to adequately glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all our attempts to do so will ever fall short of the mark.

We beg to remain forever your eternal servants,  
Your followers in Pakistan.  
(written by Amogha-līlā dāsa)

## Pandharpur, India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this auspicious day we are asking for your guidance, so that your desire may shine through to the hearts of all the devotees.

This project in the holy land of Panduranga is one of your dear projects, intended to show the people how to live a natural, spiritual life and be happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We are trying to develop agriculture, take care of the cows nicely, and distribute your books in the city of Pandharpur. The project needs improvement, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please give us the strength and enthusiasm to make ISKCON Pandharpur a success.

The devotees of ISKCON Pandharpur, India.

## Paris

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

By rendering a little service to your beautiful lotus feet, one sinful *caṇḍāla* may go back home, back to Godhead. *Vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*. Beloved master, we understand your personality to be the external manifestation of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the supreme personality of Godhead, who dwells in everyone’s heart. Dear spiritual master, if love is found in anyone’s heart, it is there because you are so kind. The holy names of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurāṅga, and yourself have appeared in order to spread the glories of *saṅkīrtana* throughout the world. It is known that no one is more merciful than Nityānanda Prabhu, that He is the last chance for the most fallen ones, and that His lotuslike hands are reaching into the deepest regions of hell to save His beloved creatures. How can one not see that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the personification of the mercy of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaura? Despite our offenses and foolishness, you have not left us here alone in Paris. So please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow Paris to receive your divine mercy once more. When you were here everything was so fortunate and full of bliss. We had a big temple for you and Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Śacīsūta to help us distribute the holy name of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Parisīśvara:

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare Hare Hare*

All your pure servants and devotees were here, lovingly adoring Their Lordships and distributing Their *prasādam*. Every day the entire family of devotees would inundate the great city of Paris with *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. That was a break in Kali-yuga, and everyone felt happy and joyful. This nectar we had, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, was due to your beloved disciples following your instruction: “Distribute my books.”

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to your lotus feet that we may understand how to reconstruct our Paris *yātrā* and show the Parisians that they are devotees of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and could become very, very happy simply by chanting Their holy names.

I’ll wait life after life to drive your car again, as I did in your physical presence, and in the meantime I have a strong desire to serve your servants. Such service is actually a position very much coveted in the three worlds, but due to Kṛṣṇa’s mercy it is very easily attainable now on this planet earth. Let us be free of all our designations, like “I am French,” “I am white,” “I am Indian,” “I am in charge of so many things,” and please let us realize and taste the sweetest of all *rasas,* your eternal serving mood.

When you visited Paris, you compared it to Dvārakā because of its beautiful buildings, palaces, parks, and monuments. Today the forces of the evil Age of Kali have forced your devotees to withdraw to the outskirts of Paris, but we know that your order is “Inundate Paris with *harināma,* distribute my books and *prasādam* to everyone.” On this most auspicious occasion, your appearance day, we pray at your lotus feet to be able to enter Paris again. Please send us some of your mighty transcendental devotees to help us distribute love of God, devotional service, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Toward that goal, here in Paris we are endeavoring to pay our heartfelt obeisances to one another’s hearts, your dwelling places, with the full understanding that we are simply servants of the servants of the servants of your extraordinary International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Your devotees in Paris, France.  
(written by Rāma dāsa)

## Pecs, Hungary

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We would like to thank you for giving us the privilege of practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Although we feel completely unable to appreciate properly what you have done, we can develop a preaching mood of a *parivrājakācārya* by seeing and following your faithful disciples. They do not care how much the Kali-yuga is advancing and influencing everyone; they just keep preaching according to time and circumstances, and with great faith in your teachings they carry out your order.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the power and enthusiasm to follow them and to help them keep nourishing the creepers of devotional service you have planted.

Thank you again, and please forgive our offenses. We are nothing but products of the sinful Kali-yuga. We are struggling day after day with our minds and senses, which are poisoned almost to death, though we are very proud to assist your disciples, such as Śivarāma Swami, Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami, Indradyumna Swami, Bhakti Bhṛṅga Govinda Swami, and all the other great souls who are the manifestation of your mercy toward us.

Please let us remain in this wonderful transcendental family forever.

The devotees at ISKCON’s temple in Pecs, Hungary.  
(written by Braja Ballabhī-Ballabha dāsa)

## Penang, Malaysia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances unto Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet.

Recently I was reading *Prabhupāda in Malaysia,* a book about your one and only visit to Malaysia, from May 3rd to May 7th, 1971. You stopped here en route to Sydney, where you would install the Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha.

Besides appreciating how you came here to inspire and enthuse your dedicated disciples, I could not help but notice another reason, which is no less important. As you stated in your letter dated 8 April 1971 to Hanumān dāsa Brahmacārī (one of the three pioneer devotees then preaching in Malaysia), “As soon as I learn that temple construction and donation of land is a fact, I shall be glad to go.”

Twenty-six days later, you flew from Bombay to Kuala Lumpur, the capital of Malaysia, bringing along the divine couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha. Almost immediately after touchdown, you were taken by helicopter to Telok Anson (188 km away) for a public evening program. This was the start of five hectic days of traveling along the western side of the Malaysian peninsular, conducting public as well as house programs from town to town, tirelessly distributing the holy names. It was indeed a very tight schedule, but you nevertheless displayed neither the fatigue nor the lethargy one might expect of one in a seventy-five-year-old body. You never ceased showering your causeless mercy upon one and all; you planted the *bhakti-latā-bīja* wherever you went, in the hearts of whoever you met. Thus you showed yourself to be an *ācārya* in the true sense of the word, the leader of a worldwide spiritual movement.

Till the very last day of your visit, you preached, as usual, with full compassion. You urged the wife of a top Indian minister to join with her husband in helping to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this land, which was formerly a source of sandalwood (as you point out in your purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 4.106). Always expert and focused on your mission to propagate the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you seized every opportunity that came, however remote. When you met with the government minister’s wife, you wanted to engage her and her husband to help in the way you know best, as you candidly stated: “I want a temple like this.” And with these words you placed into her hands a building plan for a temple that looked like the yet-to-be-built Krishna-Balaram Mandir in Vṛndāvana.

But unfortunately (or fortunately, as it turned out), neither she nor her husband could comply, and the original offer of free land and a temple also did not materialize. Since then, ISKCON Malaysia has acquired a few pieces of land in Kuala Lumpur and Telok Anson, but we never got to construct the temple you desired because obtaining planning permission for a Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Temple in this country, whose national religion is Islam, has been (apparently) utterly impossible (though we tried mightily for it).

But...the *śāstras* declare that the Supreme all-merciful Lord always fulfills the desires of His pure and dearest devotee. How do we reconcile this? From your books we know that the holy scriptures, dictated directly or indirectly by the Supreme Lord, can never be wrong.

Today, on this most auspicious anniversary of Your Divine Grace’s appearance, we are most humbled to report that merely by your desire to see a temple that “must be a branch of our ISKCON and run by us,” (as you wrote in a letter dated 25th August 1971 to Hanumān dāsa and Amogha dāsa in Malaysia), we now have the rare opportunity to offer, solely for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace, an acre of land we recently acquired for the very purpose of convincing you to make an exceptional and merciful detour to Malaysia to see a Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa temple constructed for ISKCON to own and manage just according to the standards set by Your Divine Grace. “Of course in the big cities a big temple is required.” (Letter to Amogha dāsa and Hanumān dāsa, 22 August 1971)

With the prospect of seeing the manifestation of a big temple here, hopefully in the not-too-distant future, all of us here in ISKCON Malaysia pledge to cooperate and serve together toward making your desire a reality.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, just as we are fully convinced that procuring this land, which is zoned especially for religious use, was made possible only by your causeless mercy, similarly we are fully convinced that getting the temple completed is just a matter of time because we know you are always with us all the way, not only till the full manifestation of the temple proper but beyond, for all eternity.

We now need to look ahead, to design the temple as you wanted in 1971, to source out the necessary funding, and to establish a *pakka* management system according to ISKCON’s standard. We pray that our humble endevor will meet with your kind approval.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all of us here were in the darkest region of ignorance when you came here in 1971, not knowing that a pure representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead was amongst us for that brief yet significant five days. Regretfully, we have missed the opportunity to serve you personally then. Wait a minute...you often said that we can associate with you through your books and serve you by following your instructions. This means that all we have to do now is study your books seriously and distribute them, and also serve you within ISKCON, which you said is your body. ISKCON Malaysia’s national president, His Holiness Bhakti Vrajendranandana Swami, always reminds us of this, saying “When I was in Vṛndāvana-dhāma, Śrīla Prabhupāda said that one should stay and serve in ISKCON, that one shouldn’t leave ISKCON.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, being our spiritual grandfather, you are so merciful to us that you have trained your disciples, who are our spiritual fathers and uncles, to be just as merciful, showing us genuine concern and giving us practical guidance to ensure that we do not deviate from the noble purposes you have established for ISKCON.

Last but not least, please allow us to give you a brief report from the NRC which consists of 3 branches and 3 *nāma-haṭṭas*.

1\. Our branch on Penang Island is experiencing an unprecedented success in *bhakti-vṛkṣa* preaching, with more than sixteen active groups formed in the last year. Also our annual Ratha-yātrā is one of the state’s tourist attractions, and Food for Life is also very popular.

2\. Another branch, Bukit Mertajam or Bhakti Mandira on the Penang mainland, is known for its excellent book distribution record and strict Deity worship of Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā. The center is managed solely by congregation members.

3\. The third branch, also managed by congregation members only, is in Sungai Petani, Kedah (a predominantly Muslim state). The members of this branch have established a very close rapport with the local Hindu Sangam, and the devotees are well-respected by other Hindu organizations for their discipline and devotion. All this has won much prominence for ISKCON and Your Divine Grace.

4\. Our three *nāma-haṭṭas—*in Butterworth (Penang state), Alor Star, and Kulim (Kedah state)—are also doing nicely, with the members executing their responsibilities as preachers and practitioners of *bhakti-yoga,* trying their level best to follow in the footsteps of Your Divine Grace.

Our dear and beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have nothing in our lives to live for except to please Your Divine Grace and the *guru-paramparā—*it is only your mercy that is our life air.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā *kī jaya*!

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

We beg to always remain,  
Servants of the servants of Your Divine Grace in Penang, Malaysia.  
(written by Kaleśa dāsa)

## Perth, Australia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā!

On this holy day of your appearance, we, your followers here in Perth, Australia, would like to take the opportunity to say “Thank you” for all you have given us, as well as for the mercy you are continuing to bestow on us through your books, and for the kindness of your disciples. Out of the many wonderful gifts you have given us, the one that particularly stands out—the one that we all hanker for in this world, is happiness. Throughout the translations and purports of your books, you constantly stress that this material world is a place of misery—no place for a gentleman. We can see this is true everywhere. Even here in Australia, the so-called “lucky country,” amidst the good climate, relative prosperity, and relaxed lifestyle, people are not truly and deeply satisfied, due to lack of spiritual knowledge. But out of your causeless mercy, through your books you have given us the knowledge by which we can become free from the misery and ignorance of bodily identification and cultivate knowledge of our real identity, that we are spirit souls, eternal and loving servants of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Through your books you have given us knowledge of a world far beyond the one we can experience with these blunt material senses. You tell us of a beautiful transcendental world where everyone is joyfully and constantly engaged in loving devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. And your books are full of constant glorification and beautiful descriptions of your beloved Lord, the beautiful young son of Nanda Mahārāja. You love Him so much and you desire that we will also love Him, that we will re-establish our long-forgotten relationship with Him. You have given us so many tools to help us cultivate and nurture our budding spiritual lives—the holy name; the *Bhagavad-gītā; Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* our eternal spiritual homes, Śrī Māyāpur and Śrī Vṛndāvana; the Vaiṣṇavas; and so much more. Without your kindness, how would we ever have knowledge of or access to these?

From reading and hearing of your pastimes, we gain much inspiration. The “trials and tribulations” that we encounter in our daily lives and in our attempts to perform devotional service pale into insignificance in comparison with the austerities you performed to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. Without your sacrifices, where would we be today?

Here in Perth we’re quite a small, isolated community, but like all your followers around the world, we’re trying to serve and please you by pushing on Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement as best as we can. Please bless us that we may increase the quality as well as the quantity of our efforts, for both our own purification and for the benefit of souls who haven’t received this knowledge yet.
