Also, we pray that we may learn to associate with and serve one another in a loving, Vaiṣṇava way. s

Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

We pray to remain your servants,  
The devotees of ISKCON Perth, Australia.

## Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving us from this world of illusion. You have returned us to the reality of Kṛṣṇa-centered existence. Our temple, our Deities, our books, our *prasādam,* our devotee friends and congregation, our service, our philosophy, our chanting and dancing, and our vows are only some of the many gifts you have blessed us with to help us remember Kṛṣṇa. We pray that we can show our gratitude to you by sharing these gifts of love with as many people as possible.

From the very beginning you directed our interest, our inquiry, and our devotion to the Supreme Lord. You taught us who Kṛṣṇa is, you taught us how to properly worship Him, and you encouraged us to regain our eternal and loving relationship with Him. You were our teacher, our guide, and our friend.

Generally today, when one hears of a spiritual group, it is defined by its leader’s name. Your movement is defined by Kṛṣṇa’s name: “The Kṛṣṇas,” “Hare Kṛṣṇa,” “Kṛṣṇas,” or formally, “The International Society for Krishna Consciousness.” You always remained the transparent medium, deflecting praise and honor for yourself to Kṛṣṇa.

The only honor you would accept for yourself was as a humble servant of the Lord. So today we are very fortunate, on the anniversary of your merciful appearance in this world, to honor and praise you as the most dear servant of Kṛṣṇa.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us with humility, so that we can properly utilize your many gifts and center our lives on Kṛṣṇa and thus truly honor you with our lives.

Your servants, the aspiring devotees in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA.

## Puncak, Indonesia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We, some forty servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī Prahlāda–Lakṣmī-Nṛsiṁha-deva on the top of a mountain at Puncak, Indonesia, offer you our most humble obeisances on this auspicious day of your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

We know that without your mercy none of us would have gotten the chance to know and serve Kṛṣṇa as we are now doing.

Every day we are performing *guru-pūjā* to you in Rādhā-Govinda’s temple, and we know that you are still watching and guiding us all, both directly and through your representatives.

Now it is the rainy season here, and every day rain pours down from the sky in torrents and is blown sideways by the wind. It reminds us of the mercy of Lord Caitanya, which you have caused to rain down upon the fallen souls of this Kali-yuga, who were burning in the fire of repeated birth and death.

We pray that you will continue to bless us, your spiritual grandchildren, and allow us to remain always serving you and your devotees in ISKCON.

Your servants in Puncak, Indonesia.  
(written by Devabhāga dāsa)

## Rāḍhadesh, Belgium

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa in this world, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**My respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. You are so kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya and delivering the Western countries, which are full of voidism and impersonalism.**

The longer we are allowed to be members of your ISKCON society, the more we become grateful and appreciative, realizing what a benediction you have given us by establishing it. You well understood how much we needed to associate with like-minded souls to be able to succeed in accomplishing (what you made us aware of as) the goal of life: To develop love for Kṛṣṇa and His devotees through the sublime process of devotional service.

In your instructions you do not leave any place for doubt about what we are supposed to do, because in every single purport of the many books you wrote, you clearly define, from every angle of vision, how this process of devotional service is the only way to reach perfection, and you also delineate all the benefits that such perfection can bestow upon conditioned souls.

Therefore, if we can just, with full conviction, take to this path you have chalked out for us in a simple way, then we will be able to understand, and our eternal happiness is assured:

• Chant a minimum of sixteen rounds daily, endeavoring strenuously to avoid offenses.

• Follow the four regulative principles.

• Follow the prescribed spiritual programs in the association of devotees.

• Preach what you have learned and realized so that others can be equally benefited.

There have been so many gurus who have accepted so many disciples, but who, except you, provided so completely the means to follow the devotional path? You created a network of temples, holy *dhāmas* conducive to the growth of *bhakti,* where your disciples and followers can easily put into practice your precious instructions. You had the perfect vision of all that we required, and you set us up for a life of devotional service practically nondifferent from that in the spiritual world.

Here in Rāḍhadesh-dhāma the atmosphere has become so nice that more and more devotees join together for *maṅgala-ārati,* nicely showered and in clean cloth, to glorify Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha and Their pure devotee. The *japa* meditation is so perfectly designed that we don’t feel the slightest distraction. Unfettered by other engagements, we have no business except chanting the holy name, gathering together for strength and inspiration. Then our worshipable Lordships, having been satisfied by the offerings of sixteen *upacāras* (as prescribed by Your Divine Grace), and meticulously dressed in the devotional service of Their most fortunate, loving servants, make Their divine appearance—a transcendental feast for our eyes to behold! We then worship Your Divine Grace with gratitude and submission and hear and chant your message in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class. Our devotional service does not end here, as you have given us varieties of engagements to be performed for the pleasure of Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha throughout our lives: cleaning, cooking, and preaching to the many visitors who become attracted to discover what it is that we are doing here in this divine castle, and in this way they make their first steps back home, back to Godhead.

This is how, from morning till evening, you give us an opportunity to be engaged in *bhakti-yoga,* and because we can do all this together, we feel strong, like a bundle of strings twisted together into a strong rope. Now we can perfect our human lives, which otherwise would have been a guaranteed catastrophic tragedy.

It seems that the only way we can try to thank you over and over again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will be by taking whole-heartedly to this wonderful process given to us by you, out of your unlimited compassion for fallen humanity. For our entire lives, may we ever engage in applying ourselves to your instructions. Even considering that we are slow and crippled by “greatly sinful acts performed in previous lifetimes” and thus may not be able to perfect ourselves in this very life, we pray to follow in the footsteps of Princess Rukmiṇī. In her confidential letter to the Lord, she feelingly submitted that if she could not attain His lotus feet in this very life she was prepared to perform austerities for many, many lives to achieve this end. In any case, by continuing to take ever-increasing refuge in endeavors to satisfy you, we experience ever-increasing happiness and protection from sin.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, as our allotted numbers of breaths in these perishable bodies are being used up more quickly than we can comprehend, it becomes starkly evident that your mercy was, is, and will always remain our sole hope and inspiration. Your repeated, terse reminder, “Don’t waste time!” takes on more and more relevance. We become more and more determined to take every opportunity to imbibe your Bhaktivedanta purports, thus endeavoring to “make the words of the spiritual master one with our hearts.”

As Kali-yuga progresses and the impoverished, downtrodden souls try so many different methods to uplift themselves, we become ever more convinced of the absolute perfection that you tirelessly preached and supremely exemplified. The naked truth of material desire is being revealed in all its inherent ugliness, and the beauty of Kṛṣṇa consciousness emerges as the only tangible alternative.

Please always keep us in the shade of your lotus feet and empower us to bring many others to that same shelter.

Your aspiring servants in Rāḍhadesh-dhāma, Belgium.

## Rome

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet!

We are writing this homage from the new temple in Rome. The first time you came to Rome, in 1974, you were very pleased to see that your transcendental movement was flourishing in this important city. Now again, after a lot of obstacles and accidents, through the effort of many sincere devotees we have a nice place right in the center of the city. It will surely please you to know that Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, in their most wonderful forms, have came to bless us, giving us the opportunity to serve Them and have Their *darśana* every day. Many people come daily to take *prasādam,* to see the temple, and to ask questions about Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you taught us. Actually, you are always with us. It is not, as some people say, that you are dead and now we are without protection. Besides feeling your presence in the wise guidance of your wonderful disciples who are caring for our spiritual lives and in the daily association of the sincere devotees who are part of your transcendental family, we see that you personally are also present in a very concrete way in every moment of our lives. The way you arranged things is so perfect that wherever devotees follow your instructions with complete faith, you are living there—you are giving your association—and we can take shelter there. That’s why you care so much about keeping this movement together and instructed your disciples to cooperate with one another instead of leaving to fulfill their own plans. Our lives are not separate from this ISKCON movement, and you told us many times that you are not different from your ISKCON. We cannot understand what devotional service means if we don’t see your ISKCON. The real thing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that we cannot see Kṛṣṇa without you and your wonderful ISKCON.

Your servants in Rome, Italy.

## Rosario, Argentina

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble and respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

Lamentably, I met Kṛṣṇa consciousness almost six years after you abandoned the planet. As my years as a devotee passed by, that lamentation got stronger and stronger. More and more I appreciated your achievements, your ability to give the most sublime knowledge in the right way, and your spiritual integrity to get through the many difficulties that arose in the movement.

But I have also many times wondered, “And if I had met you, what could I have done of enough importance to justify that mercy?” Many times we have heard that famous phrase “Śrīla Prabhupāda built a house in which the whole world can live.” I didn’t meet the owner of the house, but I did meet those in whose care it was left, and that was your great mercy upon me.

The caretakers are making great efforts to keep that house inhabitable, and indeed to offer the best comfort to those who knock on the door. When I arrived for the first time, they, your sons and daughters, looked after me very well: they offered me a comfortable seat, they brought me some delicious juice to drink, they made me try some tasty typical Hindu preparations, and, above all, they transmitted to me all the love they had inherited from you.

The house shone, but, as in every family, problems started arising.

Thanks to your teachings, we know that the material world is surcharged with difficulties, and real wisdom means understanding that fact and not weakening due to the divergences that may arise.

Our time in this body is so infinitesimal from the point of view of eternity that often I realize how tragic is the loss of time spent discussing themes other than the real purpose of human life: self-realization. Those sparks of understanding are due to your books, and I infinitely thank you.

I don’t have too many realizations, but the greatest and most concrete is my realization of your greatness in giving us your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Once one of your disciples once commented,

We thought that Śrīla Prabhupāda didn’t sleep at night. When he was in Italy he stayed up real late translating with his Dictaphone, and, when his voice was no longer heard we looked through the keyhole of the lock and saw he wasn’t in his bed. During several nights this happened; we thought he didn’t sleep and stayed up reading, until we found out that he slept in the same place he worked.

You translated till your last breath and lay back in that same place. Is there a greater proof of love?!

With all that love you built your ISKCON house, and it is our duty to take care of it with all our strength. At present the house is a bit agitated: some are dissatisfied, others keep their distance, and others have left. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t want to argue or to find fault with others. Every devotee I have met, with no exception, has given me the good things they learned from you, and now it’s my duty to give them to others. That is how the *paramparā* works, and we, as your grandchildren, are also small links in that transcendental chain.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please, help me assimilate your teachings, correctly preach the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and be a good guardian of your transcendental house. This house, in which sooner or later the whole world will live, is your great mercy and our most precious shelter. Here all the fears of the material world become insignificant; I hope the idea of abandoning it and abandoning you never crosses my mind.

Your servants in the ISKCON temple in Rosario, Argentina.  
(written by Janaka dāsa)

## St. Louis

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you and your ISKCON institution, a manifestation of your divine mercy!

Although I am not qualified to praise your glory on this special day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, and although I am not among those who were so lucky to have your personal association, still, requested by the other devotees here, I will try my best to satisfy you by my humble attempt at writing this offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā. Therefore I am begging you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please kindly accept this offering and be satisfied, as a loving grandfather is satisfied with the insignificant attempt of his grandson to please him.

Although I am trying to offer my time, energy, and life to your lotus feet, following your example and teachings, in one sense I still consider myself very unfortunate because I joined your movement three years after your departure from this planet and thus missed the miraculous opportunity to see you face to face. Therefore I couldn’t be trained directly by you. But still, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I feel very fortunate because I am in your ISKCON movement and I was trained by your sincere and dedicated disciples. They taught me that this ISKCON movement is a direct personification of your and Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. Therefore I am so happy to have accepted the shelter of your ISKCON movement, which gives me a lot of opportunity for a spiritual purification and enlightenment. I also feel very obligated to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because your representatives made me the president of your St. Louis temple some years ago. This responsibility is mercifully pushing me to try my best to be a good servant of the other devotees and to take care of everything as nicely as possible just to satisfy you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

By taking care of this small temple in St. Louis, I can experience how difficult it is to maintain and protect even such a small place. And what to speak of caring for the entire ISKCON, as you did, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Caring for such a big institution requires not only maintenance and development but also protection from the various bad influences of Kali-yuga.

I feel very much privileged that I can serve you like a dog at the door of your ISKCON temple, trying to maintain the peace and see that things move forward properly the way you wanted them to. During this service I experience many joys and much satisfaction, as well as sometimes a lot of anxiety and disturbances, which come due to the influence of Kali-yuga and its agents. This influence manifests in a variety of ways that aim to disturb your ISKCON home or even destroy it. Due to your divine mercy, however, and the intervention of the Supreme Lord, everything always ends in an auspicious way, exactly as you wrote in the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.3.22:

As Kaṁsa was not expected to kill the beautiful child of Devakī and Vasudeva, the uncivilized society, although unhappy about the advancement of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, cannot be expected to stop it. Yet we must face many difficulties in many different ways. Although Kṛṣṇa cannot be killed, Vasudeva, as the father of Kṛṣṇa, was trembling because in affection he thought that Kaṁsa would immediately come and kill his son. Similarly, although the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and Kṛṣṇa are not different and no asuras can check it, we are afraid that at any moment the asuras can stop this movement in any part of the world.

You also wrote in the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.11.27:

A devotee desires only that he may execute devotional service undisturbed. Actually we see, however, that even during the presence of Kṛṣṇa, when Nanda Mahārāja and the other cowherd men had the Supreme Personality of Godhead in their presence, there were disturbances. Of course, in every case Kṛṣṇa came out victorious. The instruction we may derive from this is that we should not be disturbed by so-called disturbances. There have been so many disturbances to our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, but we cannot give up our forward march. On the contrary, people are receiving this movement very enthusiastically all over the world, and they are purchasing literature about Kṛṣṇa consciousness with redoubled energy. Thus there are both encouragements and disturbances. This was so even in Kṛṣṇa’s time.

So if there were disturbances even Kṛṣṇa’s time, what to speak about our Kali-yuga time? Still, despite all those difficulties our ISKCON society is passing through, I am convinced ISKCON is the only shelter for those who seriously want to get out of this prison house of material existence and go back home, back to Godhead. ISKCON itself is a challenge to Māyā, and there are always some perturbations during a fight. But I am convinced that by your mercy all the sincere souls can survive and safely continue their service at the lotus feet of your mission.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, although the progress of Kali-yuga increases the degradation of the people in general and thus makes it very difficult to find a sincere person desiring to make spiritual progress, due to your inconceivable mercy everybody still has a chance to elevate him- or herself spiritually and progress on the path back to Godhead. Through your preaching you have not only shown people the direction civilization should go but also given them all the facilities for attaining the perfection of human life. By creating ISKCON you mercifully gave shelter to all those who are sincerely trying to go back to Godhead.

We are all extremely grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything you have done for us. Therefore I assure you that we are sincerely trying to do everything possible to propagate the glories of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, here in St. Louis.

In token of our gratitude, and to show you how much we love you and how much we appreciate what you have given us, I would like to present a few words of a report of our activities written on my request by your disciple, Cakrī Prabhu:

\* \* \*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, here in St. Louis we are carrying on little by little. You never personally came to St. Louis, but you had *darśana* with our Nitāi–Gaura-naṭarāja Deities a few times. We also have Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma on our altar. This is the only temple in America where They are worshiped regularly; we dedicate all our service and activities to Them. Our Govinda’s Restaurant has been running well since 1978, and at present we distribute *prasādam* to 30 or 40 costumers daily. Regarding book distribution, for many years our St. Louis temple has been number one among mahā-small temples in United States. And Divyānanda Prabhu, one of our temple devotees, has been number one in mahā-big book distribution in the US for a long time. He loves to distribute your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. All together we steadily distribute an average of about $800 worth of books every week. We are also regularly invited by a number of schools to make a presentation to their religious classes. To cultivate and develop more intimate relationships with our congregation, we recently started a temple newsletter, which is very much appreciated by the members of our community For the last few years we’ve had a wonderful *saṅkīrtana* festival at the end of the December marathon dedicated to Your Divine Grace. This year many devotees came from Kansas City, led by Dānavīr Mahārāja, and also from Columbus. Those who could not participate made plans to come next year. Devotees from our temple also attend festivals in other temples, such as the Ratha-yātrās in Los Angeles and Chicago.

For the last twenty-seven years I have spent most of my time distributing your books. As you said many times and also wrote in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.11.58:

Vyāsadeva has given this literature so that everyone may understand one’s transcendental position simply by discussing *bhāgavata-kathā*. Even at the present moment, everyone everywhere can be happy and free from material tribulations by following *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. There is no need of austerities and penances, which in this age are very difficult to perform. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has therefore declared, *sarvātma-snapanaṁ paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam*. By our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, we are trying to distribute *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* so that anyone in any part of the world can be absorbed in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement by chanting and hearing about the activities of Kṛṣṇa and be free from all material tribulations.

And similarly, in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.12.11:

The secret of success is unknown to people in general, and therefore Śrīla Vyāsadeva, being compassionate toward the poor souls in this material world, especially in this age of Kali, has given us the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. For Vaiṣṇavas who are somewhat advanced, or who are fully aware of the glories and potencies of the Lord, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is a beloved Vedic literature. After all, we have to change this body. If we do not care about *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* we do not know what the next body will be. But if one adheres to these two books—*Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*—one is sure to obtain the association of Kṛṣṇa in the next life. Therefore, distribution of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* all over the world is a great welfare activity for theologians, philosophers, transcendentalists and yogīs, as well as for people in general. *Janma-lābhaḥ paraḥ puṁsām ante nārāyaṇa-smṛtiḥ* (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.1.6): if we can somehow or other remember Kṛṣṇa, Nārāyaṇa, at the end of life, our life will be successful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all these things are possible and are happening only by your mercy. I would hate to think where I would be without your mercy.  
Your humble servant, Cakrī dāsa

\* \* \*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have to honestly admit, as Cakrī Prabhu has mentioned above, that I don’t want to even think where I would be and what I would do now if it weren’t for your mercy in the form of ISKCON and devotional service. You engaged us in various devotional services as the only means to glorify the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and you assured us that by being thus engaged we would achieve the highest perfection of life. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I thank you for everything you have done for us—for saving others and me. All the devotees in the St. Louis temple join with me in offering these words of thanks.

Jay Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your devotees in the ISKCON temple in St. Louis, Missouri, USA.  
(written by Surapāla dāsa)

## Saltillo, Mexico

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our limitless obeisances to your lotus feet.

On this sacred day we are praying to you, Please request Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda to send us many sincere devotees who will dedicate their lives to performing devotional service in this temple. Our congregation is increasing, and every Sunday more and more guests are coming to the festival. Śrīla Prabhupāda, could you please ask Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva to remove all the obstacles hindering the flourishing of this program? Because Your Divine Grace is our only shelter, we beg you to please, please grant us the fulfillment of this humble petition.

Your grand-disciples at the Rādhā-Govinda Mandir in Saltillo, Mexico.

## Scotland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to you!!

By your great compassion the holy names are now spreading all over Scotland and the north of England at such a fast and enthusing rate. Wherever we go people are calling out the holy name of Lord Gaurāṅga upon seeing us. People approach the devotees on the streets and just to say Gaurāṅga, smile, and go on there way. The fallen souls are very happy by this chanting. We are seeing this more and more. It is now becoming an accepted part of daily life to so many. By the fallen souls chanting Lord Gaurāṅga’s name, we can see very clearly that they are changing and are very happy by this chanting. Some young people are using the name Gaurāṅga as a general greeting whenever they meet each other, and they shout it out loudly upon seeing each other at a distance, thus benefiting so many others in the vicinity.

It is all your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You brought Gaurāṅga’s name to the West and distributed it freely. Now the fallen souls are becoming attached to that great name by your sweet mercy. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are the real mercy of Lord Nityānanda and Śrī Gaurāṅga-sundara. You are the extended mercy rays of the benediction moon spreading throughout the world and giving spiritual light in this dark age.

Prabhupāda is our light, nescience is darkness.  
Where there is Śrīla Prabhupāda there is no darkness.

Here in Scotland we are seeing that Lord Gaurāṅga’s name is fast becoming a household word, as was predicted. This is your great gift of mercy to the suffering and fallen souls of this dark and perilous age. The people of Scotland are generally very gross, but they have a great liking for this Gaurāṅga-nāma.

Thank you for engaging us fallen souls as your tiny little servants in distributing this greatest of transcendental gifts. When we are fully engaged in the distribution of Gaurāṅga-nāma, there are no worries and life is very wonderful, and the fallen souls are very happy also. What else is there for us to do? We are seeing very clearly the amazing results of distributing Gaurāṅga-nāma, so with your permission and much-needed blessings we beg to continue and hope that you are pleased.

Please forgive us for all our offenses and stupidity. We are actually useless, but you have kindly found a use for us. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to Lord Gaurāṅga’s sweet holy name, which is your great gift to the world! You are the real Gaurāṅga dāsa.

Begging to be your dāsas eternally, we remain  
Your insignificant servants in Scotland.

## Seattle

**Endless Loving Rays**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

He moved on most mysterious waves of pure love,  
in ways like no common man,  
like a child with unwavering trust in the midst of the crowds,  
clinging on to his Father’s invisible, invincible hand!

His most brilliant example was like one unspoiled apple  
that somehow remained illustrious among zillions gone rotten,  
for he left behind memories so sweet and effulgent  
in the hearts of his true followers, they cannot be forgotten!

And in his name and his fame shall always remain  
the unconquerable flame that motivates the sane.  
The flame is his love, alone for God above,  
which he kindled in all hearts by the power of his love.

This message I bring is from Him Supreme!  
And so it is eternally beyond any dream,  
floating above earth’s ground, where nothing is sound,  
and yet his gleam could be seen by all, up and down!

He stood out, a golden swan, a most innocent fawn,  
displaying God’s beautiful unlimited dawn!  
The spiritual life beyond man’s dim light,  
which shall forever light up those who’ve lost sight!

As he inspires, I write in the midst of this night,  
a most glorious plight to capture his life,  
the ripest fruit of the Absolute Truth,  
soaring in flight from a virtuous fight!

How can I make short his unlimited glories?  
How can I forsake his blissful stories?  
This most precious account can never end,  
describing the world’s most blessed friend.

O my Godbrothers and Godsisters,  
he alone can heal our blisters,  
who is perfect in his business  
in service to Śrī Kṛṣṇa!

He alone can stop the twisters  
that throw us in the stickers—  
the hell-a-vision, television,  
the box of the wicked!

He knows our trials,  
he has all our files,  
he who traveled all those miles  
to relieve us with his smiles!

We know he’s Prabhupāda,  
we who have properly done our job  
of looking into our hearts and seeing his *Never Nod*.  
Never would he break his vows to his Guru!  
Never would he speak what is false or untrue!  
All we heard was deepest wisdom from his mouth that spoke of Kṛṣṇa!  
All we saw was perfection from God’s perfect reflection!  
Always under God’s protection, as our one and only blessing!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda’s our blessing—there is no contesting!

Our blessings are from Prabhupāda, respected assembly,  
who begs us just to be one loving family!  
“For me, just cooperate together, and we shall live forever  
where everything gets better in ever increasing pleasure!”

Our lives can be most simple, making our home Kṛṣṇa’s temple  
and keeping Prabhupāda’s orders the center of our lives,  
or our lives can be most hard, removing Prabhupāda from our heart  
and believing all he told us was but lies!

“Kṛṣṇa consciousness is simple for the simple,” Prabhupāda said.  
As Jesus our Guru said, “Just be simple like a child!”  
Or we can stay proud and remain in the crowd  
of this crooked planet, running mad and wild!  
But we shall find peace only humbly at Prabhupāda’s feet,  
living only the godly life he gave in perfect style!

Thus by Prabhupāda’s grace,  
even this rascal has embraced his truth, which none can defeat!  
And so again I beg to rise above this place,  
I’m just begging at Prabhupāda’s feet!

Please, birth after birth don’t let me forget life’s worth!  
The only reason you came to this earth  
was to stop all our hurt and cleanse all our dirt  
by your perfectly channeled guidance, forever expert!

So we may see Kṛṣṇa’s form of bliss,  
the only reason we exist,  
Kṛṣṇa consciousness, endless happiness!

Your lost eternal servant, Anubhāvānanda dāsa,  
simply lives on by your grace, despite Māyā’s endless maze.  
I believe you’ll take me from this place,  
because I plead your case and I see your face!  
I see your face in your true servant’s gaze,  
I see your endless loving rays,  
your endless loving rays  
shining endlessly in endless loving ways.

Your servants in Seattle, Washington, USA.  
(written by Anubhāvānanda dāsa)

## Silicon Valley, California, USA

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, which are the boat that can easily carry us safely across the dangerous ocean of the material world and back to Godhead.

We, the devotees at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, have joined together on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā to celebrate your wondrous qualities and activities and to rededicate ourselves to your instructions. Many of the devotees here never had the opportunity to meet you personally; this, however, does not diminish in the least the mercy we feel from following your instructions, hearing from your books, and sitting at your lotus feet to chant the *mahā-mantra*.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.20.25) King Pṛthu praises the pure devotees, saying that the words emanating from their mouths are not ordinary. He says,

My dear Lord, You are glorified by the selected verses uttered by great personalities. Such glorification of Your lotus feet is just like saffron particles. When the transcendental vibration from the mouths of great devotees carries the aroma of the saffron dust of Your lotus feet, the forgetful living entity gradually remembers his eternal relationship with You. Devotees thus gradually come to the right conclusion about the value of life. My dear Lord, I therefore do not need any other benediction but the opportunity to hear from the mouth of Your pure devotee.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are finding much-needed solace and seek your benediction daily by simply sitting down together to read the books that you so mercifully left for us. Each of your purports gives us perfect instruction on how to rise to the highest goal of life by awakening our love for Kṛṣṇa—*premā pumartho mahān*. Your references to *śāstra* and your logical arguments convince us that Kṛṣṇa is the All-Attractive One, the Supreme Personality, and that we will become happy by surrendering to Him. We are experiencing the transcendental effects of these saffron mercy particles produced by your speaking.

But what we are feeling is legitimate, for you’ve delivered to us the real thing, the authorized process of *bhakti-yoga* passed down through disciplic succession. As your Guru Mahārāja, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda has written:

Causelessly merciful Kṛṣṇacandra sometimes comes personally and sometimes sends His dear associate as a teacher, preacher, or deliverer of the transcendental sounds of the spiritual world to revive the constitutional position of the unfortunate souls who have forgotten Kṛṣṇa. (*Caitanya-bhāgavata, Madhya* 1, purport).

And the *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣads* confirms that one becomes fixed in understanding the Vedic conclusion by hearing from the guru in disciplic succession: *śrotriyaṁ brahma-niṣṭham*. Considering this, we can understand that you are the certified messenger of the Lord.

So that we may monitor our own spiritual progress, you’ve often suggested that we consider the instruction given to Mahārāja Nimi by Śrī Kavi, in which he says,

Devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord, and detachment from other things—these three occur simultaneously for one who has taken shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in the same way that pleasure, nourishment, and relief from hunger come simultaneously and increasingly, with each bite, for a person engaged in eating \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.2.42\]

For us, glimpses of true satisfaction, growing spiritual nourishment, and a sense of increasing disinterest in material life when hearing and chanting according to your instructions are proof of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental existence and of your exalted position as His via medium. By your mercy we are experiencing for ourselves how pure *kṛṣṇa-kathā* brings us to the *ānandāmbudhi-vardhanam,* the ever-increasing ocean of spiritual happiness referred to by Lord Caitanya.

But why did you take so much trouble to come here to deliver these precious teachings to us? We find in you no other motives than your desire to please your spiritual master and your selfless wish to see us become truly happy by reviving our love for Kṛṣṇa. In 1972 you wrote these words in a letter to His Holiness Satsvarūpa Mahārāja:

Your first job should be to make sure that every one of the devotees in your zone of management is reading regularly our literatures and discussing the subject matter seriously from different angles of seeing, and that they are somehow or other absorbing the knowledge of Krishna Consciousness philosophy. If they are fully educated in our philosophy and if they can get all of the knowledge and study it from every viewpoint, then very easily they will perform tapasya or renunciation and that will be their advancement in Krishna Consciousness.

Who else but you so painstakingly calculated how to catch everyone in the whole world in the merciful net of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and then keep them there? Those you caught, you patiently guided toward the highest ideal of pure, unmotivated service to Kṛṣṇa. Tirelessly you revealed to us how all the seemingly desirable fruits in this world, even the luscious-looking ones, are rotten if they do not lead to pure *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. You have saved millions of fallen souls and continue to do so through the transcendental sound you’ve recorded and by the missionary tidal wave you set in motion. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja states that only a directly empowered representative of the Lord can perform such marvelous deeds: *kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana.* (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya* 7.11) Examining your methods and personality, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can find no one as charitable or as kind as you.

So it is natural that we now desire to at least try to repay you in some way. So that we might accomplish this, you have pointed out to us the means adopted by Haridāsa Ṭhākura, who sought to relieve Lord Caitanya’s transcendental anxiety by giving *kṛṣṇa-nāma* to all living entities. You write,

Everyone should adopt the cause of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to try to relieve Him from the anxiety He feels. This is actually service to the Lord. One who tries to relieve Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s anxiety for the fallen souls is certainly a most dear and confidential devotee of the Lord. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya* 3.52, purport\]

Therefore on this auspicious day we pray that we may be empowered to help you in your mission to distribute *kṛṣṇa-kathā* to the fallen souls in this world. But since we are the most fallen, we first of all pray that we may take full advantage of your teachings, gradually becoming fixed in devotional service twenty-four hours a day, and in this way please you. And may we increase our own anxiety and efforts for spreading the teaching’s of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, as you have asked us to do, so that all other living entities can come under the Lord’s shelter and go back home, back to Godhead.

Offering millions of obeisances at your divine feet, we are praying for a drop of your mercy.

Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.  
(written by Vaiśeṣika dāsa)

## Solapur, India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

You appeared on this earth to deliver bound souls like us from the ocean of birth and death in this Kali-yuga. We feel your presence in your books. You have established a house where all the world can live in peace. But bound souls like us are not able to take advantage of this opportunity. Kindly bless us so that all devotees can stay in your house peacefully.

As Lord Kṛṣṇa says to the Pracetas in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s* Fourth Canto, “My dear sons of the King, I am very much pleased by the friendly relationships among you. All of you are engaged in one occupation—devotional service. I am so pleased with your mutual friendship that I wish you all good fortune.” This proves that the spiritual master and lord Gaurāṅga like service done unitedly with love by all devotees. Kindly bless us so that we can serve your lotus feet unitedly.

On this auspicious day we pray to the Lord to be able glorify and serve Him throughout our lives by serving and glorifying you, Śrīla Prabhupāda..

By your blessings we are getting a piece of land for ISKCON Solapur, where we hopefully will establish Vṛndāvana Gardens and Govinda’s Restaurant. This will be possible only with your blessings. Kindly give us the spiritual strength so that we can serve unitedly for the pleasure of you, our spiritual master, and Lord Gaurāṅga. We need your utmost mercy so that we can serve without any personal motives.

Your humble servants in ISKCON Solapur, India.

## Stockholm, Sweden

*mahat-sevāṁ dvāram āhur vimuktes*  
*tamo-dvāraṁ yoṣitāṁ saṅgi-saṅgam*  
*mahāntas te sama-cittāḥ praśāntā*  
*vimanyavaḥ suhṛdaḥ sādhavo ye*

**“One can attain the path of liberation from material bondage only by rendering service to highly advanced spiritual personalities. These personalities are impersonalists and devotees. Whether one wants to merge into the Lord’s existence or wants to associate with the Personality of Godhead, one should render service to the mahātmās. For those who are not interested in such activities, who associate with people fond of women and sex, the path to hell is wide open. The mahātmās are equipoised. They do not see any difference between one living entity and another. They are very peaceful and are fully engaged in devotional service. They are devoid of anger, and they work for the benefit of everyone. They do not behave in any abominable way. Such people are known as mahātmās.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **5.5.2)**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious occasion of the 105th anniversary of your appearance day!

You have carried the mercy of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda to the fullest extent. Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda are the most munificent incarnations of the Lord, and Your Divine Grace is the *senapati* general of Their Lordships. By delivering the topmost yoga system, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to the most fallen *mlecchas* and *yavanas* all over the world, you have accomplished a feat previously unknown in recorded Vedic history.

Your instruction was that we followers should strive to maintain the legacy we inherited from you. It was under your order that the temple in Stockholm was established and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi were installed in the mid 70’s. In 1998 we had a major crisis here, and the temple was for some time without a GBC and the normal authority structure. It was uncertain that the temple, preaching, and congregational activities could continue at all. But remembering and taking shelter of your instructions to maintain what you had given us gave us the necessary strength and inspiration to survive the drastic reorganization necessary to keep your mission going up to the present day here in Stockholm. Actually, it was like this all the time, Śrīla Prabhupāda; the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was always running on your pure desire to give Kṛṣṇa to the whole world.

Our realization is that your leadership is always here, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and that is wonderful. When we follow the GBC and the authority structure in ISKCON, we are following you.

We humbly beg a benediction. We feel very awkward in our supplication, since we have never met you , but please hear our clumsy prayer. Our desire is that we can from now on follow your desire and no one else’s. You wanted that we would spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone, and to do so we have to cooperate, as we have heard many, many times in your lectures and in the purports in your books. So we pray to your lotus feet again and again to receive the mercy to perform *saṅkīrtana*.

Your servants in Stockholm, Sweden.

## Sydney, Australia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are the only honest man! And upon this conviction, I have based my entire life.

You once defined honesty as, in all circumstances, accepting Lord Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Knowing this in theory, is one thing, preaching it is another, but unequivocally acting out the conclusion, is something else altogether. Though a dutiful conformation to Truth may constitute a basic integrity, only when it becomes devoid of any calculation for remuneration or reward, or any consideration of personal safety or gain, does it begin to approach true honesty. Not until our whole existence however, becomes one of unalloyed affection for Him, devoid of any interest other than His, does it constitute truth absolute. Such perfection of loving intimacy can arise from only the rarest kind of virtue, a virtue wholly conspicuous in this crass and mundane world by dint of its complete absence.

Here, all living entities are simply intent on killing Kṛṣṇa, and usurping His position, becoming themselves the controller, proprietor and enjoyer of all that be. Resentful of Him, innately envious, determinedly forgetful, utterly dishonest, all of us here, hell bent, straining and desperate, striving to enjoy. Gross pleasure, to be sure. Even more though, the exhilaration of fame, the intoxication of power, ultimately, the rapture and ecstasy of being adored: being Kṛṣṇa!

Who is immune? From Indra to the ant, upon such devious motive all existence sustains. Unlike Kṛṣṇa however, who really is God, we cannot simply assert ourselves. In vicious competition, we must by irony be bound, to disguise our intent, to mask our plan. Spinning webs of illusion, deception and lies, we plot, maneuver and devise, engaging every artifice of specious sentiment and pious pose. How terrible is life? Every action, mere strategy; every affection, requisite camouflage; each earnest entreaty, a mere poker bid: enmity innate, subversion the sole aim, each entity spelling ruin for the other, and the most insidious threats reposed in the near and dear. Yet we, the self-same sower’s and reapers of the whirlwind, though seeing, are blind, for if skeptical of the rest, we yet believe in ourselves. What menace then the good man, supposing himself so, for who more convincing then one, who, in himself, is convinced? Beware the one thus, deceived in his soul, in whom honesty, is so honestly assumed. For then the *sādhu* himself becomes the very heart of deceit, and his mouth the gateway to hell. In a world where the best is worst and self is the greatest lie, where, may we ask, is truth to be found?

Only in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, whom Kṛṣṇa kindly sent. Not being of this world you simply had no interest in it. Unlike others, your presence here, indicative of no corrupt intent, no hidden agenda, no motive for self. Your thoughts lay only in Vṛndāvana, your transcendental home; your heart solely with Him, with Kṛṣṇa, your dearmost, your friend. It was simply fidelity’s pure reflection that we all saw, manifest in a loyalty and love for Śrī Guru, and for the service of Nitāi-Gaura. Śrīla Prabhupāda, steadfast and guileless, with integrity did serve, the disciplic succession, in every thought and word, so making plain what all was about, even we from the West, left in no doubt. He made plain what is truth, what is faith, service and love. “I simply follow blindly, I do not know the result.” This is the great gift Śrīla Prabhupāda has brought: the real truth, the truth in all things, the truth till it hurts! He brought it, he lived it, demonstrated it and opened our eyes to it. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s unique, “matchless gift,” to the world: pure devotion for Kṛṣṇa, perfect and sure, no motive, no compromise, ever to endure.

His mission’s success, singular, unprecedented, a reflection of himself, now shines out over all; over the whole of religion, the whole of Vaiṣṇavism, the whole of the Gauḍīya-sampradāya. Inspired by your success, perhaps more so than its cause, others now embark on odyssey’s of their own, like blown leaves, skipping and flying in the wake of a storm. Wishing to do right, no doubt, yet can we not detect, more than mere acquiescence to the divine, a hint perhaps of calculation to the purpose. More than a gracious, and seemly, footstep following, a certain sense of aspiration, to excel. Rather then the sublime carelessness of Prabhupāda, of one who actually has Kṛṣṇa, degrees of affinity for those joy’s, so much in common held, by the elderly and saintly, who don’t: the accruals of a subtle glory—validating our precious sense of self, vindicating our life’s tentative sacrifice, bequeathing us that final lustrous legacy so gratifying to the neurotic need to leave an indelible mark.

We say, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, was not an ordinary man.” Why? Because, other than by grand understatement, how can we satisfactorily highlight the way in which Śrīla Prabhupāda was unique, the way in which he was sublimely different to everyone else, every other “ordinary” man? Ultimately, where any amount of overstatement would by inadequacy offend, this is about all we can say. As Śrīla Prabhupāda said of his own spiritual master, “What can I say, he was a Vaikuṇṭha man!” Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are also a “Vaikuṇṭha man.” Though you came here, you are not from here. Unlike others, unlike all of us, you had no partiality for this world, no ulterior motive, no personal agenda to fulfill. Yours was an honesty absolute, the truth incarnate; therein lies the glorious difference that was you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were, and are, the only completely honest man! This is why I trust you, and only you, as well as those who trust you, and only you. This is why I follow you, and only you, as well as those who follow you, and only you. This is why I serve you, and only you, as well as those who serve you, and only you. And, that is why I try to love you, and only you, as well as those who love you, and only you.

Your aspiring servants at ISKCON Sydney, Australia.

## Szeged, Hungary

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We offer our service at your lotus feet with love, since we, your spiritual grandchildren, are receiving an eternal shelter at them. Though the paths of spiritual realization are often hard to follow, your teachings and your personal example rise high as a solid spiritual island in the waters of our awkward meander.

Nowadays, when spirituality is disappearing from everyday life and we are facing more and more obstacles in our chosen path, which leads to our aim, only you and your faithful followers can give us righteous instructions for any spiritual progress.

We are asking you to continue helping us in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa and to give us spiritual strength so we can keep this most important aim of our life in our mind constantly.

Your eternal servants in Szeged, Hungary.

## Taiwan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories unto your lotus feet!

On this most auspicious day, the first celebration of your divine appearance in the new millennium, we are deeply remembering your mercy upon us.

We have been wandering, suffering in different bodies, on different planets. We were looking for the Absolute Truth, wanting to attain true love for the Supreme Person. Finally we were so fortunate that by the mercy of the Supreme Lord you appeared on this planet to deliver us!

And then we got the chance to hear the transcendental message of the Absolute Truth to counteract the heavy mist of Māyāvāda and Śūnyavāda. Like that lucky tortoise, our heads poked through that hole in the board floating in the ocean of material existence. When our heads poked through that hole, we saw the hope, we saw the light of absolute knowledge. We were reconnected with the dear loving service to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! Our real selves finally rejoiced in our original situation. Because of your mercy we are now twice-born. We are alive again!

Taiwan used to be called Formosa, which is Portuguese for “the beautiful island.” Physically we are embodied in this Khasa race, the most fallen conditioned souls. Yet we are also born on the beautiful island of Formosa, and more importantly born in the Golden Age of Lord Caitanya within Kali-yuga. How fortunate we are!

You said to cultivate a devotee consumes gallons of blood. You are so merciful to us. Please empower us so that we also can inspire more and more people in Taiwan to come back to this right path, to their real home! Please guide us and protect us. Please let us live with your divine love and transcendental knowledge. Let this Taiwan become a fantastic Spiritual Formosa one day.

You told us, “Everything is there in my books,” and “There are devotees on every street corner.” Please open our eyes so we can see potential devotees everywhere. Please let all your devotees hold one another’s hands, and let us all, holding hands tightly together, go back to our original, eternal home in Goloka Vṛndāvana.

We pray for the strength to follow in your footsteps and to grow more mature. Please empower us so that we may cross this ocean of birth and death.

We are slow as snails, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so slow that we are crawling. We still suffering and lament because of our past sinful deeds. Yet we are so blissful when we are conscious of you and your mercy upon us in allowing us to serve dear Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa!

Your grandchildren in Taiwan.  
(written by Rāja Lakṣmī-devī dāsī)

## Tallinn, Estonia

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet on this auspicious day of your appearance!

The Lord can manifest Himself in many ways, and similarly the spiritual master, the Lord’s representative, can manifest his mercy in many different ways. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for being there for us by chastisement and by approval, in advancement and in setbacks, in hope and in frustration. We know now that we can rely on you no matter what happens.

You gave us a narrow but safe path to purity, which remains just a vague idea for practically all other spiritualists. Everything is clear-cut in your teachings—each of our acts is either devotional service or a materialistic activity, and there is no third alternative. We don’t have to speculate or invent anything. Thus your strictness in philosophy exhibits your topmost love for us.

Now by virtue of your immense inner power you are gaining more and more success in this place. Having heard your words, some sincere souls have come to learn about Kṛṣṇa. Now let us find the intelligence to represent you so that they will become convinced of the superiority of *bhakti*. And for those who have become convinced and have engaged themselves in devotional service, please guide us so that we can offer them a firsthand experience of unconditional love.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed your love for us by giving our lives true meaning. Now it is our turn to express our love for you by taking care of ourselves and others who have come to your shelter. Thank you for the honor of that responsibility. Please keep us close to you.

Trying to further your mission in Estonia, we remain  
Your devotees in ISKCON Tallinn, Estonia.

## Tampa, Florida, USA

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Saṅkīrtana University: Preaching is the Essence

All glories to *jagat-guru* A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

Śrīla Prabhupāda started the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement to create some first-class *brāhmaṇa* Vaiṣṇavas. He wanted all his disciples to be engaged as *brāhmaṇas* in five activities:

1\. Studying the *Vedas*  
2\. Worshiping in the temple  
3\. Teaching (*saṅkīrtana*)  
4\. Accepting charity  
5\. Giving charity

The enormous success of ISKCON is due to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s pushing brahminical life exclusively. He said that due to Kali-yuga people are forced to work as *śūdras* and *vaiśyas* and thus an imbalance of religious principles is created.

Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted an army of *saṅkīrtana* devotees engaging in brahminical work. Only *brāhmaṇas* can convert others into *brāhmaṇas*. Prabhupāda sacrificed his life for *saṅkīrtana* and expected all his disciples to do the same. He heaped praise upon any disciple engaged in *saṅkīrtana,* making it very clear what service pleased him.

As his disciples exhibited some propensities toward *kṣatriya, vaiśya,* or *śūdra* activities, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our divine master, engaged us accordingly to support his *saṅkīrtana* mission. Let’s look at how he did this.

*Brāhmaṇas:* worship in the temple. Preach through book distribution. Live poor and simple.

*Kṣatriyas:* GBC, temple presidents, intensive organizing of the *saṅkīrtana* mission.

*Vaiśyas:* Two businesses were allowed in ISKCON, farming and restaurants, directly assisting preaching. Outside businesses were encouraged by Śrīla Prabhupāda if 50% of the profit was given in charity for *saṅkīrtana*.

*Śūdras:* There is labor in temple construction and maintenance and in the most exalted service in the *saṅkīrtana* mission—pot-washing.

Let us always remind ourselves that the foundation of this ISKCON society is the *saṅkīrtana* mission. Without *saṅkīrtana* we are another dead religion. As Lord Caitanya gave *kṛṣṇa-prema* freely, we must also distribute *kṛṣṇa-prema* by distributing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books.

The unique quality of giving *kṛṣṇa-prema* by a devotee who has *kṛṣṇa-prema* is an envy-free consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda bluntly told us that if someone has envy toward anyone he is not a devotee but only a showbottle. Therefore the success of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON mission to save the world is dependent on the envy-free environment all the devotees work in, in all their departments, to keep the *saṅkīrtana* push alive.

We pray we may have your blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this happiest day, to continue struggling, propagating *saṅkīrtana* in every town and village, and always renewing our determination to please you in our service throughout our short life.

Your servants in Tampa, Florida, USA.  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Tokyo, Japan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you! And all glories to your *saṅkīrtana* movement!

I still remember how deeply I was impressed when I first watched the video “Your Ever Well-Wisher.” I was deeply touched to see what a great achievement you had accomplished. I also understood how insignificant I was, realizing how insignificant were my so-called troubles, and how trivial was my so-called happiness.

One Japanese statesman has a policy that primary-school textbooks should have articles about great heroes whom everyone can admire and look up to, so that children can have clear idea of what kind of personality they want to become.

In India they have a tradition of Vedic culture where people look up to Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira and Arjuna, described in the *Mahābhārata*, and Lord Rāmacandra and Hanumān in the *Rāmāyaṇa*. People are under the influence of these great heroes and get enlivenment from them. But in Western countries it is insignificant rock singers and movie stars who are admired and looked up to, only because people don’t have any idea of what real heroes are. Heroes are the ones who can give virtue and strength to people in general by their examples and instructions.

In India Mahātmā Gandhi is also regarded as a hero, giving strength and courage to people in general. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is actually you who are the greatest hero in the modern age. This is not just sentiment, but fact. What Gandhi gave is something temporary, but what you have given is of eternal value. You have given *vijñāna,* or realized knowledge, to the human society, not just something that appeals to their sentiment.

By reading your books we can get in touch with great heroes who set examples of ideal life for us. And by deeply studying your books, following their instructions, and teaching the messages of your books to people in general, we can get the spiritual strength to spread your glories throughout the whole world.

Lord Kṛṣṇa engages us in the effort of spreading His messages throughout the whole world. Everyone is given an opportunity to take part in this great movement. This is the mercy we can get from Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Our mission is to take part in the great movement with great pleasure.

I am thinking that a Vyāsa-pūjā offering should be a mission statement, not just some ink on paper. The word “love” should not be just a noun, but a verb. “Love” is an activity, not just something to say.

When devotees cooperate in serving you, then we can love you, and that cooperation in service is the real Vyāsa-pūjā homage we would like to offer you.

Ultimate cooperation is to unite all living entities with Lord Kṛṣṇa in the center. It is difficult to actualize this ultimate cooperation, but we must try. We simply beg your mercy so that we can keep trying, so that we can have great courage, intelligence, and strength to do so.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us an opportunity to offer you this Vyāsa-pūjā homage.

Your servants in Tokyo, Japan.  
(written by Madhumaṅgala dāsa)

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa, Italy

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your appearance in this dark world is like the appearance of the rising sun, which dispels the darkness of illusion. Your appearance also means the appearance of the knowledge of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. In the Western world this knowledge is unknown; there so much sense gratification that covers the intelligence of the spirit soul. At the right time you arrived with transcendental knowledge to show us the path, and you implored us to wake up and take it, just as Kṛṣṇa ordered Arjuna: *tasmād uttiṣṭha kaunteya yuddhāya kṛta-niścayaḥ.* ““My dear Kaunteya, get up and fight with determination.” In the same way, your coming to the Western world induced the conditioned souls to get up and fight with determination for their spiritual lives. Due to the influence of Kali-yuga, no one wanted to surrender to the Supreme Lord. But by your example you showed us how to stand up and fight for Kṛṣṇa.

From the very beginning your mission was very clear: no secrets, no mystery, no word jugglery—just the clear understanding coming down through the *paramparā* that we are all servants of Kṛṣṇa and we have to surrender unto Him. Fully aware of your identity as an eternal associate of the Supreme Lord coming from the spiritual world, you ignored the limitations brought on by age and never considered the difficulties caused by the material body; you simply used the body as a vehicle to serve the Lord, without considering happiness or distress, heat or cold. When you arrived in the USA there were no material facilities, but with faith in the instructions of your Guru Mahārāja you accepted everything as the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. Whatever Kṛṣṇa sent, you utilized properly.

Today so many fortunate people are coming in contact with the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement and taking pleasure in reading your wonderful books. Actually, the *saṅkīrtana* movement is always rising, always fresh and pure. Within your books there is everything. At any time, on any page we open to, there is always purity springing up in front of our eyes and entering deeply within our hearts. Such wonderful transcendental knowledge you are giving to the world, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Everyone feels very grateful for this wonderful achievement; it will last eternally, like the scripture. As the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are eternal, so your transcendental purports are also part of the eternal spiritual world.

On this transcendental Vyāsa-pūjā day, so blessed by the Lord, I feel very indebted to you that you have allowed me to remain in the association of your devotees for another year. Kindly allow me to remain in your home to continue chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and rendering ever-increasing service to your movement. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am eternally indebted to you. Thank you very much. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your devotees at Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa, Italy.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Vṛndāvana, India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*Blessings of the* paramparā *you brought,*  
ourselves so wretched and distraught,  
our qualifications and lives were naught,  
in Śrī Caitanya’s net you skillfully have us caught.

Such was your *guru-niṣṭhā,*  
you did not care for *pūjā, lābha,* or *pratiṣṭhā.*  
*Yugala-mūrti* Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa your *iṣṭa;*  
you taught us the art of *śiṣṭa*.

Alone you sailed on the *Jaladuta,*  
a true representative of Nityānanda Avadhūta,  
to spread Śrī Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtan adbhuta,*  
a swanlike messenger, *haṁsadūta*.

Only hope for the human race,  
Kṛṣṇa’s mercy through Your Divine Grace,  
takes us to Him at an inconceivable pace  
in your loving arms us babes do brace.

Empty is our spiritual kitty;  
upon us please take pity.  
Pour gems of *prema* from Kṛṣṇa’s city,  
our master so kind and witty.

Attaining *kṛṣṇa-prema* no more an impossible feat,  
husked paddy we will not beat.  
When we have your lotus feet,  
care what shall we for Māyā’s poisonous teat?

You mercifully changed everyone’s fates,  
be it us or Bill Gates,  
canceled permanently with Yamrāj our dates,  
gave priceless jewels of *bhakti* at hyper-discounted rates.

Weapons of Kali will bounce back,  
having no potency to attack.  
You have put us on the right and fast track,  
from which there is no going back.

Forever will you be remembered on earth;  
henceforth of love and compassion there will be no dearth.  
Cruel and horrendous Kali-yuga will give up his mirth,  
hearing of Śrī Caitanya’s *senapati’s* birth.

Near Indore, in Ujjain, did Sāndīpani Muni teach  
Kṛṣṇa and Balarām sixty-four arts outside common men’s reach.  
With your strong desire to preach,  
Māyā’s impenetrable fort did you breach.

They showed by their example the need to accept a guru,  
repeated instruction to Arjuna, child of Kuru.  
In anticipation of your desires we should be like Mahārāj Yayāti’s son Puru;  
thus we shall never encounter a creature of the name Ruru.

We are babes, help us grow, dear Pitā.  
You have taught us *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Gītā,*  
and of course *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,*  
revealing Kṛṣṇa and Rādhā, Rām and Sītā.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in the service of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi in Vṛndāvana, India.

## Warsaw, Poland

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust your divine lotus feet.

We devotees of the Warsaw *yātrā* wish to express our gratitude for the auspicious work that you performed for us.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are available to us in your books. Before transcendental book distribution, Poland was dark, cold, and sad. To illustrate how your books have turned this country around, let me cite some words from a letter from one young lady. This fortunate soul relates:

I indulged in drugs, sex, alcohol, cigarettes, and other nonsense for many, many years. And who saved me from this? Nobody but my master—Swami Prabhupāda, the most lovable man in the whole world, the most charitable soul, who is saving such fallen and senseless people as myself.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, only after reading one of your books, “The Teachings of Queen Kuntī,” was I able to stop all my nonsense. Here I found the man I was looking for my whole life. He was for me the sage, father, and mother, the supreme teacher, who knows the answers to all my questions. Why am I struggling here? For which sins? Why am I so lonely? Why is life is so difficult? Why are people dying in Ethiopia from AIDS and famine? And so on. All these questions were answered when I found him, or maybe when he found me.

After reading this book, the girl wanted very much to know more about you; therefore she visited our temple and bought your biography. Now she and her boyfriend are chanting the *mahā-mantra,* studying your books, associating with devotees by inviting them to their home, and visiting devotees in their homes. They speak many heart-touching words in appreciation of good, exemplary devotees. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the foremost and dearmost example of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s devotee in the world. Now, following in your footsteps, many of your noble sons are sacrificing their lives and caring for us, your young grandsons and granddaughters. For us, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, close association with pure devotees is the only hope for purifying our dirty hearts. This is confirmed in the beautiful and auspicious *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* in the Eleventh Canto’s 26th chapter, known as “The *Aila-gītā*”: “Just as cold, fear and darkness are eradicated for one who has approached the sacrificial fire, so dullness, fear and ignorance are destroyed for one engaged in serving the devotees of the Lord.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.26.31)

Therefore, thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for sending us your wonderful disciples like Indradyumna Swami, Trivikrama Swami, Suhotra Swami, Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra Prabhu, Prahlādānanda Swami, and many others, who are like a rain of auspiciousness. Thank you also for wonderful grand-disciples like above-mentioned girl.

Your servants in ISKCON’s temple in Warsaw, Poland.

## Yugoslavia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the holy dust of your lotus feet! All glories to Your Divine Grace !

You came to this country for the first time in 1977, in the form of one of your books, *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*. You become a dear guest in several thousands of homes. Year by year your visits became more frequent and intense. In your name devotees started a revolution: they distributed books and *prasādam,* organized public preaching programs, ran a temple, and slowly surrendered their hearts to Kṛṣṇa. With full faith we rejected this civilization of cats and dogs and tried to accept the principles of true culture and religion.

No one else could have captured our hearts like you did, although we newer saw you personally. No one else could have moved us so and made us cooperate. Your words are so powerful...so powerful that they represented you personally. Thanks to them, all of us got a new life. The only shelter that we had and have are your books and the *mahā-mantra,* which you gave us.

Even today, when we fight for each and every breath of spiritual life—without a temple, strong preaching, or mass book distribution—the only medicine to cure us consists of your divine words and thoughts. Whoever surrendered to them truly, at least once, cannot forget them and again comes back to take their shelter.

Dear Śrīla prabhupāda, we are just like a dry desert that desperately needs the rain of your mercy. If we could again dance like a puppets in your hands, then please make us dance, make us dance...

Your young and lost grandchildren in Yugoslavia.  
(written by Madhva Muni dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we here at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust have dedicated our lives to the production and distribution of your books. These books are like transcendental seeds that are cast far and wide, among all kinds of people. When those seeds take root in a fertile heart and begin to produce the *bhakti* creeper, we often receive letters of appreciation from the grateful recipients of your books. We would like to present to you a few of the many appreciations of your books we have gotten from everyday people over the last few months. These are a great source of inspiration to us here at the BBT, and to all devotees, and we hope they will please you:

February 15, 2001  
Accidentally Stumbled on!

I was at a Phish concert three years ago in New York, and as we were walking back to our tent after that night’s show, a guy handed me “Journey of Self Discovery.” I talked with him very briefly, felt happy giving him a donation for the book, and left. I looked at it briefly, but never read it. I was in a bad place at the time, and I was using a lot of drugs. I thought that drugs were to expand consciousness. I was wrong. About three weeks ago, I found the book on my bookcase. I decided to give it a chance. I couldn’t put it down!! I started chanting and I got a copy of the *Bhagavad-gita* As It Is and have been reading that too. I am looking for all the information I can find. Thank you for being here.

Hare Krishna,  
Michael S.  
Hanover Park, Illinois

March 21, 2001  
Dear Sirs,

In the preface to the *Bhagavad-gita* Bhaktivedanta Swami says anyone seriously interested in deriving benefit by studying...must take help from the Krishna consciousness movement. I am seriously interested and respectfully ask for help. Please forward my request to anyone who may offer me guidance in reading the *Bhagavad-gita.*

Many thanks,  
Melvin U.  
Omaha, Nebraska

March 8, 2001  
Dear Jagadisananda Dasa,

Thank you very much for your quick response to my request. The Gitas arrived yesterday. The gift of 35 Gitas by you and the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust will be utilized for years to come by the students in my course: History of World Religions. The texts will be a valuable resource as the students study your rich and ancient faith.

Sincerely,  
Craig B.  
Rockford Public Schools,  
Rockford, Michigan

March 2, 2001  
To ISKCON,

First of all thanks for this book I just read called Discovering the Original Person, Śrī Isopanisad, by Swami Prabhupāda. It is really very interesting to see that I am not alone in what I’ve felt to be true. Immediately I identified with the Mantras more than any other Holy scripture of the world. I write you this because I am more than interested in this book. I am being shown that it just doesn’t seem that I have a place in the world I’m in. People around have even called me crazy often times, because I don’t desire to serve this world. But I have seeked and found that there are others that are not saturated in mrtyuloka. I would like to know how I can join you, your locations, etc. Thanx for your time,

Love,  
Jason

March 1, 2001  
Dear ISKCON,

My name is Gilbert, and about 2 days ago I called the 800# listed in the book catalog and I talked to the devotee there for about 2 hours. All the questions that I had concerning Krsna and your society were wonderfully answered by him. It was incredible! I just recently started to study and inculcate in my life the Higher Spiritual principles written by Śrīla Prabhupāda in books like Dharma—The Way of Transcendence and the Śrī Isopanisad. And I have grown tremendously through the Higher Divine Truths of the *Bhagavad-gita* and the books written by Śrīla Prabhupāda. I now study every day the Holy Vedic Scriptures and the books you publish. I love all of you for the gifts you’ve given through these inexpensive books, materially inexpensive but Spiritually They Are Priceless—They Are True Treasures.

With Love,  
Your Friend,  
Gilbert

Hello,

I would like to express my gratitude for your kindly sending me Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lectures on tape. I find these to be a great source of comfort in times of anxiety and distress. I also thank you very much for kindly sending me the Back to Godhead magazine. I am truly amazed by all the wonderful work that you people are doing for spreading this movement.

Thanking you,  
Ralph

December 29, 2000  
Dear Sura,

It was a pleasure speaking to you today. I was delighted to hear about the tremendous success of last years book distribution program, and I am sure this year will be no less. I am honored to be able to contribute to further the distribution of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *Bhagavad-gita* to as many schools and libraries as possible. Enclosed is a donation of $2,500 that I hope will help this effort.

A. V.  
Your servants at the offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in North America.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

What assurance of real happiness is there in all of one’s wealth, youthfulness, sons, and family members? This life is tottering like a drop of water on a lotus petal; therefore you should always serve and worship the divine feet of Lord Hari. \[“Bhajahū Re Mana,” by Govinda dāsa Kavirāja\]

There is nothing in this material world that stays permanently the same, except the eternal process of change. Every second we witness changes when the ocean of material energy moves and causes the situations around us to alter their face. Eternal time causes great empires and civilizations to rise and again fall into oblivion. Personal relationships come to an end when our dear ones leave us, and ultimately our very bodies vanish as if they had never existed. As spirit souls we hanker for permanent love and happiness. We hope to find that secure state in devotional service and in our spiritual relationships, but sometimes we have to go through cruel tests when the waves of *māyā* separate us from our spiritual guide and we are left bewildered and seemingly alone in the midst of darkness. Where can we find a source of inspiration in a spiritual crisis? Where is our shelter?

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we humbly pray to you to help us. We have in our mind your final pastimes on this planet, when with a weakening voice you dictated the last sentences of your life’s work—the commentated translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Dutifully and lovingly you carried out your mission to the last moments of your life on earth. For you, Vedic literature was life; for us, your example is a life-giving inspiration. You gave us the light of Vedic wisdom in a relatively simple and very understandable fashion. We can always turn our faces toward its light and immediately rise above the hopelessness of material existence.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give your blessings and shelter to those devotees who are trying to maintain the book publishing and distribution mission. Grant them new visions, strength, and courage to go on in your spirit in order to relieve the misery of this earth, which cries under the load of ugly pleasures and sins.
