Your granddisciples at the offices of the Northern European BBT, in Korsnas Gard, Sweden.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Spain)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble *daṇḍavats* in the dust of your lotus feet.

Many years have passed since you first came to me in the form of your books and brought light into the darkness of ignorance that shrouds my poor self.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Ever since, by your infinite mercy, you blessed me by allowing me to remain in contact with your teachings, either by translating, proofreading, correcting, checking fidelity, or publishing them. Thank you for giving me this opportunity.

Sometimes I wonder how it was possible that I had the good fortune to serve you in this capacity. No doubt it was not due to any merit on my part. It can only be attributed to your causeless mercy and your fervent desire to make Lord Caitanya’s glories known to the world—with whatever help available.

You had a vision, and in order to achieve your goal you did not hesitate to make use of even the most useless characters like me. You were like a bee who only looks for nectar among the poisonous blossoms, or like the swan, who can separate milk from water. You knew how to extract gold from even the filthiest of places, and you knew how to fan even the faintest spark of attraction to Kṛṣṇa.

When I was given the service of translating the Second Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the cream of Vedic knowledge, was there anyone more ignorant of Vedic philosophy and *vaiṣṇava-siddhānta* than I? I had just graduated from high school and was, from any angle of vision—spiritually or academically—lacking any qualification to undertake such a sacred task. I had only recently begun to chant the *mahā-mantra,* I did not even live in the temple, and I had not yet received initiation. Still you engaged me in the most sublime and intimate of services. Who can fathom your mercy? You commented during your last days in Vṛndāvana, “I have brought these *mlecchas* and *yavanas*. Accepting those who were sent to me, and considering their qualifications and disqualifications, I tried educating them, just to get things going.”

Now, thirty years later, I am well aware that my translation work was barely suitable to get things going; it was but a feeble attempt to transmit your words in a different language. But your message, although “imperfectly composed” by your servant, still was—and is—effective. This is a miracle and proves the power of spiritual substance.

In those early days, one of the greatest challenges was to find the right balance between “not deviating even an iota” and “preserving the spirit of the message.” Being apprehensive of committing any offenses, I leaned heavily toward “the safe side” of “not deviating,” but literalism undoubtedly obscured the message at times. For this I want to apologize.

To find the right balance between “not deviating” and “entering into the spirit” is still a challenge. It is an on-going and difficult, but unavoidable, challenge. Not only for translators, editors, proofreaders, and publishers, but for every single person who claims or strives to be your follower.

Over the years, my understanding has been shifting from “literal adherence” to “entering into the spirit.” Very early on, you pointed me in this direction, but I was too immature to get the message. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 3.98, you write:

To say nothing of the remnants of food left by a pure Vaiṣṇava, *prasādam* is never polluted even if it is touched by the mouth of a *caṇḍāla*. Indeed, it retains its spiritual value. Therefore by eating or touching such *mahā-prasādam,* a *brāhmaṇa* is not degraded. There is no question of being polluted by touching the remnants of such food. Actually, by eating such *mahā-prasādam,* one is freed from all the contaminations of the material condition. That is the verdict of the *śāstra*.”

A clear and unequivocal statement, it seems. At least I took your words at that time “as they are” and concluded that I would be freed from all the contaminations of material existence by honoring the *prasādam* left by the *caṇḍāla*-like guests after the Sunday feast. After all, such food retained its spiritual value, and a *brāhmaṇa* was not degraded by eating it. In the temples, however, it was customary to throw out such remnants, an offensive practice, I felt, since *prasādam* is considered to be nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Confused by this practice, I consulted you. Your reply was a surprise: “...\[You\] may not be so advanced that you will take the karmi remnants as prasada....” Absolute words were no longer absolute, but rather relativized by taking into account the level of advancement of the person involved.

How many of us see things in this light?

I pray that you will bless me with the power of fine discrimination, so that I will be able to enter into the spirit of your teachings and not fall into the trap of literalism and its ugly cohorts, fundamentalism and fanaticism, and their even uglier by-products, abuse and violence.

History has shown that the greatest atrocities were committed in the name of God and His representatives. May we, your followers, not join the list of such unfortunate fools and rascals.

We aspire to become situated as a particle of dust at your lotus feet.

Your servants at the offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Spain.  
(written by Vedavyāsa dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Avatar Studios

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Avatar Studios is a nonprofit company comprised primarily of disciples of your disciples. We are dedicated to pleasing you by using modern media to present the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the most attractive way possible. Our goal is to help carry on your preaching mission. This year we offer a new project for your pleasure—a video magazine entitled “Innertain TV—Television for the Soul” (formerly called “Hare Krishna Today”).

One of the other ways that we continue to try and please you is to care for your disciples. This year we are fortunate to have one of your dearest disciples, Śyāmasundara Prabhu, write the bulk of our offering:

\* \* \*

O dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

How can tiny words begin to paint this glorious day—  
With colors that must move and radiate across a billion constellations—  
Pinned to this tiny page?

And where will I get such audacity  
to yank the curtain, hail the crowd with pompous gestures  
posing as deep fidelity, and  
once a year proclaim:  
I love and honor you above all others—

(When days, even weeks go by I don’t so much as think of you,  
Engrossed in my life of time, and money,  
Worry and guilt, regret for a life now nearly gone by?)

Surely all can see through this masquerade,  
The balancing act of a fool…

\[*The fool enters:*\]

O Great Swami from the mystic East!

You shouldn’t have met those friends of mine  
In the time of our revolution,  
Or sent them to me glowing with delight and knowing  
Everything there is to know, the long-lost secrets of life!

You shouldn’t have brought them your sky-blue God  
Or spoken or printed His words to convince them—  
And why did you have to walk into my city, open my door,

Glance my way: and with your dazzling smile and the blossoming of an indescribable peace, destroy all other meaning in my life?

Why you just didn’t stay away from me  
And let my young heart fly away with the winds?

\[*The clown sings*: upbeat Chuck Berry-like tempo:\]

Yeah, I made my lists, got my ducks in a row,  
I’m busy with a million excuses—  
The hands on the clock say it’s time to rock,  
And I gotta plan the scams and my ruses.

Now I got no time to be lovin’ you  
Till I tasted all the tooty-fruity juices!

I gotta catch a plane in twenty-seven minutes,  
I gotta go and get a loan,  
The pager in my pocket’s steamin’ like a rocket  
And I gotta find a telephone—

Now I got no time to be lovin’ you  
Till I blow out all my circuits and the fuses!

\[*The balls fall to hoots and hollers; the fool laments:*\]

O Swamiji!

Why did you have to speak my name?  
You shouldn’t have touched my hand,  
You shouldn’t have asked me to sing Kṛṣṇa with you,  
You shouldn’t have asked me to dance!

Each dawn you called me to your room to talk of little things, light ideas, innocent and fresh; mornings I helped you prepare for the fight—and your afternoons flamed with victory; by nights you were sheer delight!

Thus, one by one, the days spun by, each day better than yesterday, when I thought yesterday couldn’t get any better.

You shouldn’t have caused me to love you so! You shouldn’t have made me remember!

\[*The fool removes his mask, and the care-worn face of a fifty-eight-year-old man emerges:*\]

O Prabhupāda!

I grow desperate, desperate to be with you again.

In this room of shadows I’m trying to hear your voice as it really was from a beat-up tape playing faster than it should—I sometimes watch snatches of your life with us on jerky old films—I reminisce of you with my oldest friends who shared some time with you, but each year our aging brains grow more fanciful and the growing gaps get filled by increasing exaggeration—I read your books, and best of all the unedited conversations—I dream of you when I’m very lucky, shoving aside the impedimenta of matter and time, on those ever more rare occasions when I can see and play with you...

Imagining myself now on the stage with you again, hearing you speak:

“Yes, Kṛṣṇa is ready. But the thing is, Kṛṣṇa expects something. Kṛṣṇa is not dead stone. It is reciprocal. If you fully surrender to Kṛṣṇa, then Kṛṣṇa takes full responsibility. If you keep some reservation, Kṛṣṇa also keeps some reservation.”

Sensing now how those reservations have matured to a dark involuntary forgetfulness of you and Kṛṣṇa—the fact behind my desolate condition.

“Shyamsundar, you will speak something now?”  
\[Yipes! In front of a jillion people, what’ll I...\]

Er, uhhumm, O.K., *Nama oṁ* mumble mumble, yes, Your spiritual master is never far from you, he is just here, in your heart, in your mind, standing beside you at all times and in all conditions. It is not difficult to make contact with Prabhupāda, he is very easy to please and become friends. Just you have to give up any reservations, very simple. The problem is, we like this place, this material world too much, so we reserve this much, then this much, then a little bit more, until we forget about Prabhupāda, so he forgets about us—not forgets, he never forgets, but he is a little more distant. Just like you have a name, Prabhupāda has a name—and when you say it, and think of him sincerely, he must come immediately. The trick is to always remember, remember, remember your spiritual master...

Did I say that?

\[*The old man remembers:*\]

O dearest Prabhupāda!

Sitting on the edge of your bed, your *tilaka* smeared and your eyes still glistening and quickly darting from the excitement of tonight’s program (where again you pummeled the disbelievers), one by one and with such grace removing your funny saffron socks, you chuckle a couple of times and say:

“So, Shyamsundar, you did not forget?”

No, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I did not forget:

The overwhelming beauty of your face and mouth, the mobile ease and perfect lines of cheeks and crinkles wrapping around and punctuating another perfect mood; your dancing eyes, now somber, unmoving in thought, now wide and full of mischief and glee!

I remember the soft golden flesh of your shoulder—and the surprising hard tissues below; the flow of blue veins under forearms, behind knees, how they throbbed with a cool even pulse as you lounged in the sun—and roared with power when you danced all day!

I cannot forget how you dressed yourself slowly, keenly observing each cloth before choosing, lifting your thread to drape on your shoulder while you tied and tucked your dhoti with care; nor can I forget your pink fingers at the buttonholes, and how sometimes you leaned forward for me to finish off the one at the neck.

Watching you shave always had me in stitches, and the wide intent eyes and grimaces as you swiveled the mirror to check that your *tilaka* was straight—or to check on a possible loose tooth.

But most of all I remember, dear Master, how somehow you entered my mind: how after awhile there were so few words as we silently padded about your room—I just knew what to do, whatever you wanted, what you wanted to say in your letters I typed—I didn’t have to think anymore.

O dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda!

If I could but remember your every elegant gesture and deed, the perfect delivery of your every word and expression, every movement of your beautiful body, and each incidence of your unbounded intelligence, there would not be days enough in my life, you are so full and comprehensive. May this Vyāsa-pūjā day be only one of every day in the lives of your servants devoted to remembering the vast perfection of your days among us!

Your servants at Avatar Studios, Potomac, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Śyāmasundara dāsa)

## Back to Godhead magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You taught us everything, and your example was always perfect. Today, as we meditate on your divine appearance in this world and consider what we might offer you on this blessed day, we remember the gift you offered Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura on the seventieth anniversary of his appearance:

Then there were other Godbrothers. I consulted \[them\] that “Guru Mahārāja said like this. What can I do?” So they also encouraged me. I was writing. There was a paper, *Harmonist*. Then, by their desires, I started this *Back to Godhead* in 1944. That was also started on his advent day, this advent day. *Back to Godhead* was started. Yes. There was a meeting, and many friends came, and we first started this *Back to Godhead* on his advent day, this advent day, 1944.

We are in awe of such a gift. Given fifty-seven years ago, it continues to deliver Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the English-speaking world, fulfilling the desire of your Guru Mahārāja. What disciple could ever hope to give his spiritual master anything equal to it? We are also awed by our responsibility to carry on a magazine with roots in the liberated guru-*paramparā* and the spiritual world. We don’t know why we have been blessed with this task. We are floundering in this world while trying to reflect the light you shine from a place far from our reach. We can only prostrate ourselves at you sacred feet and pray that you empower us to continue the glorious legacy of *Back to Godhead*.

Your eternal servants at Back to Godhead magazine.

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The ancient Greeks had a saying: “Even the gods strive in vain against stupidity.” This is the true situation in the material world. Due to the laws of karma, people in this Age of Kali are cursed by ignorance from which there is no escape. Even the power of the demigods, or the advances of modern science, are useless against this cosmic arrangement. We are minute, insignificant particles of energy floating helplessly in an ocean of matter, which we struggle to control, without success. We identify ourselves with these bodies, which are temporary engines of pain and suffering. And yet each and every *jīva* soul imagines that he is the lord and master of his universe.

It is indeed wonderful that you are able to overcome the stupidity of the conditioned souls. It is the ability that Kṛṣṇa gives only to His most confidential and dear friends. It is such a difficult task that Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura stated that he would consider his mission successful if even one person became Kṛṣṇa conscious by his preaching. So many persons have become Kṛṣṇa conscious due to your preaching. So many stupid people from every walk of life were enlightened, from the so-called intellectuals to the hippies and worse.

Your mercy is the only qualification for ending stupidity. On this your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration we beg for that mercy. We can only prostrate ourselves before you and pray that somehow we can serve your mission and please you. When the spiritual master is pleased, Kṛṣṇa is pleased and we can develop our true identities as His eternal servants in the spiritual world.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Glorious is your compassion for all the fallen conditioned souls of Kali-yuga. It is due to your compassion that you stayed up sleepless nights writing and translating. Thus in this dark dismal Age of Kali you have made love of God freely available, by Lord Caitanya’s mercy. To chant, dance, and be happy is the formula that you gave us. So simple yet so sublime. Indeed, it has proven to be the perfect cure for our Kali-yuga consciousness. Now, as we at the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture take that medicine and feel a little strong, we are attempting to chant your teachings from the pages of your sublime books.

Despite your physical absence, your books are preaching. Thus far, since our humble beginning ten years ago, 5,212 students internationally have graduated from the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture. So far our courses, all based entirely on your books, have been conducted in Amsterdam, Houston, Canada, Hong Kong, Mauritius, Māyāpur, Nairobi, New Delhi, the Philippines, South Africa, Trinidad, and Guyana. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing us this opportunity to participate in this wonderful mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. On this auspicious day we bow down in the dust of your lotus feet and offer our prayer that you may continue to bless us with the desire, strength, and enthusiasm to assist you in carrying out your mission, which is the same as Lord Caitanya’s: “Wherever you go, whomever you meet, tell them about Kṛṣṇa.” We also beg your blessings that this program may become more and more ecstatic and successful, according to your desire and the desire of the entire disciplic succession.

Recently in Māyāpur your dear disciple Dhanañjaya Prabhu mentioned that he had heard you say that “Even if all of the temples close, if one person who has my books reads them he will be able to start this whole Hare Kṛṣṇa movement again.” From this statement we can understand how immense is the power of your teachings. Therefore you suggested on January 10, 1976, that we write examinations on the various books you have written. You once said, “My only criticism of the devotees is that they think my books are for distribution and not for reading.” Thus you indicated to us that you wanted us to fix our intelligence, preach, and purify our hearts through śāstric study. In your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 18.68you write, “Anyone, however, who tries sincerely to present *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is will advance in devotional activities and reach the pure devotional state of life. As a result of such pure devotion, he is sure to go back home, back to Godhead.” Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the formula to perfect our existence and for showing by your exemplary life how to achieve it.

Begging to remain eternally in your service, we are  
Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, Johannesburg, South Africa.  
(written by Mādrī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Cultural and Educational Library

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Thanks to your kind mercy and the prayers of your devoted disciple, His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja, we (a small committee of devotees here in Rome) wish to offer at your lotus feet the Bhaktivedanta Cultural and Educational Library.

This project is the fruit of years of cultivating friendship within the municipal administration of Rome, which culminated in our participation in an ongoing interreligious teaching program in the public school system. For two years around 12,000 students age 13 to 18 have attended didactic lessons on the Hindu/Vedic and Bhāgavata Culture as part of their regular curriculum. Our proposal to establish a library where the aforesaid students might further their study of the vast knowledge afforded by the *Vedas* and, above all, gain eternal benefit from Your Divine Grace’s commentaries was finally satisfied when the city administration awarded us a wonderful space in downtown Rome.

With your blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the possibilities are unlimited for preaching here in Rome, and a library such as we wish to create could serve to enhance the brilliance of your irreplaceable ISKCON. With the collaboration of qualified devotees, we plan to offer courses such as Bhakti Śāstrī, Mantra and Spiritual Technology, and Science and Philosophy of Life, along with various cultural programs and a transcendental exhibition gallery, all intended for the academic sphere eminent in Rome. And of course there will be distribution of *bhāgavata-prasādam* in the adjoining “Āyurvedic Tea Room.”

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless our humble contribution to the cultural revolution you speak of in your books.

Your aspiring servants at the Bhaktivedanta Cultural and Educational Library, in Rome, Italy.  
(written by Ekādaśī-nātha dāsa and Trisandhyā-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The other morning when I was in the temple room I saw you, who spread the names of Kṛṣṇa. I, who am insignificant compared to you, offered my obeisances to you. When I saw your face, which is brighter than millions of moons, I felt closer to you. I felt happy when I saw your face. I am begging for your mercy.

Your servant,  
Haṁsa, 10 (from USA)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you can give us Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. I love you so much. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy can make us go with you. Your mercy is the same as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. You are a pure devotee. I love you the same as Kṛṣṇa.

Keśava, 7 (from Nepal)  
The Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School, Vṛndāvana, India.

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley and Mumbai)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your lotus feet, by whose association all realizations come for preaching to the scientists.

Mental speculators strain their minds and their bodies to get realizations about the impersonal nature of the Absolute Truth. You taught that the highest realization of the Absolute Truth is Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Devotees of Lord Caitanya/Lord Kṛṣṇa do not have to perform severe austerities to get spiritual realizations.

At the Bhaktivedanta Institute we are especially expecting that Lord Caitanya and Your Divine Grace will reveal to us how to preach to the scientists in such a way that they will get Lord Caitanya’s mercy and become devotees. On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, we fervently pray that Your Divine Grace will reveal more and more of what to say to the scientists or to write in the scientific literature. We pray that Your Divine Grace will ask Lord Caitanya to bestow His mercy upon the scientists and philosophers. Why shouldn’t the Lord bestow His mercy upon them? Lord Caitanya bestowed his mercy upon such scholars as Keśava Kashmiri and Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya. Of course, a few scientists will refuse to accept the Lord’s mercy.

In the 1970’s you taught many techniques of how to preach to scholars, and you gave the fundamental principles of material nature, life, and consciousness that science is in ignorance about. In recent years, we feel that you have revealed more. In particular, we think Your Divine Grace is now trying to bring a new theistic science into the scientific world. This new theistic science of Lord Caitanya and the Golden Age, a science that has its roots in the teachings of Lord Kapiladeva (especially *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26), will be a science in the modern sense of the term. This science is beginning to come into focus under the name of the “relational paradigm.” Your BI scientist Rasarāja dāsa has boldly articulated these ideas in his articles in *The Journal of Consciousness Studies* (Nov/Dec 1999 issue) and in *Philosophy in Review* (Vol. 20, no. 6). We fervently pray to Your Divine Grace to reveal this new science more and more.

Your aspiring servants at your Bhaktivedanta Institute branches in Berkeley, California, and Mumbai, India.

## Bhaktivedanta Rāja-vidyālaya

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you and Lord Gaurāṅga!

It is because of your direct and specific instructions to us to preach the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā* on an institutional level that this missionary school project is going on so very well. It was started in June of 1990 with only thirty small children, but now we have three hundred, and more are coming. They study in English with a daily curriculum of four hours of Kṛṣṇa conscious programs, from *maṅgala-ārati* to evening *ārati*. The rest of the time they learn government-accredited academics. The residential children are markedly different from their peers who simply attend the academic portion of the school. Still, the latter cannot remain unaffected by the Kṛṣṇa conscious vibrations on the campus. And what to speak of their parents? They highly appreciate your explanations of *raja-vidyā,* especially as contained in your purport to the second verse of the Ninth Chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā,*

*rāja-vidyā rāja-guhyaṁ pavitram idam uttamam*  
*pratyakṣāvagamaṁ dharmyaṁ su-sukhaṁ kartum avyayam*

The parents see that you have presented, on both a competitive and comparative basis, the real meaning of education and how and where to achieve it.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your style is very much distinct from that of the rest of the world. You are the real educator, and we are obliged by your mercy. You are more magnanimous than Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu because you bestowed your mercy upon the whole world, and therefore we are trying to follow in your footsteps and execute your order to the best of our ability. I hope this will be the perfection of our lives.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, since you are empowered by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we never feel you are no longer with us. This is also your causeless mercy upon us. I trust that this is the glimpse into eternal life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for which we are always anxious. Finally, we pray at your lotus feet that your mercy may be spread all over the world, and that you will accept our humble efforts in this exalted mission.

Your eternal servants at the Bhaktivedanta Rāja-vidyālaya, Surat, India.

## College Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

A glorious day it is to remember you in the presence of our lives, as we continue the college traveling *saṅkīrtana-yajña* throughout North America. Before taking shelter of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and Your Divine Grace, many of us were attending one of these colleges. By your mercy and by the mercy of your devotees, when we came in contact with your books our enthusiasm to pursue mundane education greatly waned, and the hope and inspiration to cultivate spiritual life became a promising reality. We saw that we need no longer romanticize about spiritual ideals but could actually live them. The disenchantment we felt toward mundane education is nicely expressed in your purports to the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

Generally, people are not educated in this confidential knowledge; they are educated in external knowledge....There are so many departments of knowledge all over the world and many huge universities, but there is, unfortunately, no university or educational institution where the science of the spirit soul is instructed. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 9.2, purport\]

Ordinary knowledge obtained by a university education pertains only to matter, and it is not accepted here as knowledge....In modern education there is no knowledge about spirit; they are simply taking care of the material elements and bodily needs. Therefore academic knowledge is not complete. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 10.4–5, purport\]

Thus on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā we reflect on the great fortune of having you as the *senapati* (commander in chief) of our humble *saṅkīrtana* party. We express our gratitude for the treasure of *rāja-vidyā* that you have given to us by inviting the students and friends we meet to buy your books and take shelter of your Bhaktivedanta purports. The *saṅkīrtana-yajña* of preaching and book distribution also invites us to experience your presence in a dynamic manner. And what is that experience? It is to be a humble instrument in the presence of a conditioned soul coming in contact with Kṛṣṇa through your books, the holy names, and *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. We learn from your example how a Vaiṣṇava lives not for himself but for others, and we gratefully experience a drop of this magical mood that you so purely embodied.

In one letter you stated; “Actually, in every town and city there are many, many devotees of Kṛṣṇa; now it is our business to go around the world wherever people are congregating and pick up these sincere souls.” Thus we appeal to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you continue allowing us to serve you by “picking up souls” as we travel and thus expanding the revolution of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* mission.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your servants in the College Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party, USA.  
(written by Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

## Festival of India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

At every festival, it’s wonderful to see people becoming purified and transcendentally inspired by the timeless wisdom you have given the world. Many people walking by hear the *kīrtana,* and soon they are enjoying some *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and at the end of the day they leave with a book in their hands.

We may not see these people for years, but with your transcendental books you have planted in their hearts the seeds of devotion, and eventually they sprout. Many people have heard about you, or read one of your books, and that’s enough to change their lives.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.” \[*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **19.151\]**

Toward the end of your purport to this verse you write:

Dormant devotional service to Kṛṣṇa is within everyone. Simply by associating with devotees, hearing their good instructions, and chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, one awakens his dormant love for Kṛṣṇa. In this way one acquires the seed of devotional service.

All the different programs you have established—temples, farms, *saṅkīrtana* parties, the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, *Back to Godhead* magazine, Govinda’s restaurants, Ratha-yātrā and other transcendental festivals—all are giving conditioned souls the opportunity to associate with devotees and receive the mercy of Guru and Kṛṣṇa.

Just remembering a fraction of your divine service amazes us. You are still giving your association through your books and changing the lives of those who read them. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Begging to become your sincere disciples, we remain  
Your servants who take care of your Festival of India, USA.

## The International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, our eternal spiritual master!

We who have eaten the meat of the cow have been transformed into protectors of the cow through the gift of your transcendental instructions and knowledge. Is there any greater wonder? Your words are so powerful! They have transformed our minds and hearts and given us the strength to change our lives. Please, through your ceaseless mercy let us remember your words emphasizing the importance of cow protection. Let us consciously understand and absorb their truth every moment, every day of our lives:

When we offer our obeisances to the Personality of Godhead, we say:

*namo brahmaṇya-devāya go-brāhmaṇa-hitāya ca*  
*jagad-dhitāya kṛṣṇāya govindāya namo namaḥ*

When Kṛṣṇa comes to establish real perfection in the social order, He personally gives protection to the cows and the brāhmaṇas (go-brāhmaṇa-hitāya ca). This is His first interest because without protection of the brāhmaṇas and the cows, there can be no human civilization and no question of happy, peaceful life. Asuras, therefore, are always interested in killing the brāhmaṇas and cows. Especially in this age, Kali-yuga, cows are being killed all over the world, and as soon as there is a movement to establish brahminical civilization, people in general rebel. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.4.41, purport\]

His first business is to give all comfort to the cows and the brāhmaṇas. In fact, comfort for the brāhmaṇas is secondary, and comfort for the cows is His first concern. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.8.16, purport\]

Without protection of cows, brahminical culture cannot be maintained; and without brahminical culture, the aim of life cannot be fulfilled. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.24.5, purport\]

Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, by His personal example, taught us the importance of cow protection, which is meant not only for the Indian climate but for all human beings all over the universe. \[Light of the Bhāgavata 27\]

The Lord is the protector of cows and the brahminical culture. A society devoid of cow protection and brahminical culture is not under the direct protection of the Lord, just as the prisoners in the jails are not under the protection of the king but under the protection of a severe agent of the king. Without cow protection and cultivation of the brahminical qualities in human society, at least for a section of the members of society, no human civilization can prosper at any length. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.14.34, purport\]

The Lord said, “You drink cows’ milk; therefore the cow is your mother. And the bull produces grains for your maintenance; therefore he is your father.” \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Ādi 17.153, translation\]

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the beginning it was enough to be enlightened to the truth. It was enough to be removed from the grossness of meat-eating. How happy we were to have the window of transcendental life opened to us! But we remain here in the material world and must find a way to serve you and your words of truth. Among those words are instructions how to protect the cow here in the material world.

Protection and grazing ground for the cows are among the essential needs for society and the welfare of people in general. \[*Light of the Bhāgavata* 27\]

For the time being, if you actually want to develop such ideal asrama, we must have sufficient land, and all other things will gradually grow. For raising crops from the land, how many men will be required—that we must estimate and for herding the cows and feeding them. \[Letter to Hayagrīva dasa, 14 June 1968\]

We must have sufficient pasturing ground to feed the animals all round. We have to maintain the animals throughout their life. We must not make any program for selling them to the slaughterhouses. That is the way of cow protection. Krishna by His practical example taught us to give all protection to the cows and that should be the main business of New Vrindaban. Vrindaban is also known as Gokula. Go means cows, and kula means congregation. Therefore the special feature of New Vrindaban will be cow protection, and by doing so, we shall not be loser. \[Letter to Hayagrīva dasa, 14 June 1968\]

The intelligent class of men, the *brāhmaṇas,* are therefore especially responsible for broadcasting this knowledge of our relationship with the Lord and leading the general mass of people to the right path. The administrative class is meant for protecting the living beings so that they can serve this purpose; the mercantile class is meant for producing food grains and distributing them to the complete human society so that the whole population is given a chance to live comfortably and discharge the duties of human life. The mercantile class is also required to give protection to the cows in order to get sufficient milk and milk products, which alone can give the proper health and intelligence to maintain a civilization perfectly meant for knowledge of the ultimate truth. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.5.37, purport\]

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please do not let us slip back into the darkness of ignorance. Let us be diligent protectors of the most defenseless creatures. Your words remind us of the consequences of hearing the truth and then neglecting it.

TRANSLATION

The brāhmaṇas, the cows and the defenseless creatures are My own body. Those whose faculty of judgment has been impaired by their own sin look upon these as distinct from Me. They are just like furious serpents, and they are angrily torn apart by the bills of the vulturelike messengers of Yamarāja, the superintendent of sinful persons.

PURPORT

The defenseless creatures, according to *Brahma-saṁhitā,* are the cows, *brāhmaṇas,* women, children and old men. Of these five, the *brāhmaṇas* and cows are especially mentioned in this verse because the Lord is always anxious about the benefit of the *brāhmaṇas* and the cows and is prayed to in this way. The Lord especially instructs, therefore, that no one should be envious of these five, especially the cows and *brāhmaṇas*. In some of the *Bhāgavatam* readings, the word *duhitṝḥ* is used instead of *duhatīḥ*. But in either case, the meaning is the same. *Duhatīḥ* means “cow,” and *duhitṝḥ* can also be used to mean “cow” because the cow is supposed to be the daughter of the sun-god. Just as children are taken care of by the parents, women as a class should be taken care of by the father, husband, or grown-up son. Those who are helpless must be taken care of by their respective guardians; otherwise the guardians will be subjected to the punishment of Yamarāja, who is appointed by the Lord to supervise the activities of sinful living creatures. The assistants, or messengers, of Yamarāja are likened here to vultures, and those who do not execute their respective duties in protecting their wards are compared to serpents. Vultures deal very seriously with serpents, and similarly the messengers will deal very seriously with neglectful guardians. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.16.10\]

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You paint the most enchanting picture with your transcendental artist’s skills, your words. They inspire and show us the ideal spiritual world, in which cow protection is an integral part of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s life.

He is always with cows, and how the cows look very happy with Kṛṣṇa. And Kṛṣṇa is personally teaching how to protect cows. He became a cowherd boy. He was king’s son, Mahārāja Nanda; but His business was to take the cows and the calves daily to the pasturing ground. And it was very sportive engagement with the cowherd boys. The cows were grazing, and the boys, they took their meals in a pot, tiffin carrier. Not tiffin carrier in those days. Some way or other. And they used to eat them, distribute amongst the friends. Sometimes a tiffin carrier was stolen by one boy, and he was searching, and then it was...So just like the boys do. This was the children’s life, to take protection, to give protection to the cows, to the calves. The small children, up to six years, seven years old, they used to take care of the calves, and the elderly men, the used to take care of the...Or elderly boys, they used to take care of the grown-up cows. So the cows were fed very nicely. Vrajān. Therefore Vṛndāvana is called Vrajabhūmi, “where there are many cows.” It is called Gokula. Gokula. Go means “cows,” and kula means “group.” Gokula. Govardhana. Govardhana Hill. Because the cows were grazing on the hill, and profuse grass was being grown, and they are enjoying. So there should be arrangement. Just like here we see, there are so many open fields and the cows are grazing. But they cannot be happy because they know that they are simply raised for being killed. They cannot be happy.

So our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is not a sentimental movement. It takes care of all-round social organization. It is not something like religious sentiment. Everything should be taken care of. Therefore we say cow protection, cow protection. \[Lecture, London, 25 November 1973\]

Your servants at The International Society for Cow Protection.

## ISKCON Public Relations and Communications (Mauritius)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The ISKCON Public Relations and Communications (IPR & C) office of Mauritius has the delicate task of preserving the image of ISKCON in Mauritius.

Since the founding of the IPR&C more than two years ago, we have known many difficulties: the effort to maintain our finances; the effort to retain devotees as members of the IPR&C; the fight against McDonalds, which wanted to open one of their horrific restaurants next to our temple; the effort actions to counteract the negative image caused by of the child abuse case; and many others.

All these tasks seemed impossible for us. Yet due to your mercy, the services we rendered achieved positive results. The only forces that gives us the courage to move ahead are your instructions and your personal example. The word “impossible” exists only in a fool’s dictionary.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although our intention is to render service to you and your movement, we depend completely on you to help us execute this service. We know that any successes achieved by the IPR & C are nothing but extensions of your mercy.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at ISKCON’s Public Relations & Communications office in Mauritius.  
(written by D. Khoodeeram)

## ISKCON School, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

“Your mercy is all we are made of,” we sing, and it is a simple truth. Our school, our lives, and our goals are all products of the compassion you showed by traveling around the world, patiently and repeatedly explaining and exemplifying both the simple and the esoteric truths of spiritual life.

Please continue to bless us so that all the children ISKCON School serves can be successful in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and also become even materially the first-class citizens you envisioned. Let your vision of education be our vision, and give us the strength to continue day after day.

Your servants at ISKCON School, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.

## ISKCON Television

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

ISKCON Television (ITV) has continued to preserve your legacy and broadcast your glories through the evolving technologies. Since the auspicious event of your last Vyāsa-pūjā, ITV, in partnership with the North American BBT, has released a double CD multimedia experience, *Ask Śrīla Prabhupāda*. Installed on almost any computer, the CDs enable the participant to select from a variety of spiritual subject matters, review a list of relevant questions, and choose one—and lo and behold! you appear on screen like a luminous sun and give your transcendental answer. Then the person can link to the text and read it. We hope that this direct interaction will allow the users to have a glimpse of the magic of your personal association. Children and teenagers and countless others who never saw you personally will have an opportunity to experience you more directly. Everyone can now feel your gravity and warmth, your insight and humor, your conviction and mercy—your love.

In addition, ITV has finally completed the translation of the movie *Rāmānujācārya* and made it available to the English-speaking world. You encouraged us to preach the glories of the Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas,* especially those who established the principles of *bhakti* in a world of voidism and Māyāvādism. Without you, we would not even be aware of some of these awesome personalities to whom we are eternally indebted for having paved the road of devotion to Kṛṣṇa for all of the fallen, conditioned souls. I am the most fallen.

But you have lifted me up by offering this wonderful opportunity of serving the community of Vaiṣṇavas. In 1972 you wrote a letter containing advice that has sustained me in times of doubt and confusion:

By all means we must preserve our spiritual status, that is the point, not that we are mad after big buildings, many devotees, life-members, this, that—no, these are only ways to engage the devotees so that they may apply the principles of devotional living to some kind of work for practical realization of these principles. \[Letter to Tejiyas dāsa, 19 December 1972\]

I understand that by your mercy you have allowed me to engage in this work so that I may make some little progress on the *bhakti-mārga*. I am eternally indebted to you for this chance of many lifetimes. Please continue to engage me. We can only go on by your mercy, and we see it daily.

We beg to remain  
Your servants at ISKCON Television.  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa)

## ISKCON Youth Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This year’s ISKCON Youth Ministry homage was written by two devotee youth sisters in New Zealand, Subhadrā Gopālī-devī dāsī and Nitya Līlā Dāsī, representing a glimpse of the youth spirit and our feelings for you.

\* \* \*

Haribol dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I have come to a very special time in my life, and I feel it couldn’t have happened without your profound wisdom throughout my whole upbringing as a devotee. You are the continuation of my soul, and I pray every day to have you guide me. Without fail, you are always there in everything I go through. I experience great joy after each day. I will never be able to fully express my love for you down here, as nothing on this planet can show how great my love for you is. I will always feel this way. Thank you for letting me know such love. Everything you have taught me is used largely; without it all, I hesitate to imagine what my life would have been like. Without Śrī Kṛṣṇa and you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I feel life might have been much harder. But with both your love and light I am able to live happily, full of joy and love for you and Kṛṣṇa. I look forward to the day when we meet in Goloka Vṛndāvana. But until then I enjoy my days here and thank you for such an experience.

Love always, dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Eternally your loving servant,  
Subhadrā Gopālī-devī dāsī

\* \* \*

A ROCKIN’ SRILA PRABHUPADA! “That’s ridiculous!” I hear you say. “Oh, yes, he rocks! Without a doubt,” I reply without delay. Just take a moment, c’mon, hear me through. Put on your 3D glasses, wait for my cue. Prepare for Ratha-yātrā. Feel the fun, the stares...“Hey lady, that’s only normal. These Hari’s sure don’t join up to be formal!”

Follow me. Some gurukulis are swinging *karatālas* on an autumn day. Lord Jagannātha is cruisin’ His Boy Racer outfit, you might say. Devotees jumpin’, crowds bumpin’, the mood’s pumpin’! “Here man, have some sweets, come shave up and join the club.” Some take the nectar and say “Yum!” Others, “I’m cool, lady, I’m late for the pub!” A group of dudes and dudettes see the fun and ask to play. “Sure! Come pull the cart and join the fray.”

With an intellectual leap of thought, one cautiously poses the question: “You guys are cool, but what are you on?! Is there some secret mysticism?” Yep, I know, you’ve heard it before, but the truth is, we’ve tasted Kṛṣṇa’s mercy—there’s no pill or cure. So, in answer to this serious question I ask Śrī Kṛṣṇa for help (Oh, thank you, on goes the light!): “The reason we have all of this is because of one special person; he’s just behind you, to the right. See that ‘dude’ sitting at the front of the Ratha cart today?” “Yeah?” “Well, that’s our Śrīla Prabhupāda, showing the way!”

Tricky questions posed, tentative explanations followed. (Thank you Kṛṣṇa, for that light!) “What do you think, babe, could these guys be right?!” “Umm, I don’t know, let me think,” dudette replies. (I can almost hear the tick, tock.) “But there’s one thing I do know: good grub, cool music, runnin’ clothes man—these dudes ROCK!” Yeah! That’s true, I realized. I’d never given it much thought. Headlines, stardom, it flashed up quickly. Oh no, that way is fraught! *Śikṣāṣṭakam* prayers come to mind. “Humbler than the straw in the street”...

Yes, I remember, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I rest at your feet. I may do the service, but it’s not me that holds the ace card. The reason I have Śrī Kṛṣṇa is because of you, our Rockin’ Śrīla Prabhupāda! In loving dedication to my savior father, Śrīla Prabhupāda, showing me the way, I pray always for your mercy and guidance in helping me on my journey to Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Please help this fallen soul.

Your servant, Nitya Līlā Dāsī

\* \* \*

Your servants at the ISKCON Youth Ministry.

## Israel Druze Project

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We bow to your lotus feet and pray that you accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and you, Their faithful follower!

Everyone knows that this tiny nation of Israel is a focal point for a troubled planet. But only the great storehouse of Vedic knowledge that you opened for all of us can fully explain why, for it details the grief and suffering that proliferate from blind material consciousness. And it is sadly ironic that here, where several major religious traditions began, developed, and still claim historical title, only some new spiritual knowledge can keep them from vicious material conflict brought on by completely false bodily identification. The hope of that is possible only because of your unending effort and mercy, and in your name and the holy name, the devotees here will continue to try to be accurate and convincing conductors of that knowledge.

The Druze are a practical people with enough of their own scriptural knowledge to understand the madness of this conflict between Muslims and Jews, Palestinians and Israelis. Though Israeli citizens, they understand the Arabic culture and mind frame. They see the blindness on each side and feel the suffering of each. They have been more and more welcoming to the devotees, Lord Kṛṣṇa, and the bottomless wisdom of the Vedic *śāstras*. And through your mercy we hope to bring more and more of your books, your universal tolerance, Lord Kṛṣṇa’s healing and purifying *prasādam,* and devotee counsel to them and their families throughout the Middle East, introducing a new perspective for tolerance and peace that fortifies the truths in their own scriptural tradition.

This coming month \[March\] an enthused delegation of eleven Druze elders and leaders will be touring the three great holy *dhāmas* (Vṛndāvana, Māyāpur, and Jagannātha Purī) with two members of our project and many other senior devotees as guides. This will immeasurably expand their hearts and their consciousness, and that mercy is all pouring from you. You are our example and our foundation. You have brought so much knowledge and hope from this great *paramparā* and introduced its healing potencies in so many parts of the world. And we pray that this influence through the somewhat impartial culture of the Druze will begin to heal this most wretched, fear-filled, and furious conflict.

This helps us to understand that so many who don’t even know of you yet have so much to be grateful for. You literally wanted to serve everyone, and that we must never forget.

Your humble servants at the Druze Project in Osafia , Israel

## Mayapur Institute of Higher Education

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

Your society is now facing its biggest challenges since your departure for the spiritual world. These challenges have arisen because your followers have made mistakes, particularly in leading and managing your movement, in protecting women and children, and in perpetuating the *paramparā*.

Of course, as a society we have learned lessons “through the school of hard knocks,” but to move in a progressive direction and safeguard ourselves from making similar mistakes again, we need extensive training and education for all ISKCON members.

The Māyāpur Institute of Higher Education is anxious to develop general devotee training and specialized training for many of the services that ISKCON devotees perform, but to move ahead we need the involvement and support of your leaders. We then feel we can make a substantial contribution in strengthening your movement.

For instance, devotees assuming leadership and management positions should not have to individually “reinvent the wheel” in executing their responsibilities. There is a wealth of information available from you and other experts in this field. With the cooperation of qualified devotees, we can develop strong Kṛṣṇa conscious curricula and then arrange training for many of your leaders when they come to Māyāpur once a year for the GBC meetings. We feel quite strongly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that such training will enable your leaders to more effectively perform their duties in less time with less stress, and in stronger Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda we are working cooperatively with other devotee educators to fulfill your long-cherished desire for systematic śāstric study leading to Bhakti-śāstri, Bhakti-vaibhava, Bhaktivedānta, and Bhakti-sārvabhauma degrees. With the wide-scale implementation of these study programs, we hope to see brahminical culture flourish within ISKCON.

We are anxious to develop devotee training unlimitedly. Please give us your blessings and empower us to make a contribution in this area.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all of your wonderful gifts, and in particular the gift of being able to serve within your society.

Your servants at the Māyāpur Institute of Higher Education.

## Ministry of Gṛhastha Development

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet!

We miss you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Feeling your heart-melting presence at your *samādhi,* we beg to stay eternally connected to your personal service. Relief only comes through your soul-sustaining instructions.

We miss you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

Daily practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness reveals new truths reverberating through our lives.

In gratitude we:

Remember your lion-like courage and fatherly compassion!  
Remember your rock-solid determination and *gulabjamun*-like humility!  
Remember your golden purity and your stark simplicity!  
Forever we will remember your childlike adore-ability!

We miss you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

When we were lost, you found us! When our spirits lagged, you enlivened us! When Māyā beckoned to us, you stood by, ever inviting us to share your enthusiasm for Kṛṣṇa’s holy names! It is only due to your grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we know anything about Kṛṣṇa!

We miss you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You are the light at the end of our tunnel-like existence, guiding us forward, inspiring us onward, reviving us when we are weary. You stand as a beacon lighting our darkness and inviting us home, back to Godhead.

In gratitude we:

Remember your smile.  
Remember your grace.  
Remember your enthusiasm.  
Remember your face!

Still we miss you , Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please, dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, we beg you, please let us see Śrīla Prabhupāda’s face and serve his beautiful lotus feet again!

Begging for mercy, we remain  
Your fallen servants at the Ministry of Gṛhastha Development.  
(written by Śrutakīrti dāsa and Kuśā-devī dāsī)

## Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Every year at this time an internal assessment takes place within all our hearts. How are we doing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness? What can we all do to please you more? After all, this is the success formula for spiritual life. We are in the jailhouse of this material world and all of us should be greatly concerned to get out of this suffering condition. As individuals we should be maintaining good *sādhana* with sincerity, and as a community, as a whole, we should be trying to implement your instructions on farming communities. There are cows being protected here and enough milk for almost everyone; some of the bulls are being periodically used for plowing, and almost every household has a garden of some form or another every year. Some of the devotees here are running a restaurant in a nearby city, there is a weekly program for distributing *prasādam* and preaching to mostly students at a nearby university, your books are being distributed here and there in this area regularly, and a temple/school complex is being constructed. Of course, faults can be found here, and of course they should be corrected, but if one has an optimistic attitude, in a lot of ways your disciples here are endeavoring to please you in a vast variety of ways. All we can do is pray to you to provide us with the guidance to increase and improve in all the ways we are trying to serve and please you. Please inspire us within all our hearts to act purely and dynamically in your service.

Your servants at Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA.  
(written by Śauri dāsa)

## Rūpānuga Vedic College

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories unto you.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance in this world, we are happy to show our unlimited appreciation for what you have done for us. Just imagine where we would be if you hadn’t come. Many of us would have continued with sinful activities, and many would have continued with so-called spiritual searching, which is nothing but nicely packaged sense gratification...unless the result of that search is acceptance of a bona fide spiritual master. And look at all these so-called spiritual masters who were writing commentaries on the *Bhagavad-gītā,* covering its real meaning.

Without you we are lost.

Many Indians are now accepting the Western style of life. So we clearly see that without your heroic determination to serve the will of your spiritual master, we would have wasted our lives. We can learn from your life that accepting of the will of one’s spiritual master and following that will with all one’s heart is the most intelligent thing to do. Only then will our lives be full of meaning and successful. It doesn’t matter if it is hard or easy, successful or a failure, or whether we like it or not. If we do that, then Kṛṣṇa is pleased.

Your servants at the Rūpānuga Vedic College in Kansas City, Missouri, USA.  
(written by Urukrama dāsa)

## The Saraswati Charitable Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your inconceivable compassion for all of the suffering conditioned souls!

As time relentlessly moves on, one reality becomes more and more in focus with the passage of each year: Your glories only become greater, and our fallen state only becomes clearer! How you managed to move forward so successfully, on so many fronts simultaneously, with such astounding determination (you indeed “sweated blood”) and perfect spiritual purity—despite the bungling mistakes, confusion, nonsense, and endless in-fighting of your disciples—is simply beyond all understanding. It simply cannot be explained materially. Especially when we can now see, with the benefit of more than two decades of hindsight, how incredibly difficult it is simply to maintain what you gave us. The question is not “How could you be—” but “How could you *not* be a *śaktyāveṣa-avatāra*?!”

Still, you are so endlessly kind to us. Your mercy itself—causeless, oceanic—makes us weep when we can minutely comprehend what is really happening. And what is happening is that you are refusing to stop your flow of pure mercy despite our fallen nature and utter inability to really please you in any consistent manner. That in spite of it all, you keep appearing in so many amazing ways: always encouraging, always revealing the reality of Kṛṣṇa’s presence in our hearts. Making us realize that we really do have an eternal, blissful home with the most amazing, loving friend, son, paramour who will never abandon us. If we could only wake up!

Thank you for the opportunity to serve you in some insignificant way by supporting transcendental book distribution and *prasādam* distribution projects dear to your heart. Please give us the intelligence and ability to increase this more and more, and to someday really bring you some happiness. Please let us always be in the blessed association of mature, sincere devotees. And please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, don’t ever stop your flow of mercy. How else will we reach the pure consciousness needed to actually bring that broad smile to your lotus face? How else will we ever please Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava? Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are truly lost!

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!!!

Your servants at The Saraswati Charitable Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## South Budapest, Hungary Preaching Center

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you on the occasion of your appearance day!

Unfortunately, we sometimes forget to glorify, you although this is most important. We just think about our own desires plans and about realizing our projects, but we forget that if you hadn’t gone to the West and given Lord Caitanya’s mercy, we would only be among the many drowning souls in the ocean of material existence. We would never have heard of Gaura Pūrṇimā or Janmāṣṭamī. Where we live there wouldn’t be any temples, *mūrtis*, centers, farms, restaurants, or devotees. All of these are only expansions of your desire, transmitted to us by your sincere disciples. We should always be grateful for them.

What you have done and are doing continuously is more than we could ever comprehend or repay in any way. So instead of big words or promises, we would like to show our gratefulness by deeds.

In your books you have described what you want from us, and we just have to follow your instructions and success is guaranteed.

We promise we will try to stop being an obstacle for you in your mission to fulfill the desires of your spiritual master, the *guru-paramparā,* and Lord Caitanya, so that everyone can get Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your insignificant servants at the South Budapest Preaching Center and Govinda’s Restaurant, Budapest, Hungary.

## Śrī Mayapur Project Development Committee

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Because you chose to carry on,  
Today I rose before the dawn.  
Beads in hand, I made my way  
To the temple room to bow and pray.

In Māyāpur, this blessed place,  
*Pūjārīs* blew the conch today  
And pulled the curtains to the side.  
A thousand sets of Vaiṣṇava eyes

Turned to Rādhā, dressed to please  
Mādhav, garland to His knees.  
Ten thousand Vaiṣṇava fingertips  
Rose for Them as Vaiṣṇava lips

Sang out sacred Vaiṣṇava prayers,  
And other Vaiṣṇavas everywhere  
Gladly kept the same routine,  
As you alone had once foreseen.

How many lives have been restored  
With praise of Śrī Vṛndāvan’s Lord?  
How many cries of “Nitāi-Gaur!”  
In days to come, how many more?

It’s all the shadow of one man,  
Where scriptures urge the wise to stand.  
You kindly take and keep command  
Of those who serve the best they can.

Your servants at the Śrī Māyāpur Project Development Committee.  
(written by Kālakaṇṭha dāsa)

## Tauranga, Bay of Plenty, New Zealand.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Prabhupāda, our dear founder, you’re the savior of the world.  
Many years have passed since your wondrous pastimes were beheld.  
Unsurpassed feats that have altered the course of history.  
With the Lord your guiding hand, you displayed the greatest mastery.

Another may think what you did to be not so significant,  
But isn’t illusion, suffering, and death such an awful predicament?  
There are those who don’t acknowledge—still, who can say  
How much the Lord’s causeless mercy has unknowingly changed their own way?

As *ācārya,* your example is paramount for all of us,  
Your life of trials and tests, and how you pushed on regardless.  
Not one to be disappointed, you remained a fixed servant.  
You gave us the outlook “Enthusiasm, confident endeavor, and patience.”

Then, at a ripe age, avenues opened for you to meet your Guru’s requests,  
And you glorified the whole *paramparā* with your tremendous success.  
How ecstatic you must have been to see the amazing results  
As our illusory picture faltered under your constant assaults!

Unmatched philosophy coupled with you, the living proof;  
Who could deny Lord Kṛṣṇa to be the Absolute Truth?  
You won over our hearts with your incomparable example.  
Your special mercy was that through you the spiritual world we could sample.

Showing us real love and compassion, respect and integrity,  
Humility, forgiveness, Godliness, arising from genuine spirituality.  
Our goal was satisfaction and peace, which you had in abundance;  
We too could become like you through the Hare Kṛṣṇa experience.

Here in Tauranga there’s a group with our focus being your mission—  
Diverse individuals and plans, all fitting within your broad vision.  
Learning to live and grow together, fulfilling all our needs,  
We feel positive with our efforts as we are propagating your seeds.

There’s much to do and little time, all we can do is but try,  
Keeping Kṛṣṇa-centered, with the proper heart’s desire.  
If He is pleased, He may make us dance in our humble attempts,  
And at the end, if we remember Him, it will be a benediction God-sent.

We thank you, dear Prabhupāda, for everything you’ve given;  
It’s solely by your mercy that our lives are worth living.  
We pray for your blessings, for without them there’s no use;  
And with them anything is possible, and there’ll be no excuse!

Begging for the shelter of your divine lotus feet, we remain  
Your eternally indebted servants in Tauranga, Bay of Plenty, New Zealand.

## Vaishnava Academy for Girls

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, here at the Vaishnava Academy we’re a team. We study together, chant together, distribute your books together, and try, somehow or other, to serve your lotus feet by helping one another remain fixed in your instructions. Now we have worked together to write a poem in honor of your appearance day. The thread that weaves throughout is made of your wonderful qualities. The fabric is woven of our fledgling devotional sentiments. Please accept our humble offering.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’re extremely merciful;  
you’re a model of vast toleration.  
Although at times we seem unheedful,  
being steeped in sense gratification,

You’re determined to show us compassion,  
to be our mentor and guide.  
You work tirelessly with each generation,  
with Kṛṣṇa right by your side.

There’s only one reason why you came,  
not to earn money, glory, or fame.  
Your mission, O Prabhupāda: to make us tame  
So that we’ll always chant Kṛṣṇa’s name.

You gave us an enormous treasure;  
you told us how to find real pleasure.  
No one can even try to measure,  
and repaying our debt will take forever.

You transformed the hippies from habits grotesque  
and changed their lives from horribly obscene.  
You taught them to live by Gaurāṅga’s process,  
converting so many to “revolutionary clean.”

The *Vedas* seem to point to *mokṣa*  
and worship of the demigods.  
But at the heart is pure devotion,  
love and unalloyed service to God

Knowing this without a question  
made your preaching powerfully sound.  
By your steadfastness and conviction,  
the truth you could perfectly expound.

In the front of all your books  
you give a little speech,  
Revealing all the Vaiṣṇavas  
who empowered you to preach.

You said it was their mercy  
that gave you *kṛṣṇa-prema,*  
That we should take it seriously,  
not live a little game.

You were so wise and smart  
but never glowed with pride.  
Though you left and broke our hearts,  
you remain our eternal guide.

You are skilled and so your dreams were fulfilled,  
and yet your way of life was simple.  
It was due to you that love of God was instilled  
and we sipped your nectarine Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’re simple in many ways.  
You went to America with not much at all—  
some rupees and books that would last a few days.  
And now you have centers all over the world.

In the nectar of devotion graveness is defined—  
when you look at someone’s face you can’t tell what’s on their mind.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, this quality you most certainly do hold:  
we never grasped your plans for changing the whole world.

Revolution on your mind  
but compassion on your face,  
adjusting Kṛṣṇa consciousness  
to fit the time and place.

When giving class or giving books,  
when you’d chant and dance and sing,  
with kind instructions or stern looks  
a friendly feeling you would bring

Or when a gift of berries made,  
though they were mainly small and few,  
you happily took the gift and bade  
the devotees have some too.

You showed kindness to your children,  
gladly passing out *prasādam* sweets.  
You are kind to every man and woman,  
telling Kṛṣṇa stories to everyone you meet.

In the last days in Vṛndāvan,  
when your health was very bad,  
you stopped eating *prasādam*—  
*caraṇāmṛta* was all you had.

When disciples begged you to stay  
and finally you agreed,  
You ate only one strawberry  
and we were all so pleased.

Never taking more then needed,  
you ate with respect and grace,  
and when your body didn’t need to,  
you didn’t hanker for the taste.

In your Bhaktivedanta Purports  
your words are pure and clear.  
Each analogy gives strong support  
and is pleasurable to hear.

When you arrived in Boston Harbor,  
you immortalized that day.  
You spoke your heart in poetry  
and taught us how to pray.

So benevolent that you came  
To the hub of Māyā’s hold.  
Standing alone, you gave the Name,  
your message strong and bold.

And although we were unqualified  
and knew nothing of Kṛṣṇa’s glories,  
you crossed o’er the sea and exemplified  
how the Lord works through His devotees.

Though we’re children stumbling on the path,  
you are anxious to show us the way.  
Thank you for your kindness, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
We pray at your feet we’ll stay.

To spread this movement you opened your heart,  
with Kṛṣṇa guiding you right from the start.  
Your worldwide movement is a great work of art.  
Now we as your followers must do our part.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, never fighting for your own name,  
despite envious insults, by our side you remain.  
Only really wanting to spread Kṛṣṇa’s fame,  
you endure all our tumults again and again.

You preached His message to everyone.  
Seeing the soul was your duty,  
not seeing the difference in anyone,  
fixed on the souls’ ultimate beauty.

Equal to both a pebble and gold,  
friends and enemies you treat as one,  
giving the message of Gaura strong and bold.  
I offer my respects, all-virtuous one.

Your servants at the Vaishnava Academy for Girls, Alachua, Florida, USA.

## Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education (VIHE)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

The VIHE is now entering its fifteenth year of serving you. There have been many changes in those years—changes to ISKCON, to ISKCON Vṛndāvana, to our little institute—but one thing has remained constant: we are still regularly teaching from and studying your books.

As the years go by and I continually try to explain what I’ve learned from your Bhaktivedanta purports, I find that I’ll never be able to say, “I know the *Bhagavad-gītā*.” The more I study, the more I see that there is more to realize—ad infinitum. Your commentaries are truly transcendental, and I’d like to try to glorify you by glorifying your books:

**Your books are like...**

...a law library. They are the guiding principle in the lives of your followers.

...a seeing eye dog, guiding us blind inhabitants of Kali-yuga around the obstacles of this age.

...a life raft. Just by purchasing a book and “reading one line,” the nondevotees are benefited beyond their expectations and saved from an animal birth.

...a sweet Vṛndāvana breeze. When we are in good consciousness, reading your books uplifts our spirits and helps us feel closer to our Lordships, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara.

...a psychiatrist. I recall as a new devotee referring again and again to the index of your *Gītā* when things were tough and doubts and misgivings arose. I remember especially looking up “mind,” trying to figure out how to control my wayward thoughts.

...a brisk slap in the face. Reading passages about material life and the urgency of taking to Kṛṣṇa consciousness serves to wake us up—often suddenly.

...an adventure we put off doing till late in life. After regularly studying your books for days in a row, I often have lamented, “Why didn’t I do this years ago?”

...a beautiful ornament. Having a full sets of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* in any devotee’s house makes the home appear regal and majestic.

...a detailed crossword puzzle. It takes great concentration and hard work to appreciate all the intricacies in your books, and how the philosophical flow comes together verse after verse, chapter after chapter.

...an open offer to sit at your lotus feet and hear from you. You reveal so much about yourself and your Kṛṣṇa conscious insights in your purports, e.g., your relationship with your Guru Mahārāja, your often mentioned “secret of success.”

...a gift from the most pure-hearted soul to all of humanity. You worked so hard, getting up at midnight, just to present your gift of the *Bhāgavatam* to the world.

...an intoxicating pill. As a dose of an intoxicant makes us forget reality, if we just take the medicine of opening your books, our consciousness is uplifted and we transcend mundane “reality,” feeling above the worries and anxieties of the world.

...the polestar. So many things come and go with the passing of time, but your books remain a fixed reference point.

...a huge weight. As difficult as it is to pick up a weighty object, it seems similarly hard sometimes to muster up the strength to open one of your books and read the contents.

...a glimpse into the spiritual world, full of descriptions of what the kingdom of God is actually like.

...a security blanket. Carrying one of your books around wherever I go reminds me of a young child feeling secure by carrying his blanket around with him.

...a threat to many—whether one is an atheist, a Māyāvādī, a gross sense enjoyer, or a premature *rasika,* your books pose a threat to such people’s (mis)conceptions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for laboring so hard to write your books. You told a reporter that you sleep only two hours so that you could get up at midnight and translate the *Bhāgavatam*. Such incredible dedication!

My prayer is that for the remainder of this life I can regularly study and apply your teachings. I also hope I can teach whatever I’ve learned from you to others. The VIHE’s logo comes from something you once said in New Vrindaban: “Learn and Teach Others.” I hope I can carry that instruction of yours in my heart and help many come in contact with your books and understand something of their import.

Your servants at the Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education (VIHE).  
(written by Braja Bihārī dāsa)
